# v2.4 Audit Ledger (Backend + Frontend)

**Scope:** unified ledger — backend audit (Phase 1-4 closed at v2.4.1 tag, commit `c2bd969`) + frontend audit-01 (2026-05-18). Backend findings retain their original IDs (`CRIT-001`, `HIGH-001`, …); frontend findings carry the `FE-` prefix (`FE-CRIT-001`, `FE-HIGH-001`, …) to avoid collisions.

**Backend source:** this document (Sections 1-29 below + the Sweep Classification scaffolding).
**Frontend source:** `docs/audits/2026-05-18-v2.4-frontend-audit-01.md` — evidence, screenshots, methodology notes. The findings are transcribed into Sections 9-12 of this ledger; the source doc remains as canonical evidence reference.

**Product scope decision (2026-05-18):** Desktop-only product. Mobile / narrow-viewport breakpoints are NOT in scope. Future audits do not test responsive behavior below ~1100 px. Audit-01 Open Question #1 ANSWERED.

**Operator product decisions captured 2026-05-18 (Task 110):** Audit-01's 5 Open Questions are now all ANSWERED. Detail:
1. Mobile / narrow viewport: NO — desktop-only (above).
2. Calculator Recent card timestamp format: RELATIVE for < 24 h (`2h ago`, `Yesterday 22:37`), ABSOLUTE for older. Drives FE-MED-005 fix.
3. `/fragments/ws_status` 1 Hz polling: INTENTIONAL — the endpoint carries live latency, not just connection state. FE-HIGH-004 reframed; see entry below.
4. BTCUSDT events in Trade Events Log: TEST POLLUTION — no real BTCUSDT trading in last 3 days. FE-HIGH-003 + FE-MED-004 scope confirmed; downgraded to MED severity for HIGH-003.
5. Add Account modal Bybit/MEXC presentation: BETA TAG — expose with `(Beta)` suffix on the label. Drives FE-HIGH-002 fix.

## Overall Verdict

The v2.4 codebase is **operationally solid for a single-user deployment**. The
core trading loop (fill processing, position lifecycle, risk calculations) works
correctly under normal conditions. 1247 tests pass. The history page redesign
(Tasks 68-82) shipped cleanly with consistent patterns.

The primary risk areas are: (1) a logic bug in exec_link where TP/SL matching
always returns True (self-comparison), (2) data export always uses account_id=1
regardless of active account, (3) missing authentication on all API endpoints,
(4) N+1 query pattern in the fill drawer, (5) division-by-zero paths in edge
cases, and (6) WebSocket reconnect can stack overflow on persistent auth failures.
Items 1, 2, and 4 are v2.4-era regressions; the rest are pre-existing. None are
show-stoppers for current single-user operation but several affect multi-account
correctness.

Architecture is well-layered (core + adapter ring, DB mixins, pub/sub abstraction)
but shows coupling creep via 24 direct `db._conn` access points that bypass the
DatabaseManager API. Test coverage by module count is 30% (18/59 core modules have
dedicated tests), though integration coverage is higher via cross-cutting test files.

Resolved in Task 139 (FE-HIGH-009 + FE-MED-030 Calculator countdown race cluster, single fix closes both): Root cause was hypothesis 2 — event-bus-publish-vs-htmx-load race. POST `/calculator/calculate` calls `event_bus.publish()` which only enqueues to an asyncio.Queue; the actual `insert_pre_trade_log` runs on a separate task drained by `EventBus.run()`. The response embeds `<div hx-trigger="load,every 5s">` which fires the first poll within microseconds of receipt — frequently BEFORE the DB INSERT commits. Route handler's SELECT returns no row → `pretrade_ts_ms=None` → `compute_link_window_status` correctly returns EXPIRED (conservative for unknown calc). But the EXPIRED template branch is TERMINAL (no `hx-trigger`) — polling stops, widget stuck at PLAN EXPIRED forever (the 30s-old-calc observation in FE-MED-030 is the screenshot timing, not the poll timing). Fix: new PENDING state at the route-handler layer. When pretrade is None, handler short-circuits with `status="PENDING"` (NOT a call to compute_link_window_status). Template gains PENDING branch with `hx-trigger="every 1s"` (fast retry — race window is sub-second). `compute_link_window_status` unchanged — its pure-function behavior is correct. FE-MED-030 resolves automatically with the same fix (same root cause).
Combined active after Task 139: 98 (0 CRIT / 3 HIGH / 56 MED / 39 LOW). Net −2. Top list shrinks from Top 4 → **Top 3** (composition: HIGH-001, FE-HIGH-008, HIGH-002). FE-HIGH-008 promotes to slot 2 (only remaining actionable HIGH). No MED-tier promotion considered — Top 3 is the right shape (1 actionable + 2 architectural-deferred). Implementation: backend +38 LOC in `api/routes_calculator.py` (PENDING short-circuit + extensive anchor comment), frontend +27 LOC in `templates/fragments/link_window_countdown.html` (PENDING branch + doc-comment).
Resolved in Task 140 (FE-HIGH-008 RegimeFetcher.close orphan call): Root cause was a one-line bug at `api/routes_regime.py:108` — `await fetcher.close()` called on a `RegimeFetcher` instance that has no `.close()` method. RegimeFetcher holds only `self._adapter` (caller-owned); each `fetch_*` method uses its own `async with httpx.AsyncClient(...)` context manager which closes per-call. Nothing to clean up at the fetcher level. **Fix shape: removed the orphan call** (NOT added a no-op `close()` method — that would obscure intent). Anchor comment in the call site explains why. Tests: 8 cases in `tests/test_task140_regime_backfill_close.py` — call removed, anchor comment present, RegimeFetcher class still has no `.close()` (deliberate pin), AttributeError still reproducible if called directly (documents pre-fix failure mode), event-bus-pattern-check pins (backfill uses `asyncio.create_task` + in-memory `_regime_jobs` dict, NOT event_bus). FBF: 2/8 fail when fix stashed (source-pin tests); 6 pass (class-shape + AttributeError-reproducibility + create_task-pattern — all unchanged by stashing). Restored → 8/8 pass.
Filed in Task 140 (post-Task-139 latent failure mode): FE-MED-032 (Calculator PENDING state has no upper bound — silent-broken if event-bus consumer crashes mid-poll; cap PENDING at N seconds with error transition).
Event-bus-pattern investigation outcome (Task 140 step 3): Regime backfill does NOT share Task 139's calculator race pattern. Different shape: `asyncio.create_task(_run())` + shared in-memory `_regime_jobs` dict, registered BEFORE task fires (line 96 vs `create_task` at 127). Status endpoint reads same dict (in-memory, GIL-protected). No "row not yet written" window. No new race-surface finding to file. Test class `TestBackfillUsesInMemoryJobDictNotEventBus` pins the pattern so a future refactor onto event_bus will trip a re-evaluation.

Framing-note correction (Task 99.1): HIGH-007 + HIGH-014 were paraphrased as "race-framed" in earlier Top-5 commentary, but the audit text itself is not race-framed for either (HIGH-007 = observability; HIGH-014 = drift / missing reconciliation). Neither finding counts toward the race-framing false-positive calibration. Count below remains 8/15 (53%).

**Race-framing calibration (as of Task 96):** 8 of ~15 audit findings using "race", "concurrent", or similar framing have verified false (53% false-positive rate in this category). The audit consistently assumed Python runtime semantics permit concurrent mutation where local-variable binding, GIL serialization, and cooperative asyncio actually provide structural protection. The correct pattern for closure-capturing mutable data already exists in the codebase: `lambda f=fill: ...` default-arg snapshots are the right shape and are used in places where mutation is a genuine concern.

Implication for future audit-vs-codebase work: race-framed findings should default to verify-before-assume — start with await-analysis of the loop body and trace of the mutation paths before accepting the audit's described mechanism.

**Audit-impact-imprecision pattern (as of Task 139, seven examples):** Distinct from race-framing. The audit has repeatedly described the *mechanism* or *direction of impact* of a finding incorrectly, even when the underlying bug is real. Seven observed examples:
- HIGH-019 (Task 93): audit assumed the engine actively places TP/SL orders. Engine is a passive observer; Quantower plugin places. Wrong architecture — finding verified false and reframed as HIGH-027 (calc-to-fill window).
- HIGH-026 (Task 95): audit described a silent-swallow try/except. Logs already existed via `log.exception`. The real gap was missing structured engine_event emission, not silence — finding redrawn during fix.
- HIGH-013 (Task 102): audit framed downstream impact as "risk engine allows excessive position sizing". Tracing the consumers showed equity=0 actually collapses via `risk_engine.py:351`'s `total_equity if > 0 else 1.0` fallback to 1.0 USDT phantom equity → micro-positions, not excessive. Wrong direction. Real harm is silent corruption of known-good prior state (BOD/SOW freeze, etc.).
- HIGH-008 (Task 103): audit said the scheduler "crashes silently" on AuthenticationError. The existing broad `except Exception` already caught it — the task did not crash; every 15-30 s tick hammered the exchange with invalid creds, generating log noise + IP-ban risk + no operator signal. Wrong mechanism.
- FE-HIGH-004 (audit-01, reframed Task 110): audit-01 framed 1 Hz `ws_status` polling as "wasteful for what's typically a static-ish boolean (WS up/down)". Operator clarification 2026-05-18: the endpoint carries live latency display (`+437ms` in header), not boolean connection state. The 1 Hz cadence is by design. Reframed as FE-MED-016 (SSE migration is optimization-only, not bug). Wrong premise — the auditor didn't know what the endpoint actually rendered.
- FE-MED-018 (Task 135): audit framed dual-period-selector on Equity Curve as "precedence ambiguity between two competing controls." Investigation revealed the page-level period selector is a **dead control** on Equity Curve (handler `frag_analytics_equity` doesn't read `period`) — no actual conflict, no precedence rule needed. Wrong mechanism (orthogonality vs competition). Fix is Model D: dim+disable the dead control on tabs that don't consume `period`. Same shape may exist on Regime (FE-MED-007 parent) — flagged for re-investigation, not refiled.
- FE-MED-030 (Task 139): audit framed countdown widget bug as "widget tracks 'last submitted calc' rather than 'currently-displayed form values' — semantic mismatch with form state." Investigation revealed the visible bug was actually the **event-bus-publish-vs-htmx-load race** causing the FIRST poll to render TERMINAL EXPIRED (no `hx-trigger`), polling stopped, widget stuck. The "30s old calc shows EXPIRED" observation was screenshot timing, not widget-tracking semantics. Wrong mechanism (race-window terminal-state-stuck vs semantic-tracking-mismatch). Same root cause as FE-HIGH-009; single PENDING-state fix closed both. The semantic-tracking observation is still valid as a separate UX call (should the widget clear when form drifts from submitted calc?) — but that's a different, smaller question, deferred unless operator wants to file as new finding.

**Task 136 follow-on (FE-MED-007 Regime parallel investigation):** Applied the same orthogonality lens to Regime; outcome diverged. Regime has no page-level period bar (panel-scoped selector inside Macro Signals card on Overview only; sub-tabs switch client-side via `showPanel()`); both global "All:" and per-card selectors are functional (NOT dead controls); a coherent precedence model exists in code (`setAllCardRanges` overrides per-card; `setCardRange` clears global active state). BUT the investigation surfaced a different mechanism mismatch — a **Task-125 PeriodSelector migration regression**: two JS callsites still queried the dead `.global-range-btn` class post-migration, so the visual clear-on-override silently stopped working. Fix is class-selector replacement (`.global-range-btn` → `.global-range-group .preset-btn`) + title-attribute documentation of the precedence model. Pattern enrichment without counter increment: **orthogonality investigation can surface migration regressions in addition to dead controls** — both are mechanism mismatches with the audit's stated framing. The counter stays at 6 (Regime's audit-text-vs-mechanism mismatch was about precedence-language vs the migration-regression mechanism, but the AUDIT framing on Regime was the same as Analytics; what diverged was the investigation's discovered-mechanism, not a 7th distinct audit-text imprecision). The pattern-roster value-add is the mechanism category, not the counter.

Implication for future audit-vs-codebase work: don't anchor on the audit's described impact mechanism. Trace the downstream consumers independently when investigating a finding. The bug may be real but with a different harm shape than the audit predicted — fix the real harm, not the described one. The fix-task report should explicitly note when the audit's mechanism/direction was wrong, so the calibration record grows. **Precedence-framed findings now default to investigate-orthogonality-first** (FE-MED-018 example, reinforced by FE-MED-007 Task 136): a "two selectors with unclear interaction" claim should first verify whether both selectors actually have effect; one being a dead control OR a migration regression having broken the cross-clearing is more common than genuine precedence ambiguity. **Post-primitive-migration sweep checklist** (new, surfaced Task 136): when primitive migrations rename CSS classes, audit all JS callsites that query the old class — silent visual regressions are easy to miss otherwise.

## Top 5 Highest-Priority Fixes

Note (Task 141 — Phase 5 closure, Option 3 applied): Top list **restructured into two subsections**. Composition rationale: After FE-HIGH-008 RESOLVED (Task 140), all remaining HIGHs are architectural-deferred (HIGH-001 Phase 8, HIGH-002 Phase 6). A single "Top N" list dominated by deferrals communicated nothing actionable. Per Task 140's reported options and Task 141's operator decision: split into **Architectural Deferrals** (phase-gated, separate tracking) + **Active Top N** (operationally prioritized work — currently MED-tier candidates). Severity counts and the "active HIGH" arithmetic are unchanged; only the navigational shape of this section differs.

### Architectural Deferrals (phase-gated, not actionable in Phase 5+)

These items remain in the active count but are blocked on broader phase work. Their inclusion in the Stat Summary preserves auditor-intent visibility; their separation from the Active Top N reflects that neither is a "next-task" candidate.

1. **[HIGH-001]** No authentication on any API endpoint. Architecturally deferred to Phase 8 (deployment-context decision needed — auth model depends on whether the engine runs single-tenant local, multi-tenant cloud, or hybrid). No work in Phase 5+ planned until the deployment context is settled.
2. **[HIGH-002]** Architectural coupling — engine and Quantower plugin share intimate state coordination via WebSocket + DB polling rather than a well-defined contract. Deferred to Phase 6 (broader architectural rework). The scope is too large for any single Phase-5-style task; needs a Phase 6 design document first.

### Active Top N — operationally prioritized (post-Task-141 entry point for Phase 6+ work)

Five candidates surfaced as the highest-leverage non-deferred work. Selection criteria: (a) recurring user-visible failure mode, (b) cross-cutting impact (one fix closes multiple findings), (c) Phase-5-style fit (small-to-medium effort, no architectural prerequisites). Ordered by recommended task-execution sequence, not strict severity:

1. **[FE-MED-032]** Calculator PENDING state has no upper bound. Latent failure mode introduced by Task 139's fix; silent-broken if `handle_risk_calculated` fails. Small effort (cap PENDING at 5s with error-tone transition; reuses Task 134 EmptyState infrastructure). Highest priority because it's a regression-class concern from in-flight infrastructure.
2. **[FE-MED-029]** Calculator first-submit 422. Recurring user-visible bug (each fresh page load: click once → 422 → click again → 200). Two parts: backend race investigation + frontend toast wording. The toast-wording portion overlaps with FE-MED-031.
3. **[FE-MED-031]** Error toast wording surfaces raw exception strings. Cross-cutting: closes the message-quality gap that FE-HIGH-008 + FE-MED-029 individually exhibited. One centralized helper applied to all htmx error paths. Reuses Task 134 infrastructure.
4. **[format_price helper consolidation]** (FE-MED-021 + FE-LOW-022 + FE-LOW-024). A single `format_price(symbol, value)` / `format_contracts(symbol, value)` helper consulting exchange spec tick size closes three findings in one move. Not yet filed as a parent finding; one of these three should be promoted to the family-tracker.
5. **[Bundle-A remaining migrations]** Multiple FE-LOW findings cite the EmptyState / PeriodSelector / TableRow primitives as the leverage point but each consumer-site migration is its own small task. Operator picks the next consumer (e.g., FE-LOW-023 Calculator empty-state migration). Not a single Top-N entry per se but a class of small-leverage work that fills gaps between larger tasks.

Slot-reshuffle rationale entries below (Task 103 through Task 140) assume the pre-Task-141 combined-list shape. They remain accurate as historical record; the Top-N composition they discuss is now split into the two subsections above. No backfill edit needed to historical rationale text.

Slot reshuffle rationale (Task 110): FE-HIGH-003 demoted to FE-MED-017 (BTCUSDT test pollution, not real corruption — operator confirmation). FE-HIGH-004 reframed to FE-MED-016 (1 Hz polling is intentional for latency display, not a bug — audit-impact-imprecision 5th example). Both leave the HIGH bucket; slots 4 + 5 promoted. FE-HIGH-001 takes slot 4 (operator-misread severity). FE-HIGH-006 takes slot 5 — UX-only but operationally the most-used Trade Events surface, and a small fix lands the leverage. HIGH-029 / HIGH-030 / HIGH-031 / HIGH-002 stay below Top 5 (deferred follow-ups + architectural).

Slot reshuffle rationale (Task 111): FE-HIGH-002 RESOLVED — Bybit / MEXC now in the Add Account modal with `(Beta)` suffix. Slot 2 promotes to HIGH-028 (which Task 111 explicitly unblocks for end-to-end verification — Phase 5 Bundle B sequence-critical). FE-HIGH-001 takes slot 3. FE-HIGH-006 holds slot 4. New slot 5: HIGH-029 — the credential-traceback parallel to Task 100. Reasoning: with FE-HIGH-002 closed and HIGH-028 promoted, the next operational concern is the second-largest credential-leak surface (5 exchange-credential entry points still unwrapped). HIGH-030 / HIGH-031 / HIGH-002 stay below Top 5 (deferred follow-ups + architectural).
