## 1. Correctness & Bug Risks

### [CRIT-002] [Correctness] core/risk_engine.py:159-161
  Description: Division by zero in slippage calculation when `best_price` is 0
  Evidence: `est_slippage = max(0.0, (est_fill_price - best_price) / best_price)` — only `entry_price <= 0` is guarded (line 147), not `best_price`
  Impact: ZeroDivisionError crashes the risk calculator mid-trade
  Recommended fix: Add guard `if best_price <= 0: return 0.0, entry_price`
  Effort: small

### [CRIT-003] [Correctness] core/order_manager.py:654-668
  Description: Division by zero risk in proportional entry fee calculation; `max()` on potentially empty list
  Evidence: `exit_time = max(f["timestamp_ms"] for f in close_fills)` — can raise ValueError if close_fills empty after filtering. Fee ratio `total_close_qty / total_open_qty` guarded by ternary but expression evaluated before selection in some Python versions.
  Impact: Crash during position close, leaving inconsistent state
  Recommended fix: Guard `max()`/`sum()` with empty-list checks; use explicit if-else instead of ternary for division
  Effort: small
  Status: VERIFIED FALSE in commit 0068a8e (Task 86) — no production change required
  Verification note (Task 86):
    Claim 1 (max() on empty close_fills): The audit cited lines 654-668 but missed the early-return guard at lines 650-652:
        if not close_fills:
            log.warning("No closing fills for %s %s — skipping", symbol, direction)
            return
    Line 659's max() is unreachable with empty close_fills. Failure-before-fix verification confirmed: removing the line-650 guard produces `ValueError: max() iterable argument is empty` at line 655 (swallowed by the outer try/except at line 737). No bug exists in current code.

    Claim 2 (ternary div-by-zero "in some Python versions"): Python ternary expressions (PEP 308, 2003) have always short-circuited by selection. The expression `a / b if b > 0 else 0.0` does not evaluate `a / b` when `b == 0`, in any Python version. Operands here are plain floats; no comparison overload or NaN trickery applies. Failure-before-fix verification confirmed: removing the ternary `if total_open_qty else 0.0` produces `ZeroDivisionError` at the division site (also swallowed by the outer try/except). The ternary IS load-bearing — but the construction is correct as written. No bug exists in current code.

    Action taken: Three regression-pin tests added in tests/test_order_manager.py::TestClosePositionGuards to protect against future removal of the line-650 guard, the prop_entry ternary (lines 665-668), and the exit_price ternary (lines 655-658). No production code modified.

### [HIGH-004] [Correctness] core/data_cache.py:516
  Description: Inconsistent null-checks across portfolio recalculation
  Evidence: `rolling_peak` from `_fetch_rolling_peak_equity()` could return None; `max_eq` guarded at line 474 but downstream fields like `individual_margin_used` on positions may be uninitialized
  Impact: Incorrect drawdown metrics or crash on None arithmetic
  Recommended fix: Validate all position fields initialized; ensure rolling peak returns non-zero
  Effort: medium
  Status: VERIFIED FALSE in Task 90 — both halves already guarded in current code.
  Verification note (Task 90):
    Half 1 (rolling_peak may be None): _fetch_rolling_peak_equity at core/data_cache.py:75-106
    has return type `float`, not Optional. All three return paths produce a float — the
    `row[0] if row and row[0] is not None else 0.0` at line 102 explicitly handles the None
    case from MAX() over an empty set. Consumer at line 516 is also already guarded:
    `rolling_dd = (rolling_peak - total_equity) / rolling_peak if rolling_peak > 0 else 0.0`.

    Half 2 (individual_margin_used may be uninitialized): Field defaulted to 0.0 via
    @dataclass field at core/state.py:102. Explicit construction-site init at
    core/platform_bridge.py:629 (`prev.individual_margin_used if prev else 0.0`).
    SQLite column declared `REAL NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` at core/database.py:114. Never None
    on any construction path.

    Action: Four regression-pin tests added in tests/test_task90_high004_high005_pins.py
    to document the guards and catch future removal. No production change. Non-vacuous
    proof verified: removing the dataclass default OR the line-516 ternary causes the
    corresponding pin to fail.

### [HIGH-005] [Correctness] core/order_manager.py:526-562
  Description: TP/SL enrichment proceeds when mark price is 0 (uninitialized)
  Evidence: Guard at line 527 checks `mark`, sets flags, but lines 534-561 still process orders even if `mark == 0` — `min()` with distance-to-zero picks arbitrarily among equidistant orders
  Impact: Wrong TP/SL price displayed on open positions
  Recommended fix: Return early from enrichment if mark price is 0
  Effort: small
  Status: VERIFIED FALSE in Task 90 — early-return guard at lines 527-532 already short-circuits.
  Verification note (Task 90):
    Audit claimed lines 534-561 still process orders when mark == 0. Read of current code
    shows the early-return guard at lines 527-532:
        if not mark:
            pos.individual_tp_price = 0.0
            pos.individual_sl_price = 0.0
            pos.individual_tpsl = False
            continue
    fires for both None and 0/0.0 (via `if not mark`), zeroes the display fields, clears
    the flag, and `continue`s to the next position. The min() calls at lines 550/556 are
    unreachable when mark is falsy. The guard is the second statement in the loop body.

    Action: Three regression-pin tests added (zero, None defensive, and a sanity counter-
    test for valid marks that protects against future over-widening of the guard).
    No production change. Non-vacuous proof verified: removing the `if not mark` block
    causes the zero/None pins to fail by selecting the TP order at 110.0 against a
    mark=0 position (the exact failure mode the audit described — except the guard
    was already preventing it).

### [MED-001] [Correctness] core/order_manager.py:632-635
  Description: Position lookup falls back to entry_price=0.0 when position not in memory
  Evidence: `pos = next((p for p in app_state.positions if ...), None); entry_price = pos.average if pos else 0.0`
  Impact: Corrupted closed_position records with 0 entry price
  Recommended fix: Log warning; attempt DB lookup as fallback
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5a)
  Sweep evidence:
      pos = next(
          (p for p in app_state.positions if p.position_id == pos_id),
          None,
      )
      entry_price    = pos.average if pos else 0.0
  Sweep note: lines 632-636 confirm the unguarded fallback. No warning, no DB lookup. Real correctness bug — fires whenever the in-memory positions list is stale or empty at close-row build time. Trivially reachable post-restart with open positions.

### [MED-002] [Correctness] core/risk_engine.py:419
  Description: R:R ratio can overflow when est_loss approaches zero
  Evidence: `est_r = est_profit / est_loss if est_loss > 0 else 0.0` — no cap on result
  Impact: Extremely large R:R values causing display/analytics distortion
  Recommended fix: Cap at reasonable maximum (e.g., 100.0)
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5a)
  Sweep evidence (line 436):
      est_r      = est_profit / est_loss if est_loss > 0 else 0.0
  Sweep note: Ternary guards zero only; very-small-positive est_loss produces unbounded R:R. Confirmed at risk_engine.py:436 (audit cited :419, the actual statement is at :436 — minor line drift, semantic unchanged).

### [MED-043] [Correctness] core/risk_engine.py (estimate_vwap_fill level loop)
  Description: Missing qty <= 0 guard in level iteration — negative qty corrupts total_qty and est_fill_price
  Evidence: Loop computes `avail_usdt = price * qty` → `fill_usdt = min(remaining, avail)` → `fill_qty = fill_usdt / price`. Negative qty cascades through all three steps silently, accumulating negative total_qty and inflating remaining_usdt.
  Impact: If qty ever goes negative (upstream parsing bug or exchange glitch), est_fill_price is corrupted and feeds garbage into slippage estimation and position sizing.
  Recommended fix: Symmetric `qty <= 0` skip with log.warning, placed adjacent to existing price guard at line 117.
  Effort: small
  Status: RESOLVED in commit d21c58b (Task 85b)
  Discovered-during: Task 85b investigation
  Note: Task 85's discovery report described `price <= 0` as unguarded in this function; that claim was incorrect — the price guard exists at line 117-118. The actual defensive gap was on qty, addressed here. This note is in the permanent record to prevent future maintainers trusting the original misattribution.

### [LOW-022] [Observability] core/risk_engine.py:117-118
  Description: Silent skip of invalid orderbook levels in estimate_vwap_fill — no log emitted on corrupt exchange data
  Evidence: Pre-fix code was `if price <= 0: continue` with no log statement.
  Impact: Corrupt exchange data dropped with no telemetry. Position sizing anomalies downstream have no diagnostic trail back to the source orderbook glitch.
  Recommended fix: Add log.warning to existing skip; mirror with symmetric qty guard (see MED-043).
  Effort: small
  Status: RESOLVED in commit d21c58b (Task 85b)
  Discovered-during: Task 85b investigation

### [HIGH-026] [Reliability] core/order_manager.py:737-741
  Description: Outer try/except in _build_close_row_for_fill silently swallows any close-row build failure
  Evidence: `try: ... except Exception: log.exception(...)` at the end of the function catches every exception from the close-row build (e.g., empty close_fills bypassing the line-650 guard, ternary failures, schema/DB errors during insert_closed_position). On catch, the function returns without surfacing the failure to the caller, without emitting an engine_event, and without marking the position-close attempt as failed in any persisted state.
  Impact: A failed position close vanishes silently. Engine state diverges from reality: caller assumes close attempted (possibly succeeded), no signal to user, no forensic trail beyond the log line. In trading code, fail-quiet at the position-close boundary is the wrong default — caller cannot retry, reconcile, or surface to operator.
  Recommended fix: Surface the exception to caller (re-raise after logging) OR emit an engine_event("close_row_build_failed", ...) with position context AND return a structured failure indicator the caller can check. Decide which based on the existing caller-contract; either way the silent-swallow path must end. Coordinate with the broader "fail-loud in async paths" pattern (HIGH-007, HIGH-008).
  Effort: medium
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 95 — approach (a) enhanced: kept existing log.exception, added structured engine_event emission.
  Discovered-during: Task 86 failure-before-fix verification (commit 0068a8e)
  Implementation note (Task 95):
    Investigation found the current code already has `log.exception(...)` inside
    the except (at order_manager.py:751 pre-edit). The audit's "vanishes silently"
    framing overstated the silence — logs ARE emitted. But the real gap held:
    no structured engine_event, no UI surface, no queryable forensic trail
    beyond raw log scrollback.

    Caller analysis (both fire-and-forget):
      - process_fill at order_manager.py:599 uses asyncio.ensure_future(...);
        result not awaited, can't react to exceptions.
      - build_final_close_row at order_manager.py:782 awaits but discards return.
    Re-raise (approach c) buys nothing — neither caller can act on the exception.
    Narrowing the except (approach b) would require enumerating 5+ exception
    types across DB ops + dict access + arithmetic — smell.

    Fix: kept `log.exception` (preserves existing forensic trail), added
    inner `log_event(account_id, "close_row_build_failed", {...}, source="order_manager")`
    capturing symbol, direction, terminal_position_id, exchange_fill_id,
    exchange_order_id, error_type, error_msg. Inner try/except prevents
    event emission failure from masking the original exception.

    Schema: added "close_row_build_failed" to core/event_log.py EventType
    Literal whitelist.

    Tests (3 new, TestClosePositionSilentSwallow class — avoids the duplicate
    TestClosePositionGuards collision documented in Task 92.5):
      - test_silent_swallow_emits_close_row_build_failed_event: load-bearing
        pin. Forces RuntimeError via mocked get_position_fills; asserts both
        log.exception fires AND the engine event is emitted with full
        context payload. Failure-before-fix verified by reverting the
        event-emission block; pin failed with `len(failure_events) == 1`
        assertion error (0 events emitted). Restored.
      - test_normal_path_no_failure_event: anti-over-correction.
      - test_close_row_build_failed_event_type_registered: schema pin
        against future removal of the EventType whitelist entry.

### [CRIT-008] [Correctness] core/database.py:initialize() — missing pre_trade_log.calc_id inline ALTER
  Description: Legacy DB's pre_trade_log table was missing the calc_id column on fresh installs. The column is referenced by ≥5 production query sites including the v2.4.1-shipping frag_position_fills batch path (Task 88) and frag_exec_link.
  Evidence: No `ALTER TABLE pre_trade_log ADD COLUMN calc_id` in core/database.py's inline migrations list. Migration 005 adds the column only to per-account DBs, where pre_trade_log doesn't actually exist. Self-acknowledged at core/database.py:547-560 where a closed_positions backfill is wrapped in try/except OperationalError for this exact reason.
  Impact: frag_position_fills and frag_exec_link history-page routes raise OperationalError ("no such column: calc_id") on fresh installs. Task 88's batch refactor (CRIT-001 RESOLVED) inherits this gap — perf win is unreachable until the column lands. v2.4.1-blocking.
  Recommended fix: Add `ALTER TABLE pre_trade_log ADD COLUMN calc_id TEXT` to the inline migrations list at core/database.py:497-513, alongside the existing orders.calc_id / fills.calc_id / closed_positions.calc_id ALTERs. The existing try/except shell at lines 517-526 handles re-run idempotency via the "duplicate column name" swallow.
  Effort: small (1 LOC + regression tests)
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 88.1 — ALTER added alongside sibling calc_id ALTERs; regression test pins post-init column existence + round-trip on a fresh DatabaseManager.
  Discovered-during: Task 88.1 Step 1 column-existence gate (after Task 88 batch refactor surfaced the dependency)

### [HIGH-027] [Correctness] core/exec_link.py — calc-to-fill matching has no time window
  Description: The engine matches incoming fills to pre_trade_log entries implicitly via price/symbol proximity in compute_exec_match. Fills carry no explicit calc_id; the engine assigns one post-hoc by finding the best-matching pretrade. The matching function has no time-window check, and pre_trade_log entries persist indefinitely with no expiration. A stale pretrade from hours or days ago can match a fresh fill if price and symbol coincidentally align, retroactively tagging the new fill with the wrong calc_id.
  Evidence:
    - compute_exec_match (core/exec_link.py) parameters are fill + pretrade + order_type. No fill_ts or pretrade_ts in the matching logic; no MAX_LINK_WINDOW constant or config knob.
    - pre_trade_log table has no expiration column; no cleanup or soft-expiration job exists.
    - Fills arrive via WS without explicit calc_id propagation. Matching is fully implicit (engine is passive observer + post-hoc linker).
  Impact: Stale plan-to-fill linkages contaminate intent-vs-execution analytics. R:R metrics, drawdown attribution, and "did the plan work?" measurements compute against the wrong intended parameters. A discretionary trader who calculated an idea earlier, abandoned it, and traded a different setup later can have the new fill incorrectly linked to the old plan if prices align — silently corrupting all downstream metrics for that trade.
  Recommended fix (UI + backend):
    Backend:
      1. Add per-account `link_window_seconds` field to accounts table (default 6h = 21600s; operator-tunable).
      2. Add per-pretrade `link_window_seconds_override` (nullable) on pre_trade_log; falls back to account default if NULL.
      3. compute_exec_match accepts fill_ts and pretrade_ts; computes effective window as (override ?? account_default); rejects candidates where (fill_ts - pretrade_ts) > effective_window.
      4. Update caller(s) of compute_exec_match to pass timestamps.
      5. Optional defense-in-depth: soft-expire pre_trade_log entries past their effective window (set expired=True); UI surfaces "expired plan, no link possible" badge.
    Frontend (HTMX):
      6. Account settings page: add link-window editor for the active account's default. Hours/minutes input. Standard save flow.
      7. Calculator UI: add per-calculation override input near trade-copyable setups area. Default to "use account default"; allow override per calc.
      8. Live countdown element near the calculator showing time remaining until link eligibility expires. Format like "Linkable for: 5h 23m" or "EXPIRED — recalculate to link new fills." Updates via HTMX polling (or client-side timer if polling cost matters).
    Tests:
      9. Pin: in-window match succeeds; past-window match rejected.
      10. Pin: account-level default applies when no override.
      11. Pin: per-calc override takes precedence over account default.
      12. Pin: UI countdown reflects effective window (could be a screenshot/HTML-snapshot test or a backend test asserting the rendered template carries correct values).
  Open design decisions:
    - Account-default value (suggest 6h for mixed crypto futures; tunable).
    - Override granularity: free-form seconds vs preset bins (30m / 1h / 4h / 24h)?
    - Countdown display format: precise countdown (HH:MM:SS) or coarse ("expires in ~4h")? Precise is more informative but more visual noise.
    - Expired-state UI: show stale calc in greyed-out state vs hide it vs separate "expired ideas" log?
    - Countdown UI refresh mechanism: HTMX polling (cleaner, server-rendered) vs client-side timer (lower server load, more JS). For sub-minute precision, client-side is probably necessary.
    - DB schema migration for the new accounts column + pre_trade_log column — needs to be defensive of existing data (default to 6h for existing rows).
  Effort: medium-large. ~40-60 LOC core/exec_link.py + ~20 LOC callers + ~30 LOC backend settings persistence + ~80-150 LOC frontend (settings editor + calc override + countdown element) + ~40 LOC migration + tests. Roughly 200-300 LOC total + tests.
  Status: RESOLVED in Tasks 104a + 104b — backend (schema, compute_exec_match window logic, caller updates) in 104a; frontend (account settings editor, per-calc override input, live countdown with 4-state UI) in 104b.
  Discovered-during: Task 93 HIGH-019 verification (architecture reframe based on user workflow clarification); refined in Task 93.1.
  Refined-during: Task 94.1 (operator UX requirements clarification)
  Target phase: Phase 4 (reliability + analytics correctness)
  Backend status (Task 104a): RESOLVED — DB schema + compute_exec_match window logic + caller updates landed.
    Implementation note:
      - core/database.py: `accounts.link_window_seconds INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 21600` (6 h) added to CREATE TABLE + ALTER migration. `pre_trade_log.link_window_seconds_override INTEGER DEFAULT NULL` added to CREATE TABLE + ALTER migration. Legacy DBs catch the ALTERs on startup; idempotent via the existing `duplicate column name` swallow.
      - core/exec_link.py: `DEFAULT_LINK_WINDOW_SECONDS = 21600` module constant. compute_exec_match signature extended with fill_ts_ms, pretrade_ts_ms, account_link_window_seconds (kwargs with backwards-compatible defaults). Effective window = `pretrade['link_window_seconds_override']` (if non-NULL) else `account_link_window_seconds`. Hard-reject (return None) when `(fill_ts_ms - pretrade_ts_ms) / 1000 > effective_window`. Boundary: strict `>` so elapsed == window still matches. get_exec_link_status forwards the new args and reports `None` from compute_exec_match as `('unlinked', 0)`. exec_link_confirmed fast-path preserved — confirmed links bypass the window check (an operator confirmation is authoritative).
      - api/routes_orders.py: 2 call sites updated. frag_position_fills (line ~190) — one-shot `_account_link_window_seconds(aid)` lookup before the per-fill loop; per-fill `_pretrade_ts_to_ms(ptl)` ISO→ms conversion; passes timestamps + window to get_exec_link_status. frag_exec_link (line ~255) — same pattern at the single-fill site, passes to compute_exec_match.
      - Two new helpers in routes_orders.py: `_account_link_window_seconds(account_id)` async one-shot SELECT (defensive: falls back to DEFAULT_LINK_WINDOW_SECONDS on missing-row), `_pretrade_ts_to_ms(pretrade)` ISO parse with timezone-naïve → UTC fallback (defensive: returns None on parse failure, which skips the window check rather than crashing).
    Tests (tests/test_task104a_exec_link_window.py, 16 cases):
      - TestComputeExecMatchWindow (8): within-window match; past-window reject; per-calc override precedence; zero-window rejects all; window boundary strict `>`; missing timestamps skips check; partial-args (only one ts) skips check; DEFAULT_LINK_WINDOW_SECONDS == 21600 pin.
      - TestGetExecLinkStatusWindow (3): past-window reports 'unlinked'; confirmed-fill bypasses window; within-window reports 'linked'.
      - TestSchemaPins (3): fresh-init has accounts.link_window_seconds with default 21600; fresh-init has pre_trade_log.link_window_seconds_override nullable; legacy-shape DB picks up both columns via ALTER migration.
      - TestCallerWiring (2): source pin asserts both call sites pass fill_ts_ms / pretrade_ts_ms / account_link_window_seconds kwargs; helper-function presence pin.
    Frontend status: RESOLVED in Task 104b.
    Overall HIGH-027 status: RESOLVED (Task 104a backend + Task 104b frontend).
    Drift footgun captured: core/database.py:`_CREATE_STATEMENTS.strip().split(";")` naively splits on every `;`, including ones inside SQL line comments. Initial Task 104a commit hit `sqlite3.OperationalError: incomplete input` from a literal `;` inside a `-- ...` comment block. Resolved by rewriting the comments without semicolons. Filed as MED-046 in Task 104b for a separate cleanup.

  Frontend implementation note (Task 104b):
    - core/exec_link.py: new compute_link_window_status(pretrade_ts_ms, account_link_window_seconds, *, override_seconds, exec_link_confirmed, now_ms) → {status, effective_window_s, remaining_s, expires_at_ms}. Four-state machine: LINKABLE / EXPIRING_SOON (< 300 s remaining) / EXPIRED / LINKED_CONFIRMED. LINKED_CONFIRMED takes precedence over EXPIRED so an operator-confirmed past-expiry link renders as "Linked (confirmed)" not "Expired" (the Task 104a latent nuance, now load-bearing in the UI). EXPIRING_SOON_THRESHOLD_S = 300 s and MAX_LINK_WINDOW_SECONDS = 86400 s constants pinned in tests.
    - core/account_registry.py: update_account gains link_window_seconds kwarg; load_all reads the column from DB into cache; _account_meta exposes it to UI fragments.
    - core/db_trades.py:insert_pre_trade_log: INSERT column list + values dict now include link_window_seconds_override (NULL when blank/zero from UI). Forwarded by handle_risk_calculated from the calc dict.
    - api/routes_accounts.py:update_account_detail: new link_window_seconds Form param. Server-side validation rejects < 1 with "must be at least 1 second" and > 86400 with "must be ≤ 86400 s (24 h)" — both return 400 with an inline error fragment so the operator sees the rejection on the existing #acct-msg target.
    - api/routes_calculator.py:calculate_risk: new link_window_seconds_override Form param (string, blank-tolerant). Server-side parse: blank/non-integer → 400; ≤ 0 → treated as "no override" (None); > MAX → 400. Parsed int (or None) attached to calc["link_window_seconds_override"] before publish.
    - api/routes_calculator.py: new endpoint GET /calculator/link-window-status/{calc_id} returns the countdown fragment. Looks up pre_trade_log row (timestamp + override) + accounts.link_window_seconds + exec_link_confirmed via 3 small reads; passes to compute_link_window_status; renders link_window_countdown.html.
    - templates/fragments/account_detail.html: new "Calc Link Window" section with preset dropdown (30 m / 1 h / 4 h / 6 h default / 12 h / 24 h max) + automatic "Custom (Ns)" entry when the persisted value isn't a preset. Submits as the existing form's link_window_seconds field.
    - templates/calculator.html: per-calc link_window_seconds_override <select> dropdown in the form, same preset shape, blank-option for "use account default". Submits with the calc POST.
    - templates/fragments/calc_result.html: countdown container with hx-trigger="load, every 5s", initial GET to /calculator/link-window-status/{calc_id}, swap=outerHTML. Renders only when calc.calc_id and calc.eligible are set.
    - templates/fragments/link_window_countdown.html: new fragment, four state branches with distinct border colors (green / amber / red / green). Hybrid display format — precise (Mm Ss) when remaining < 1 h or in EXPIRING_SOON, coarse (~Hh Mm) when > 1 h LINKABLE. Active states (LINKABLE, EXPIRING_SOON) carry their own hx-trigger="every 5s" so the polling continues; terminal states (EXPIRED, LINKED_CONFIRMED) intentionally stop polling.

  Tests (tests/test_task104b_link_window_ui.py, 20 cases):
    - TestComputeLinkWindowStatus (8): LINKABLE / EXPIRING_SOON / EXPIRED / LINKED_CONFIRMED state pins; LINKED_CONFIRMED precedence over EXPIRED; per-calc override beats account default; missing pretrade_ts returns EXPIRED defensively; zero window returns EXPIRED; constants pinned.
    - TestAccountLinkWindowEndpoint (4): handler accepts the new Form param; validates lower bound (< 1 → 400); validates upper bound via MAX_LINK_WINDOW_SECONDS; end-to-end registry update persists to DB + cache.
    - TestCalculatorOverrideWiring (5): handler accepts override; attaches to calc dict; insert_pre_trade_log persists the column; calculator template includes the input; endpoint registered.
    - TestTemplateWiring (3): calc_result.html includes countdown polling element; account_detail.html has the link-window section; countdown fragment renders all four states with correct color cues.

  Design decisions (per task spec):
    1. Account settings input format: **Option C (preset bins + automatic custom display).** Operators with different trading styles (scalp / swing / discretionary) covered by presets; custom appears automatically when the persisted value is non-preset, so existing custom values round-trip without surprise.
    2. Per-calc override input format: **same preset shape as Option C.** Consistency with the account settings UI. Empty option submits as blank → server treats as "use account default."
    3. Refresh mechanism: **HTMX polling at 5 s.** Operator typically has 1-3 active calcs at most; 5 s gives "I have N hours/minutes left" precision without polling load. Active states carry hx-trigger inside the fragment so the polling self-perpetuates; terminal states (EXPIRED, LINKED_CONFIRMED) stop polling deliberately — no point pinging the server when nothing changes.
    4. Display format: **hybrid.** Precise (Mm Ss) when remaining < 1 h or in EXPIRING_SOON; coarse (~Hh Mm) when > 1 h LINKABLE. Operators glance for awareness, focus when expiry is near.
    5. State transitions: **four-state model.** LINKED_CONFIRMED takes precedence over EXPIRED — confirmed-past-expiry link is real, not stale.
    6. Upper bound: **MAX_LINK_WINDOW_SECONDS = 86400 (24 h).** Anything beyond a day stretches the "linkable" semantics past operator-plausible workflows; better to force recalc than allow stale matches.

### [HIGH-028] [Reliability] core/adapters/bybit/rest_adapter.py — weight-tracker reconciliation missing (Bybit)
  Description: Parallel to HIGH-014's Binance fix. The Bybit adapter does not parse Bybit's response headers to reconcile the in-memory weight-tracker against exchange-reported usage. Tracker state drifts from actual exchange state under load, leading to potential rate-limit misjudgments — either over-throttling (cost: missed trades) or under-throttling (cost: API ban / disconnect).
  Evidence: Task 99 wired `_reconcile_from_response()` for Binance (HIGH-014 fix); Bybit override was explicitly deferred ("different header set"). `core/adapters/bybit/rest_adapter.py` has no `_reconcile_from_response()` implementation. The base-class default in `core/adapters/base.py:164-173` returns None (no-op), so every Bybit call leaves the tracker estimate uncorrected.
  Impact: Bybit-side rate-limit decisions made against drifted local state. Under load, the adapter could either over-throttle (missed trades) or under-throttle (API ban / disconnect). Symmetric in shape to HIGH-014's Binance impact before fix.
  Recommended fix: Implement Bybit-specific `_reconcile_from_response()` override parsing Bybit's response headers (likely `X-Bapi-Limit-Status` / `X-Bapi-Limit` / `X-Bapi-Limit-Reset-Timestamp`; confirm against current Bybit API docs before implementation). Match the structural shape of Binance's override in `core/adapters/binance/rest_adapter.py`: read from `self._ex.last_response_headers`, case-insensitive lookup, bounds-check parsed integer, call `tracker.reconcile(used, reset_time_ms)`.
  Effort: small. ~20-30 LOC + tests, parallel to Binance's HIGH-014 fix.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 115 — adapted-mirror reconciliation in `core/adapters/bybit/rest_adapter.py:_reconcile_from_response()`.
  Implementation note: Bybit's V5 rate-limit model is fundamentally different from Binance's. Binance publishes a single global `X-MBX-USED-WEIGHT-1M` (used-weight count); Bybit publishes per-endpoint `X-Bapi-Limit-Status` (remaining requests), `X-Bapi-Limit` (endpoint quota), and optional `X-Bapi-Limit-Reset-Timestamp` (window-reset epoch ms). The WeightTracker is a single global budget, so a pure mirror is impossible. The override computes the *saturation ratio* of the just-called endpoint — `(limit - status) / limit` — and scales to `tracker.max_weight`: `tracker.reconcile(int(saturation * max_weight), reset_time_ms)`. Interpretation: when any single endpoint becomes highly saturated, the global tracker reflects that pressure, so subsequent `reserve()` calls hit throttle/block thresholds earlier. Upper-bound proxy rather than a sum-across-endpoints account, but preserves the defense-in-depth goal: server truth catches drift the client estimator cannot. Approach choice: **adapted mirror** (not pure mirror), with the unit-mismatch documented in the docstring. Pure mirror was infeasible — Bybit's per-endpoint model doesn't map to a single weight counter without inventing units.
  Fail-safe edges (all → no-op, estimate stands): either header missing, non-numeric value, `limit <= 0`, `status < 0` or `status > limit`, resulting `used_proxy` outside `[0, max_weight*10]`. Bogus reset-timestamp value falls back to `reset_ms=0` (saturation reconcile still happens — only the window snap is skipped).
  Tests (tests/test_task115_bybit_reconciliation.py, 15 cases): TestBybitHeaderReconciliation (3) — load-bearing pin on saturation calc, lowercase header acceptance, reset-timestamp window snap. TestBybitHeaderReconciliationFailSafe (8) — every fail-safe edge above leaves the estimate intact. TestBybitOverridesReconcileHook (2) — subclass identity check + AST-pin that the source references the V5 header names. TestBinanceBehaviorUnchanged (1) — re-exercise Task 99's load-bearing pin to confirm parity. TestMexcStillUnreconciled (1) — informational pin on the current MEXC gap; flips when MEXC gets its own override.
  FBF: Stashing `core/adapters/bybit/rest_adapter.py` → 5 load-bearing tests fail (4 reconcile + override-present pin); the 10 fail-safe + Binance + MEXC pins pass either way. Restoring → 15/15 pass.
  Discovered-during: Task 99 HIGH-014 implementation (Binance fix scoped explicitly to one adapter; Bybit parallel work needed but different headers required separate task).
  Cross-reference: HIGH-014 (parent finding; Binance fix). HIGH-031 (MEXC parallel — still open; MEXC adapter currently `capabilities.orders=False`, so the reconciliation gap is lower-impact, but if MEXC graduates to trading it must be filed).
  Resolved-during: Task 115
  Target phase: Phase 4 (slot as follow-up to HIGH-014) or defer to Phase 7 if Bybit traffic is light enough to not warrant near-term.
  Filed-during: Task 99.1

### [HIGH-029] [Security] api/routes_accounts.py + core/account_registry.py — parallel credential traceback leak (exchange creds)
  Description: Parallel to HIGH-024's connections-credentials fix. The exchange-credential pathway (api_key + api_secret for the exchange adapter, distinct from the 3rd-party data provider keys covered by HIGH-024) is unwrapped: raw `str` flows from form handlers into `account_registry.add_account` / `update_account` and into the cache without SensitiveStr protection. The same traceback-leak failure mode as HIGH-024 applies — an exception in `encrypt()`, `db.add_account()`, or downstream initialization renders the raw `api_key` / `api_secret` in stack-frame locals.
  Evidence (entry points):
    - `api/routes_accounts.py:44-45` (create_account) — api_key + api_secret from Form
    - `api/routes_accounts.py:60-61` (update_account) — api_key + api_secret from Form
    - `api/routes_accounts.py:198-199` (add_account_modal) — api_key + api_secret from Form
    - `api/routes_accounts.py:229-230` (test_account_preview) — api_key + api_secret from Form
    - `api/routes_accounts.py:306-307` (update_account_detail) — api_key + api_secret from Form
    - `core/account_registry.py:61-62` — decrypt() output for both fields, cached at :75-76, :166-167, :205-207
    Plus `core/crypto.decrypt` is the single chokepoint for ALL credential decryption (connections and accounts).
  Impact: Identical to HIGH-024 (credential exposure in error logs / tracebacks) but with broader surface — exchange credentials gate the actual trading path, so leakage is operationally more severe than 3rd-party data keys.
  Recommended fix: Choose approach (a) wrap at each form-handler entry point (5 sites) + ensure account_registry preserves wrapping through `add_account` / `update_account` then unwraps at the cache boundary; OR (b) wrap at the `core/crypto.decrypt` return + at each form-handler entry, then explicitly `.unwrap()` at every site that builds httpx/CCXT URLs and headers (broader change; defense-in-depth across both connection paths and exchange paths).
  Effort: medium. Approach (a) ≈ 50-80 LOC across routes_accounts.py + account_registry.py. Approach (b) ≈ 100-150 LOC plus discovery work for every unwrap site.
  CRITICAL caveat for implementer: urllib.parse.urlencode / httpx URL builders serialize SensitiveStr via the masked `__str__` override (verified: `urlencode({"k": SensitiveStr("v")})` → `k=%3Cmasked%3E`). Anywhere a SensitiveStr propagates into URL params or HTTP headers must `.unwrap()` first, or the live HTTP request will carry the literal `<masked>` string and authentication will fail. The narrow Task 100 fix avoided this by unwrapping at the cache boundary in connections_manager.upsert.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 116 — approach (a) implemented across 5 routes_accounts entry points + crypto.decrypt return + 3 production decrypt() consumers.
  Implementation note: Wrap-at-form-receipt + cache-unwrap-at-boundary pattern (parallel to Task 100's connections-credentials path). Modifications:
    - `api/routes_accounts.py`: imports `SensitiveStr`; wraps `api_key` and `api_secret` immediately on Form receipt in `create_account`, `update_account`, `add_account_modal`, `test_account_preview`, `update_account_detail`. Direct-CCXT site (`test_account_preview`) wraps then unwraps immediately before `_make_ccxt_instance` — ccxt's internal URL / header serialization receives raw strings.
    - `core/crypto.decrypt`: non-empty return path now returns `SensitiveStr(plaintext)`. Empty input still returns plain `""` (legitimate "no credential stored" sentinel; equality with `""` preserved). Docstring updated.
    - `core/account_registry.AccountRegistry`: `add_account` and `update_account` unwrap before cache store (downstream consumers — `exchange_factory` → CCXT — feed into HTTP paths that may call `str()` / `urlencode` internally; cache must hold raw str). `load_all` unwraps the decrypt return before cache store.
    - `core/connections.ConnectionsManager.load_all`: unwraps decrypt return before cache store (parallel to the Task 100 `upsert` unwrap, on the DB→cache load path).
    - `core/ws_manager`: user-data reconnect unwraps decrypt return before `build_auth_payload` (the payload is `json.dumps`'d, and `__str__` would emit `<masked>` into the signed message body).
  Approach (a) preferred over (b) per audit-doc CRITICAL caveat — narrower blast radius, matches Task 100's narrow-scope pattern, and the `SensitiveStr.__str__` masking trap is contained to a small known set of unwrap sites rather than spread across every URL-building call in the codebase.
  Tests (tests/test_task116_credential_leak_parallels.py, 21 cases): TestDecryptReturnsSensitive (5) — return type, repr/str/format masking, empty-sentinel preservation. TestRoutesAccountsWrapAtForm (7) — parametrized presence pin across the 5 sites + AST-pin (wrap precedes downstream call in `create_account`) + direct-ccxt unwrap pin for `test_account_preview`. TestAccountRegistryUnwrapsAtCache (2) — `type() is str` check on cache values after add_account / update_account (would catch a SensitiveStr leak into the cache breaking ccxt). TestConsumersUnwrapDecryptReturn (3) — source-pin each of the 3 production decrypt consumers. TestTask100NarrowScopeUnchanged (1) — anti-regression: HIGH-024's connections.upsert unwrap still present. TestTracebackExposureScenarios (2) — logging + locals-repr behavioral simulation. TestEncryptRoundTripPreserved (1) — anti-over-correction: encrypt(SensitiveStr) → ciphertext → decrypt → SensitiveStr round-trips correctly.
  FBF: Stashing the 5 modified source files → 17/21 tests fail (everything except the 4 that exercise SensitiveStr in isolation: traceback-simulation + Task-100 anti-regression). Restoring → 21/21 pass. Full suite: 1553 passed, 6 skipped, 29 s — no regressions.
  Latent observations during investigation:
    - `scripts/test_papi_conditional.py:45` calls `decrypt()` and now receives SensitiveStr. Out of scope — utility/diagnostic script, not production path. If the script is ever folded into a route handler, it inherits the new wrap automatically; current state is acceptable.
    - No additional credential-touching code paths surfaced beyond the 5 audit-cited routes_accounts entry points + the 3 decrypt() consumers (account_registry / connections / ws_manager). `core/crypto.encrypt` accepts SensitiveStr transparently via `.encode()` C-slot — verified by the round-trip test.
  Discovered-during: Task 100 HIGH-024 investigation (entry-point inventory revealed the parallel exchange-credential surface).
  Cross-reference: HIGH-024 (parent finding; same wrap technique applied).
  Resolved-during: Task 116
  Target phase: Phase 4 follow-up or Phase 5. Approach (a) chosen.
  Filed-during: Task 100

### [HIGH-030] [Correctness] core/adapters/bybit/rest_adapter.py — Bybit account-field validation missing (parallel to HIGH-013)
  Description: Parallel to HIGH-013's Binance fix. Bybit's V5 fetch_account uses the same `info.get(field, 0) or 0` pattern for critical balance/equity fields without validation. If Bybit's response omits a field or returns null, the account state silently corrupts to 0, producing the same downstream consequences identified in HIGH-013's downstream analysis: risk_engine.py:351 falls back to 1.0 phantom equity (micro-positions), or BOD/SOW init freezes at 0 (data_cache.py:670).
  Evidence (core/adapters/bybit/rest_adapter.py fetch_account, lines 86-99):
      total_equity = float(account.get("totalEquity", 0) or 0)
      available = float(account.get("totalAvailableBalance", 0) or 0)
      unrealized = float(account.get("totalPerpUPL", 0) or 0)
      initial_margin = float(account.get("totalInitialMargin", 0) or 0)
      maint_margin = float(account.get("totalMaintenanceMargin", 0) or 0)
      ...
      for coin in coins:
          if coin.get("coin") == "USDT":
              total_equity = float(coin.get("equity", total_equity) or total_equity)
              available = float(coin.get("availableToWithdraw", available) or available)
              unrealized = float(coin.get("unrealisedPnl", unrealized) or unrealized)
  Additional structural concern unique to Bybit: the response is deeply nested (`info → result → list[0]`). Survey confirmed at lines 78-84 — if `info` is missing, OR `result` is missing, OR `list` is empty, the `account` dict is `{}` and ALL fields cascade-default to 0 silently. The fix must validate the structural path as well as the leaf fields.
  Critical-field set (parallel to Binance's totalWalletBalance + availableBalance): totalEquity (or per-coin USDT equity) + totalAvailableBalance (or per-coin availableToWithdraw). Non-critical (warn + default): totalPerpUPL, totalInitialMargin, totalMaintenanceMargin.
  Impact: Identical to HIGH-013 — silent equity-state corruption that downstream consumers can't distinguish from legitimate zero state. Risk-engine sizing-collapse OR frozen BOD/SOW baseline.
  Recommended fix: Apply HIGH-013's Shape A (raise ValueError on missing critical fields) for totalEquity + totalAvailableBalance, plus a structural-path check that raises if `info["result"]["list"][0]` is missing/empty (Bybit-specific addition). Apply Shape B (default + log) for the three non-critical fields. Match the structure of Binance's fix in core/adapters/binance/rest_adapter.py:113-150.
  Effort: small. ~25-35 LOC (slightly more than HIGH-013's Binance fix due to the structural-path validation); ~6-7 tests parallel to TestBinanceFetchAccountCriticalFieldValidation.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 117 — adapted-mirror validation in `core/adapters/bybit/rest_adapter.py:fetch_account()`.
  Implementation note: Approach choice **adapted mirror**, not pure mirror. Bybit's V5 response has two layers (account-level aggregate via `result.list[0]` + per-coin USDT via `coin[]`); pure mirror of Binance's single `totalWalletBalance` check would have rejected legitimate isolated-margin UNIFIED configurations where the account-level aggregates zero out and per-coin USDT is authoritative. The validation accepts either layer as the source of truth: a critical field is "present" iff it appears at account level OR in the USDT coin entry. Both missing → raise ValueError with Bybit-specific guidance ("Check Bybit account-type configuration (UNIFIED vs CONTRACT)"). Account-type decision: queries UNIFIED (existing code passes `params={"type": "unified"}`); validation matches the V5 UNIFIED response schema. Critical-field set: `totalEquity` (or `coin.USDT.equity`) + `totalAvailableBalance` (or `coin.USDT.availableToWithdraw`). Non-critical: `totalPerpUPL`, `totalInitialMargin`, `totalMaintenanceMargin` — warn + default 0 (parallel to Binance's non-critical handling). Structural-path checks: `result.list` must be non-empty, and `list[0]` must be a dict — both fail loud before touching field-level extraction.
  Tests (tests/test_task117_bybit_account_validation.py, 11 cases): TestBybitFetchAccountStructuralValidation (2) — empty list + non-dict list[0]. TestBybitFetchAccountRequiredFields (4) — both layers missing equity, both missing available, account-level-only succeeds, per-coin USDT-only succeeds. TestPhantomEquityScenarioBlocked (1) — malformed response no longer produces silent zero. TestBybitFetchAccountNonCriticalFields (1) — margins absent → warn-only. TestSourceHasHigh030Reference (1) — anti-revert source-pin. TestAntiRegression (2) — Binance HIGH-013 still raises; Bybit HIGH-028 reconciliation override still present.
  FBF: Stashing `core/adapters/bybit/rest_adapter.py` → 7/11 tests fail (the 4 that pass exercise valid-response paths or the unrelated Binance/HIGH-028 anti-regression pins). Restoring → 11/11 pass. Full suite: 1564 passed, 6 skipped, 24 s — no regressions.
  Latent observations during investigation:
    - Bybit's `fetch_positions` (line 203) and `fetch_orders` paths use the same `.get(field, 0) or 0` shape but on contracts / price / qty fields. Position-level silent zero is less catastrophic than equity-level silent zero (skipped positions are visible to the operator via the open-positions list; corrupted equity silently mis-sizes future trades). Consider future audit work for parity, but not in scope for HIGH-030.
    - The Binance adapter has a parallel `fetch_positions` shape (line 162) — same observation applies symmetrically. Out of scope.
    - `MED-049` (filed Task 115) and the Bybit position-fetch / order-fetch validation gaps could naturally bundle into a "Bundle B remnant" task if revisited.
  Discovered-during: Task 102 HIGH-013 implementation (Binance fix surfaced parallel concern); confirmed in Task 102.1 source survey.
  Cross-reference: HIGH-013 (parent finding; Binance fix). HIGH-028 (sibling Bybit-deferred work from Task 99 — both Bybit-only follow-ups to Binance fixes from the same audit pass; both now resolved).
  Resolved-during: Task 117
  Target phase: Phase 4 (alongside HIGH-028) or Phase 5.
  Filed-during: Task 102.1

### [HIGH-031] [Correctness] core/adapters/mexc/rest_adapter.py — MEXC account-field validation missing (parallel to HIGH-013)
  Description: Same shape as HIGH-013 and HIGH-030. MEXC's fetch_account uses `usdt.get(field, 0) or 0` for the two critical fields (total / free). Plus a structural-path concern parallel to Bybit's: `usdt = raw.get("USDT", raw.get("total", {}))` falls back to an empty dict if both keys are missing, cascading all fields to 0 silently.
  Evidence (core/adapters/mexc/rest_adapter.py fetch_account, lines 111-119):
      raw = await self._run(self._ex.fetch_balance)
      usdt = raw.get("USDT", raw.get("total", {}))
      return NormalizedAccount(
          currency="USDT",
          total_equity=float(usdt.get("total", 0) or 0),
          available_margin=float(usdt.get("free", 0) or 0),
          unrealized_pnl=0.0,  # CCXT doesn't split this for MEXC
      )
  Critical-field set: usdt["total"] (→ total_equity), usdt["free"] (→ available_margin). Plus the structural-path: `raw.get("USDT")` must succeed.
  Impact: Reduced relative to HIGH-013 / HIGH-030 because the MEXC adapter is read-only (capabilities.orders=False at adapter init — Task 9). Phantom-zero equity does not gate position placement for MEXC accounts; the harm is bounded to display, analytics, BOD/SOW init, and any downstream calculations that read MEXC-account total_equity (e.g., aggregate portfolio reporting). Still a real silent-corruption vector for the dashboards that DO consume MEXC equity.
  Recommended fix: Apply HIGH-013's Shape A for both fields (total + free) plus a structural-path check for the USDT/total fallback. Smaller footprint than HIGH-030 because there are no non-critical fields to handle (MEXC only exposes the two; unrealized_pnl is hardcoded to 0.0).
  Effort: small. ~15-20 LOC + ~4 tests.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 118 — pure-mirror validation in `core/adapters/mexc/rest_adapter.py:fetch_account()`.
  Implementation note: Approach choice **pure mirror** of HIGH-013 with one Bybit-style structural-path addition (the `raw['USDT'] or raw['total']` fallback). Simpler than HIGH-030 because MEXC's adapter exposes only the two critical fields and there are no non-critical fields requiring warn-but-proceed handling (unrealized_pnl is hardcoded 0.0 per CCXT's MEXC limitation). Structural-path checks: response is a dict, and either `raw['USDT']` or `raw['total']` resolves to a dict. Critical-field checks: `usdt['total']` and `usdt['free']` must be present AND non-null (null distinguished from absent because CCXT can surface explicit None on partial responses). Zero values pass — distinguishes legitimately empty USDT account from broken response.
  Read-only context: MEXC `capabilities.orders=False` (Task 9, line 48) bounds the harm to dashboards, BOD/SOW init, and aggregate-portfolio reporting; no live-trade sizing impact. The validation still fails loud because the silent-corruption shape is identical to HIGH-013/HIGH-030 — operator should see the failure, not silently bad data.
  Tests (tests/test_task118_mexc_account_validation.py, 15 cases): TestMexcFetchAccountStructuralPath (4) — non-dict, both keys missing, total fallback works, USDT precedence. TestMexcFetchAccountRequiredFields (5) — missing total / missing free / null total / null free / zero values succeed. TestMexcFetchAccountValidResponse (1) — anti-over-correction on full response. TestSourceHasHigh031Reference (1) — anti-revert source-pin. TestAntiRegression (4) — Binance HIGH-013, Bybit HIGH-030, Bybit HIGH-028 reconciliation override, MEXC capabilities still read-only.
  FBF: Stashing `core/adapters/mexc/rest_adapter.py` → 7/15 tests fail; the 8 passes exercise valid-response paths or anti-regression pins independent of the validation block. Restoring → 15/15 pass. Full suite: 1579 passed, 6 skipped, 26 s.
  Discovered-during: Task 102 HIGH-013 implementation; confirmed in Task 102.1 source survey.
  Cross-reference: HIGH-013 (parent finding; Binance fix). HIGH-030 (sibling Bybit fix). HIGH-013 + HIGH-030 + HIGH-031 close the "adapter response sanity" family — all three production REST adapters now have account-field validation. The MED-049 (market_type whitelist) + MED-050 (position/order-fetch validation) follow-ups remain for full defense-in-depth.
  Resolved-during: Task 118
  Target phase: Phase 5.
  Filed-during: Task 102.1

### [MED-044] [Maintainability] core/ws_manager.py:_reconnect_user (recursion vs iteration)
  Description: _reconnect_user uses async tail-recursion for retry loop. Bounded by config.WS_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS=15 today, so safe, but stack depth scales linearly with the config value.
  Evidence: `await _reconnect_user(attempt + 1)` at line 496; bound via line-477 guard. If WS_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS were ever raised to several hundred (e.g., to "be more generous about retries"), Python's default recursion limit (1000 frames, plus async coroutine overhead) could become a real risk.
  Impact: Defense-in-depth concern only at current config. Real failure mode if config is bumped.
  Recommended fix: Convert tail-recursion to a `while` loop in _reconnect_user. ~10 LOC. Preserves all existing behavior; eliminates the recursion-depth dependency on a config value.
  Effort: small
  Status: OPEN — target Phase 4 alongside other reliability hardening
  Discovered-during: Task 89 (CRIT-005 verification surfaced the recursion-vs-iteration concern)

### [MED-046] [Reliability] core/database.py — _CREATE_STATEMENTS.split(";") fragile against SQL line-comment semicolons
  Description: core/database.py:465 splits the CREATE TABLE blob on ";" to execute each statement separately. The split is naive — it includes semicolons that appear inside SQL line comments (`-- ... ;`), producing incomplete statement fragments that sqlite3 rejects with "incomplete input".
  Evidence: Task 104a hit `sqlite3.OperationalError: incomplete input` during the test run because two added `-- ...` comments contained semicolons ("...link_window_seconds; NULL → use account default" and "...crypto-futures swing/intraday workflows; tunable via..."). Resolved inline by rewriting the comments without semicolons, but the underlying fragility persists for any future contributor adding multi-sentence SQL comments.
  Impact: Loud failure mode (test errors during init, fresh-install fails to come up) rather than silent data corruption. Practical impact is wasted developer time + a debugging side-quest the first time someone hits it.
  Recommended fix: Either (a) switch from split-and-execute-each to sqlite3's `executescript()`, which handles full SQL blobs with comments correctly, OR (b) strip line comments before splitting. Option (a) is simpler and idiomatic. The migration list is already iterated as a Python list rather than a SQL blob, so this only affects the CREATE TABLE bootstrap.
  Effort: small. ~5-10 LOC + a regression test that exercises a SQL blob containing a comment with a semicolon.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 119 — option (a) applied. `core/database.py:DatabaseManager.initialize` now calls `self._conn.executescript(_CREATE_STATEMENTS)` instead of looping over `_CREATE_STATEMENTS.strip().split(";")`. executescript() uses sqlite3's proper SQL tokenizer (handles `--` line comments, including ones containing `;`).
  Implementation note: The two leading `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` / `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` calls remain as explicit `execute(...)` calls before `executescript()` because PRAGMAs inside executescript() are harmless no-ops here. Migration list (the per-ALTER loop after `executescript()`) is unchanged — it was already iterated as a Python list, not split from a blob.
  Tests (TestMed046ExecutescriptHandlesCommentSemicolons, 3 cases): direct executescript() reproduction with a comment-with-semicolon SQL blob; anti-revert FBF asserting the OLD `.split(";")` loop raises `sqlite3.OperationalError` on the same input; source-pin that initialize() now contains `executescript` and no longer contains the old `_CREATE_STATEMENTS.strip().split` signature.
  FBF: the naive split test is itself the FBF — it constructs the broken behavior in isolation and asserts it fails.
  Discovered-during: Task 104a HIGH-027 backend implementation.
  Filed-during: Task 104b
  Resolved-during: Task 119
  Target phase: Phase 5 or any future task that touches database.py.

### [MED-047] [Reliability/Testing] Template wiring pins use source-string greps without compile-test
  Description: Wiring pins for template-touching tasks (introduced in Task 97.1, extended through Phase 4) verify presence of strings / symbols in template source via grep. They do NOT compile or render the template. Jinja2 syntax errors (including Python-only constructs like list comprehensions, walrus operators, f-strings) pass source-string greps and only surface at actual render time — which is at runtime in a browser session, not in pytest.
  Evidence: Task 104b shipped `{% set lw_is_preset = lw_current in [p[0] for p in lw_presets] %}` (Python list comprehension, not Jinja2-valid) into `templates/fragments/account_detail.html`. Source-string grep wiring pin passed. Full pytest passed. v2.4.1 release tagged. Bug discovered only by Claude-in-Chrome's audit-01 session via real browser-driven render. Tracked separately as the FE-CRIT-001 audit-01 finding (frontend-audit-01 doc, pending merge of Task 107 into main). Hotfix shipped on the `v2.4.1/hotfix-t108-crit001` branch (also pending main merge).
  Impact: Template syntax errors silently ship to release. Could affect any other latent Jinja2-incompatible expression added during Phase 1-4 if one exists. Affects defense-in-depth for future template work.
  Recommended fix: Standard pattern for template wiring pins, going forward:
    1. Source-string grep for the symbol / element (existing pattern, retain).
    2. Compile + render with minimal context using Jinja2's FileSystemLoader.
    3. Assert rendered output contains expected HTML structure.
  Apply pattern to: all existing wiring pins that touch templates (retrofit during Phase 5 work that touches the relevant files). Apply going forward to: any new template-touching task.
  Effort: small (per-template). Per-task overhead: ~5-10 LOC for the compile-render test.
  Status: OPEN — filed for Phase 5 housekeeping; retrofit done opportunistically when each template is next touched.
  Discovered-during: Task 108 FE-CRIT-001 hotfix investigation (the audit-01 finding pre-dates Task 108 but its underlying class of bug is what MED-047 documents).
  Filed-during: Task 109
  Target phase: Phase 5 (housekeeping mini-phase, opportunistic retrofits).

### [MED-048] [Security/Input] routes_accounts.create_account trusts client-supplied `exchange` value
  Description: After Task 111 constrained the Add Account modal's EXCHANGE dropdown to registered adapters, the server-side handler still accepts arbitrary `exchange` strings on POST. The form parameter is passed through to `account_registry.add_account()` and persisted to the DB without validation against the adapter registry. A subsequent adapter lookup (during connection test or scheduled refresh) will raise `ValueError("No REST adapter registered for '<bogus>:<market_type>'")` from `core/adapters/registry.py:get_rest_adapter`, but by then the account row already exists in inconsistent state.
  Evidence: `api/routes_accounts.py:create_account` (and `add_account_modal`, `update_account_detail`) accept `exchange: str = Form(...)` with no whitelist check. `core/adapters/registry.py` exposes `list_registered()` and the new `list_rest_exchanges()` helper — either is suitable for validating against.
  Pattern: parallel to HIGH-022 / HIGH-023 / MED-036 from Task 97 (route input validation) — client trust on form params, server-side validation gap. Also parallel to Task 101's column whitelist (`_ALLOWED_FILTER_COLS`).
  Impact: Defense-in-depth gap. Current UI cannot exercise this since the dropdown is now constrained to registered options, but direct curl / scripted POST bypasses the UI. Failure mode is loud (adapter lookup raises) rather than silent corruption — practical impact is debugging time + an orphan account row to clean up.
  Recommended fix: Validate `exchange` against `list_registered()["rest"]` (or the registry directly) at route entry in all 3 account-creation handlers. Reject with `HTMLResponse(..., status_code=400)` carrying an inline error fragment. Same pattern as Task 97's `_validate_date_range` and Task 101's `validate_sort_params`.
  Effort: small. ~20-30 LOC across the 3 handlers + a shared `_validate_exchange` helper. Tests parallel to Task 97 / Task 101 patterns.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 114 — shared `_validate_exchange()` helper + `is_valid_rest_exchange()` / `get_supported_rest_exchanges()` registry primitives.
  Implementation note: `core/adapters/registry.py` exposes `is_valid_rest_exchange(exchange_id, market_type)` (strict case-sensitive lookup against `_REST_REGISTRY`) and `get_supported_rest_exchanges(market_type)` (sorted list for error messages). `api/routes_accounts._validate_exchange(exchange, market_type)` maps the legacy `future` form value through `map_market_type()` before lookup, returns `""` on success or an operator-friendly message like `"Unknown exchange: 'fakeexch'. Supported: binance, bybit, mexc."` on rejection. Four call sites wired: `create_account` (POST /accounts, JSONResponse 400), `add_account_modal` (POST /accounts/add-and-reload, HTMLResponse 400 fragment), `test_account_preview` (POST /accounts/test-preview, HTMLResponse 400), `update_account_detail` (POST /accounts/{id}/update, HTMLResponse 400 — only validates when `exchange` form field is present; falls back to the account's current `market_type` if only `exchange` is being changed). Case-sensitivity decision: strict lowercase only. Registry keys are lowercase by convention; accepting case variants would mask frontend tampering, and the Task 111 dropdown emits canonical lowercase IDs.
  Tests (tests/test_task114_exchange_validation.py, 19 cases): TestRegistryValidator (7) — canonical-valid, unknown rejected, empty rejected, None rejected, uppercase rejected, unknown market_type rejects known exchange, supported-list matches registry. TestValidateExchangeRouteHelper (4) — valid pair → empty string, invalid → quoted message with supported list, uppercase rejected, empty rejected. TestRouteWiring (5) — source-pin each of the 4 routes calls `_validate_exchange`, plus AST-pin asserting in `create_account` the validator call precedes `account_registry.add_account` (would catch a "validate after persist" revert). TestRejectionResponseShape (3) — all three creation routes return `status_code=400` on rejection.
  FBF: Stashing `api/routes_accounts.py` only (registry helpers preserved) → 12/19 tests fail (route-wiring + rejection-shape + validator-helper tests). Restoring → 19/19 pass.
  Discovered-during: Task 111 latent observation (surfaced in the Task 111 report).
  Filed-during: Task 112
  Resolved-during: Task 114
  Target phase: Phase 5 Bundle B.

### [MED-049] [Security/Input] update_account_detail accepts `market_type` without independent whitelist
  Description: Task 114's MED-048 fix whitelisted client-supplied `exchange` at route entry across the 4 account-creation/preview/update handlers. The parallel `market_type` form field is not whitelisted independently — only validated indirectly when paired with `exchange`. A market_type-only edit (changing market_type on an existing account while exchange stays the same) skips re-validation. Adapter lookup at activation would still fail (`get_rest_adapter()` raises on unknown key), but the persist-then-fail-later anti-pattern persists for that field.
  Evidence: `api/routes_accounts.py:update_account_detail` — when `exchange is None` and `market_type is not None`, the validator block is skipped entirely. `db.update_account(..., market_type=market_type)` runs unconditionally.
  Pattern: parallel to MED-048 / HIGH-022 / HIGH-023 / MED-036 (client-trust at route entry).
  Impact: Defense-in-depth gap, same shape as MED-048 (pre-fix): direct POST bypasses the UI. Failure mode is loud (adapter lookup raises) rather than silent corruption — practical impact is debugging time + an inconsistent account row to clean up. Real exposure is small because the UI's market_type dropdown is static (only "future" / "spot" today), but the gap is symmetric to MED-048 and worth closing for completeness.
  Recommended fix: whitelist `market_type` at route entry. Two options: (a) hard-code the allowed set (`{"future", "spot"}`) since the registry already maps `future → linear_perpetual` and only these legacy DB values are valid; or (b) derive the allowed set from registered adapters (`{k.split(":", 1)[1] for k in _REST_REGISTRY}`) via a new `get_supported_market_types()` registry helper paralleling `get_supported_rest_exchanges()`. Option (b) is more future-proof (when a new market_type adapter is registered, validation tracks it automatically); option (a) is simpler. Reject with 400 + structured error fragment.
  Effort: small (~10-15 LOC + a few tests parallel to Task 114).
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 119 — option (b) applied. `core/adapters/registry.py` adds `is_valid_market_type(market_type)` and `get_supported_market_types()`. `api/routes_accounts._validate_market_type()` wires the registry check. `update_account_detail` calls it inside the `if market_type is not None:` branch — symmetric to MED-048's exchange check.
  Implementation note: Strict case-sensitive (parallel to MED-048). Accepts `"future"` (legacy DB alias — `map_market_type` rewrites to `linear_perpetual`) plus any canonical registered market_type. Today only `linear_perpetual` is registered, so the supported set is `{"future", "linear_perpetual"}`. As new adapters register (spot, inverse_perpetual, etc.), the validator picks them up automatically without code changes.
  Tests (TestMarketTypeRegistryHelpers + TestValidateMarketTypeRouteHelper + TestUpdateAccountDetailWiresMarketTypeValidation, 11 cases): registry leaf checks (legacy alias, canonical, unknown, empty, None, uppercase, supported-list contents); route-helper integration (valid pair returns empty, invalid returns message, uppercase rejected); source-pin that `update_account_detail` references `_validate_market_type` and the MED-049 anchor.
  FBF: stashing all 6 modified source files → 7 of the MED-049 tests fail (registry helpers absent, route helper absent, source-pin absent); restoring → 11/11 pass.
  Discovered-during: Task 114 (surfaced in the Task 114 report as a latent observation alongside the MED-048 fix).
  Filed-during: Task 115
  Resolved-during: Task 119
  Target phase: Phase 5 Bundle B remnant.

### [MED-050] [Reliability/Data] Position-fetch + order-fetch use silent-zero fallback across adapters
  Description: Task 117 (HIGH-030) surfaced that Bybit's `fetch_positions` (`core/adapters/bybit/rest_adapter.py:203`) uses `.get(field, 0) or 0` on contracts / price / qty fields — silent zero on missing data. Symmetric pattern in `core/adapters/binance/rest_adapter.py:162` and almost certainly in `core/adapters/mexc/rest_adapter.py:123-149`. Account-level equity is now validated end-to-end (HIGH-013 / HIGH-030 / HIGH-031); position-level and order-level fields are not.
  Evidence — Bybit (rest_adapter.py:209-213):
      contracts = float(r.get("contracts", 0) or 0)
      if contracts == 0:
          continue
      side_raw = r.get("side", "")
      side = "LONG" if side_raw == "long" else "SHORT"
  Same shape in `fetch_open_orders` (likely lines parallel to Binance:181-209) and across `fetch_positions` in all three adapters. A response with missing `contracts` returns 0, which the `continue` skip silently drops — the position vanishes from the returned list.
  Impact: Position-level silent zero is LESS catastrophic than equity-level (which the resolved HIGH-013/030/031 family addresses). Operator visibility differs:
    - Equity-zero (pre-fix) → silent corruption that downstream sizing math reads as 1.0 phantom equity → micro-positions on next trade. Hard to detect.
    - Position-zero → the position simply disappears from the open-positions list. Visible to the operator via UI (a position they know exists isn't shown).
    The harm is still real — distorted PnL display, missing rows in the positions table, broken aggregate calculations — but the failure mode is operator-detectable rather than silent. Severity MED rather than HIGH for that reason.
  Pattern: same trust-but-verify, applied to position / order fetch paths across all three adapters.
  Recommended fix: Mirror HIGH-013's validation pattern at each fetch site. Critical fields per fetch path:
    - fetch_positions: symbol, side, contracts/size, entry_price.
    - fetch_open_orders: orderId, symbol, side, type, status, quantity.
    - fetch_my_trades: tradeId, orderId, symbol, side, price, qty.
    Bundle with MED-049 (market_type whitelist) as "Bundle B remnant: defense-in-depth at all fetch sites" — coherent cluster, ~50-80 LOC across 2-3 adapters + 12-15 tests.
  Effort: medium. Multiple sites across 2-3 adapters; the existing HIGH-013/030/031 patterns are templates.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 119 — per-record validation across all 3 adapters at fetch_positions, fetch_open_orders, and Binance's fetch_algo_open_orders.
  Implementation note: **Granularity choice: per-record drop, NOT fail-closed at the response level**. Different from HIGH-013/030/031 (which fail-closed at account-fetch). Reasoning — account-fetch failure corrupts ALL downstream sizing (one bad equity field cascades into phantom-zero across the entire risk engine). Position/order data is list-level: a vanishing position is operator-visible in the UI (row goes missing), but blanking the entire list silently is far harder to detect (account looks empty, broken aggregates everywhere). Per-record drop preserves visibility of bad rows via log.warning while letting valid records through unchanged. Critical fields per site (chosen as "can't route or identify the record without it"):
    - fetch_positions (Binance): symbol + positionAmt
    - fetch_positions (Bybit, MEXC): symbol + contracts (CCXT-normalized field)
    - fetch_open_orders (Binance): orderId + symbol
    - fetch_open_orders (Bybit, MEXC): id + symbol (CCXT-normalized)
    - fetch_algo_open_orders (Binance): algoId + symbol
  Non-critical fields (entry_price, mark_price, quantity, etc.) keep the existing `.get(..., 0) or 0` default — operator can spot zero-priced/zero-qty rows in the UI; routing fields are the only ones whose absence makes the record un-displayable.
  Tests (TestMed050* classes, 7 cases): per-adapter missing-symbol / missing-amount tests + cross-adapter granularity test (one bad record amid many good drops only the bad one). Anti-over-correction: valid records still parse normally (no false-positive skips).
  FBF: stashing all 3 adapter files → 6 MED-050 tests fail (the missing-field cases all rely on the new validation block). Restoring → 7/7 pass.
  Latent observations during MED-050 implementation:
    - `fetch_user_trades` (all 3 adapters) has the same shape — not in MED-050 scope per the original audit text, but the gap is symmetric. Filing as MED-051 was considered but rejected: the surface is smaller (trades are historical / read-only display), and adding more findings to the MED bucket inflates the ledger without operational urgency. Recorded here for future audit reference.
    - The fee-rate fetch + funding-rate fetch use single-value response shapes, not list-of-records — the trust-but-verify pattern doesn't apply the same way. No follow-up filed.
  Discovered-during: Task 117 latent observation (surfaced in the Task 117 report).
  Filed-during: Task 118
  Resolved-during: Task 119
  Target phase: Phase 5 Bundle B remnant (with MED-049).

### [MED-045] [Reliability] main.py:50 — log rotation fails on Windows under multi-process
  Description: Python's stdlib RotatingFileHandler uses os.rename for rotation, which fails on Windows when another process holds the file open. Multi-worker uvicorn (or any subprocess that opens the log file) triggers PermissionError [WinError 32] on every rotation attempt, causing the rotation to silently fail. Log file grows unbounded; rotation-attempt error spam appears in stderr.
  Evidence: Runtime stack trace from engine startup, captured 2026-05-18 after commit 1cb8787 (Task 103). PermissionError at logging/handlers.py inside os.rename(source, dest) — source file held by a uvicorn worker subprocess (visible in call stack: multiprocessing.spawn → uvicorn server → engine startup).
  Impact:
    - Logfile grows unbounded; disk-space exhaustion possible over long-running deployments.
    - stderr / console noise on every rotation attempt after the maxBytes threshold is reached.
    - Historical log integrity degrades — rotation discipline is broken, so investigating past incidents requires manually managing the growing logfile.
    - Affects both development (cmd-window noise) and production (audit-trail reliability).
  Recommended fix: Replace logging.handlers.RotatingFileHandler with ConcurrentRotatingFileHandler from the concurrent-log-handler PyPI package. Drop-in replacement that uses OS-level file locks (msvcrt on Windows, fcntl on Unix) to coordinate rotation across processes. Single-line config change in main.py + add concurrent-log-handler to requirements.txt.
  Effort: small. ~5 LOC + dependency addition.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 103.5 — ConcurrentRotatingFileHandler swap.
  Implementation note: main.py:46-65 — `from concurrent_log_handler import ConcurrentRotatingFileHandler` replaces the stdlib import; handler instantiation passes the same maxBytes (10 MiB), backupCount (5), encoding (utf-8) plus `use_gzip=False` to match prior behavior. core/log_formatter.py docstring updated to reference the new handler. requirements.txt pinned to `concurrent-log-handler>=0.9.20,<1.0` (installed at 0.9.29). Single handler — no other RotatingFileHandler instances in the codebase.
  Tests (tests/test_task103_5_log_handler.py, 8 cases):
    - TestLogHandlerClass (3): source-pin asserts main.py imports ConcurrentRotatingFileHandler; anti-revert pin asserts the old stdlib import is NOT present; instance check asserts main._json_handler is a ConcurrentRotatingFileHandler.
    - TestLogHandlerConfigPreserved (3): maxBytes, backupCount, encoding all unchanged after the swap.
    - TestSingleProcessRotationSmoke (2): real ConcurrentRotatingFileHandler with maxBytes=200 + 10 log records → rotated .1 file appears; handler.close() does not raise on empty-write path (lifespan-shutdown safety).
  Manual smoke: cross-process rotation can't be unit-tested cheaply; verify post-commit by starting the engine and confirming no PermissionError [WinError 32] in the console after log rotation should have triggered.
  Discovered-during: Runtime observation by operator after Task 103 commit (1cb8787).
  Target phase: Phase 4 (infrastructure follow-up, addressed same commit).

---

## 2. Security

### [HIGH-001] [Security] main.py / api/router.py
  Description: No authentication or authorization on any API endpoint
  Evidence: No middleware, no `Depends(verify_token)`, no session validation. All routes — including account creation, trading history, and admin — are fully open.
  Impact: Any network user can access all accounts, balances, positions, and trading endpoints
  Recommended fix: Add FastAPI security middleware (JWT or session-based) before all routes
  Effort: large
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5a)
  Sweep evidence: grep of `Depends|HTTPBearer|verify_token|JWT|authenticate` across main.py: 0 hits. Across api/: only api/routes_platform.py has any auth-adjacent code (and that's platform identification, not request auth).
  Sweep note: Real for the as-written claim. Note: this is a single-user-deployment app per the Overall Verdict ("operationally solid for a single-user deployment"); the audit's severity weighting may treat this differently when multi-user is a target. Status quo of "fully open" is accurate either way.

### [MED-003] [Security] api/routes_orders.py:46-50, api/routes_history.py:70-75
  Description: `sort_by` and `sort_dir` parameters passed to DB layer without whitelist validation in some routes
  Evidence: Query helpers do whitelist `sort_by` against `_ALLOWED_SORT_COLS` sets — but the route handlers don't validate before passing. Defense-in-depth gap.
  Impact: SQL injection if whitelist is bypassed or new routes skip it
  Recommended fix: Validate at route level; add `sort_dir in ("ASC", "DESC")` check in routes
  Effort: small
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 101 — shared validator at core/sql_safety.py:validate_sort_params; wired into all 7 sortable route handlers in routes_orders.py (4) + routes_history.py (3).
  Implementation note: validate_sort_params(sort_by, sort_dir, allowed_cols) raises HTTPException(400) on unknown column or invalid direction; upper-cases the direction so the DB-layer match against ("ASC", "DESC") doesn't need its own normalization. Routes call it with the appropriate DB-layer sort-cols set (db._ORDERS_SORT_COLS / db._FILLS_SORT_COLS / db._CLOSED_POS_SORT_COLS / db._EXCHANGE_HISTORY_SORT_COLS / db._PRE_TRADE_SORT_COLS / db._TRADE_HISTORY_SORT_COLS). Visible 400 replaces the prior silent fallback to a default — operator can now see when bad input is rejected. routes_analytics.py:296-317 was inspected but does in-Python sorting (not SQL ORDER BY) and already has an inline whitelist; out of MED-003 scope.
  Tests (tests/test_task101_sql_injection_defense.py): TestValidateSortParams (7 cases: valid/invalid combos, lowercase upper-casing, injection in sort_by, injection in sort_dir, empty-string sort_dir), TestRouteWiring (4 cases: import + call-count pins for both modules), TestRoutesRejectInjection (3 e2e cases via TestClient: injection payloads return 400, valid request returns 200).
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5a)
  Sweep evidence (routes_orders.py:43-50):
      sort_by: str = "created_at_ms", sort_dir: str = "DESC",
      ...
      rows, total = await db.query_open_orders(
          account_id=app_state.active_account_id,
          sort_by=sort_by, sort_dir=sort_dir, ...
      )
  Sweep note: Route accepts sort_by/sort_dir as raw strings and forwards them unchanged. DB layer at core/db_orders.py uses _ORDERS_SORT_COLS / _FILLS_SORT_COLS sets to whitelist, so today the path is safe end-to-end. But the route-level gap is real defense-in-depth — any future query helper that forgets the whitelist becomes injectable from this route. Audit's framing is accurate.

### [MED-004] [Security] core/crypto.py:37-38
  Description: Key derivation uses plain SHA256 hash, not a proper KDF (PBKDF2/scrypt)
  Evidence: `key_bytes = hashlib.sha256(master.encode()).digest()` — no salt, no iteration count
  Impact: If master key is leaked, Fernet key derivable in microseconds
  Recommended fix: Use PBKDF2 with random salt; acceptable for current single-user deployment but should improve before multi-user
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5a)
  Sweep evidence (crypto.py:37):
      key_bytes = hashlib.sha256(master.encode()).digest()
      return Fernet(base64.urlsafe_b64encode(key_bytes))
  Sweep note: Bare SHA256, no salt, no iteration count. Confirmed at line 37. Caveat the audit itself raised applies — single-user deployment makes the offline-crack-cost trade-off acceptable for now, but the recommendation to upgrade is correct on its merits.

### [LOW-001] [Security] api/routes_history.py:41
  Description: Ticker input uppercased but not validated (could contain special chars)
  Evidence: `"ticker": ticker.upper()` — no format check
  Impact: Minor; parameterized queries prevent injection, but good practice to validate
  Recommended fix: Validate against `[A-Z0-9]+` pattern
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5a)
  Sweep evidence (routes_history.py:41):
      "ticker": ticker.upper(), "side": side,
  Sweep note: No regex/format validation on ticker. Confirmed. Audit's own severity assessment is right — parameterized queries downstream prevent injection, so this is hygiene rather than exploitable. Low-priority.
