## 3. Performance

### [CRIT-001] [Performance] api/routes_orders.py:159-184
  Description: N+1 query pattern in position_fills endpoint
  Evidence: For each fill in the loop, 2 individual queries fire: `SELECT * FROM pre_trade_log WHERE calc_id=?` and `SELECT order_type FROM orders WHERE exchange_order_id=?`. A position with 50 fills = 100 extra queries.
  Impact: Endpoint latency scales linearly with fill count; visible delay on positions with many fills
  Recommended fix: Batch-fetch pre_trade_log and orders by calc_id/order_id lists, then join in memory
  Effort: medium
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 88 — batch-fetch via IN-clause replaces per-fill queries
  Implementation note (Task 88):
    Added db.get_pretrade_logs_by_calc_ids() and db.get_order_types_by_ids() in
    core/db_orders.py. routes_orders.frag_position_fills now executes 3 queries
    total (fills fetch + 2 batched enrichment queries) regardless of fill count,
    down from 2N + 1 where N = entry-fill count.
    Benchmark on a 50-fill position (in-memory SQLite, 15-iter median):
      N+1 form:   10.6 ms / 100 queries
      Batch form: 0.34 ms / 2 queries  (excluding the prior fills fetch)
      Speedup:    31× / 50× fewer queries
    Real disk-backed DB will show larger absolute deltas because each per-fill
    query previously paid a roundtrip cost.
    Helpers dedupe input ID lists; SQLite default param limit is 32766
    (since 3.32, 2020) so no chunking needed at realistic fill counts.
    Phase 6 (db._conn refactor, Task 92) will systematize similar helpers
    across routes_orders.py — this task seeds the pattern.

### [HIGH-003] [Performance] core/database.py (missing indexes)
  Description: No index on `pre_trade_log.calc_id` or `orders.calc_id` despite frequent lookup by calc_id
  Evidence: `SELECT * FROM pre_trade_log WHERE calc_id=?` appears 6+ times across routes_orders.py — full table scan each time
  Impact: 10-100x slower calc_id lookups as tables grow
  Recommended fix: Add `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_pretrade_calc_id ON pre_trade_log (calc_id)` and same for orders
  Effort: small
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 88.1 — two indexes added inline in core/database.py:initialize() after the ALTER block, alongside existing idx_pretrade_ts / idx_orders_* declarations.
  Implementation note:
    Scope verified against actual schema before patching. The audit's original column inventory (pre_trade_log.calc_id, orders.calc_id) was correct.
    A speculative third index (orders.exchange_order_id) was investigated and dropped — that column is already covered by SQLite's auto-index for `UNIQUE(account_id, exchange_order_id)`. Every production caller prefixes account_id, so the composite auto-index handles them. The third index would have been write-cost duplicate.
    Audit's query-site count for orders.calc_id was inflated (claimed "6+", actual ~3 equality + 2 aggregate sites) but the index is still warranted by calc_correlation.py's hot path.
    Indexes installed via inline DDL in initialize() (not as a new migration file in core/migrations/) because pre_trade_log and orders live in the legacy single-shared DB, which the per-account migration runner does not target. Pattern matches existing idx_pretrade_ts, idx_orders_* declarations.
    EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN verified pre/post:
      Before: `SCAN pre_trade_log` (single-key and IN-clause), `SCAN orders`.
      After:  `SEARCH ... USING INDEX idx_pretrade_calc_id (calc_id=?)` for both single and IN forms; `SEARCH orders USING INDEX idx_orders_calc_id (calc_id=?)`.
  Depended on: CRIT-008 — the pretrade index could not be added until the underlying column existed. Both ALTER and indexes landed in the same commit.

### [MED-005] [Performance] core/trade_event_log.py:99+, core/calc_correlation.py:75+
  Description: Sync sqlite3 calls in modules called from async handlers
  Evidence: `conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)` used directly; trade_events endpoint wraps in `asyncio.to_thread()` (correct) but other callers may not
  Impact: Event loop blocking for 1-5ms per call
  Recommended fix: Ensure all async call paths wrap sync DB calls in `to_thread()`
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5a)
  Sweep evidence: trade_event_log.py:99 uses sync `sqlite3.connect(db_path)` inside a sync `log_trade_event` function. Callers in core/order_manager.py (an async file) invoke `log_trade_event(...)` directly at lines 343, 353, 420, 452, 474, 494, 723 — no `asyncio.to_thread` wrap.
  Sweep note: Inside async coroutines, sync DB I/O blocks the event loop. Per-call cost may be small for INSERTs into a separate trade_events DB, but the pattern is real and the audit is correct. The trade_events READ endpoint wraps in to_thread (correct path); writes from order_manager don't.
  Sweep scope-expansion note (batch 1b add): Audit's recommended fix mentions to_thread() wrapping but did not enumerate primary caller-violation sources. Grep across core/order_manager.py during sweep 1a found ≥7 sync log_trade_event call sites in order_manager.py alone. Fix task should treat the to_thread() wrapping as a wider refactor than the audit's text implies — likely a multi-file pass across order_manager.py, trade_event_log.py, calc_correlation.py.

### [MED-006] [Performance] templates (HTMX polling)
  Description: 5+ HTMX elements poll at `every 30s` concurrently (including hidden tabs); 3 elements at `every 1s`
  Evidence: All tabs fire `hx-trigger="load, every 30s"` regardless of visibility
  Impact: 8+ concurrent requests per 30s cycle; 3 RPS from 1s pollers
  Recommended fix: Use HTMX `intersect` trigger or visibility API to pause hidden-tab polling
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5a)
  Sweep evidence: grep of `every 30s|every 1s|hx-trigger` across templates/: 31 occurrences spread across 10 files. history.html and dashboard.html dominate (7 each). No visibility-aware gating in the grep results.
  Sweep note: Exact counts of "5+ at 30s, 3 at 1s" not individually verified, but the structural claim (no intersect/visibility gating, all-tabs-poll-always pattern) is accurate. Frontend Phase work — defer.

### [MED-007] [Performance] core/data_cache.py:777-789
  Description: OHLCV cache grows without hard memory bound
  Evidence: `cache.append(candle)` — soft trim at `ATR_FETCH_LIMIT + 10` but no max-age eviction or symbol cleanup
  Impact: Memory growth on long-running sessions with many symbols
  Recommended fix: Add per-symbol max-age eviction; clean up disconnected symbols
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: SCOPE-SHIFTED (Task 90.5a)
  Sweep evidence: lines 781-783 confirm the soft trim at ATR_FETCH_LIMIT+10. BUT lines 791-800 contain `evict_symbol_caches(self, active_tickers: set)` which DOES remove cache entries for symbols no longer in any active position (across ohlcv_cache, orderbook_cache, and mark_price_cache):
      def evict_symbol_caches(self, active_tickers: set) -> None:
          """Remove cache entries for symbols no longer in any active position."""
          for sym in list(app_state.ohlcv_cache.keys()):
              if sym not in active_tickers:
                  del app_state.ohlcv_cache[sym]
  Sweep note: Audit said "no max-age eviction OR symbol cleanup". Symbol cleanup exists (evict_symbol_caches). Only the max-age eviction half of the finding is real. Severity may also be lower (MED → LOW?) — long-running sessions with stable symbol set would have bounded per-symbol cache, just with stale data. Scope: focus a fix on max-age eviction within an active symbol's cache; symbol-set growth is already handled.

## 4. Architecture & Coupling

### [HIGH-002] [Architecture] 24 locations across codebase
  Description: Direct access to `db._conn` (private attribute) bypassing DatabaseManager API
  Evidence: `async with db._conn.execute(...)` in api/routes_orders.py (8x), core/monitoring.py (3x), core/platform_bridge.py (8x), core/reconciler.py (1x), plus others
  Impact: Encapsulation violation; code breaks if DatabaseManager refactors connection management
  Recommended fix: Create public helper methods (`db.get_closed_position()`, `db.batch_get_pretrade_logs()`, etc.)
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5b)
  Sweep evidence: grep `db\._conn\.` across production code (api/, core/) shows 6 hits in api/routes_orders.py (was 8 pre-Task-88; the batch refactor removed 2), 8 in core/platform_bridge.py, 3 in core/monitoring.py, 1 in core/reconciler.py — production total ~18 sites (audit said "24 locations" likely including some other modules I didn't enumerate; magnitude matches).
  Sweep note: Encapsulation violation as described. Task 88 already seeded the pattern of moving these to public helpers (get_pretrade_logs_by_calc_ids / get_order_types_by_ids). Phase 6 work to systematize.

### [MED-008] [Architecture] core/event_bus.py vs core/pubsub/bus.py
  Description: Two parallel pub/sub systems without clear usage boundary
  Evidence: `EventBus` (asyncio.Queue-based, internal) coexists with `PubSubBus` (Protocol with InProcess/Redis backends). Some modules use one, some the other.
  Impact: Confusion for contributors; risk of events not reaching all subscribers
  Recommended fix: Document when to use each; long-term consolidate onto PubSubBus
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5b)
  Sweep evidence: both files exist (core/event_bus.py and core/pubsub/bus.py per the file structure). The "two parallel systems" claim is structural — confirmed by file presence and the audit doc's adjacent HIGH-018 / LOW-017 entries that also reference both. Mixing of subscribers across the two buses is the documented concern.
  Sweep note: Architectural finding; not a bug per se. Fix is documentation + long-term consolidation. Phase 6 territory.

### [LOW-002] [Architecture] 40+ function-level imports across core/
  Description: Import-inside-function anti-pattern to work around circular dependencies
  Evidence: `from core.state import DEFAULT_PARAMS` inside function bodies in account_registry, data_cache, order_manager, etc.
  Impact: Hides dependencies; delays ImportError to first invocation
  Recommended fix: Restructure module boundaries to eliminate circulars; acceptable for now
  Effort: large
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5b)
  Sweep evidence: grep `^\s+from core\.` (indented core imports — function-scoped) in core/ returns 113 occurrences across 30 files. Audit's "40+" is conservative; actual count is ~2.8× higher.
  Sweep note: Real anti-pattern, well above audit's stated count. Fix scope is genuinely large; correct as "acceptable for now" until module-boundary refactor (Phase 6).

## 8. Exec Link Logic Bug

### [CRIT-004] [Correctness] core/exec_link.py:55-56
  Description: TP and SL match criteria compare pretrade values against themselves — always True
  Evidence: `tp_match = price_near(pretrade.get("tp_price"), pretrade.get("tp_price"))` — both args are the same dict key. Same for sl_match on line 56.
  Impact: Exec link badge shows 3/3 LINKED for all limit orders regardless of actual TP/SL accuracy. Partial (2/3) can never appear for TP/SL mismatches — only entry price slippage triggers partial.
  Recommended fix: Compare pretrade TP/SL against actual TP/SL orders on the exchange. Short-term: document that TP/SL criteria are plan-vs-plan (always pass) and the real value is entry price comparison. Long-term: query orders table for actual TP/SL placed.
  Effort: medium (to fix properly); small (to document limitation)
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 87 (short-term 1/1 semantics) — full plan-vs-actual deferred to Phase 9 (TP/SL modification tracking)
  Implementation note (Task 87):
    The typo on lines 55-56 (price_near self-comparing pretrade values) was
    NOT fixed in the literal sense. A real plan-vs-actual comparison requires
    threading actual placed TP/SL order data through compute_exec_match's
    callers, which is Phase 9 scope. Short-term fix: dropped TP/SL contributions
    from match_count and auto_link, shifting semantics from "x/3" to "x/1".
    tp_match/sl_match remain on ExecMatchResult as informational stubs (still
    self-comparing) with explicit comments documenting the limitation. Badge
    now reads honestly: 1/1 LINKED when entry matches, 0/1 UNLINKED otherwise.
    Test `test_tp_sl_remain_informational_stubs_post_CRIT_004` pins the stub
    to prevent accidental half-fix without Phase 9 caller threading.
    Templates updated: templates/fragments/history/exec_link_panel.html (badge
    /3 → /1, TP/SL rows marked "(plan-vs-plan stub)" with footer note);
    templates/fragments/history/position_fills.html (badge /3 → /1).

## 9. WebSocket & Adapter Risks

### [CRIT-005] [Correctness] core/ws_manager.py:496
  Description: WS reconnect recurses without stack depth limit on persistent auth failure
  Evidence: `_reconnect_user(attempt + 1)` called recursively. Max-attempt guard at line 477 protects the outer path, but `create_listen_key()` failure at line 496 recurses before hitting that guard.
  Impact: Stack overflow crash if Binance persistently returns 401/403 on listen key refresh
  Recommended fix: Add `if attempt >= max_attempts: return` before the recursive call at line 496
  Effort: small
  Status: VERIFIED FALSE in Task 89 — line-477 max-attempt guard runs at top of every recursive entry; recursion bounded at config.WS_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS=15, well below Python's default 1000-frame limit.
  Verification note (Task 89):
    The audit's "stack overflow" framing was incorrect. Read of core/ws_manager.py:470-504
    shows `if attempt >= config.WS_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS: return` at line 477 fires for every
    recursive call entry. With WS_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS=15 (config.py:63), max stack depth
    from this recursion is 15 frames. The audit's claim that the recursion fires "without
    first checking the max-attempt guard" was wrong — the guard is the second statement
    in the function body (after the _stopping check).
    Regression pin test_reconnect_user_bounded_by_max_attempts_no_recursion_error
    asserts the bound holds; failure-before-fix confirmed by removing the guard, which
    caused unbounded recursion (~947 attempts before Python's recursion-limit RecursionError).
    Related concern recorded as MED-044 (recursion-vs-iteration as a defense-in-depth
    smell if WS_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS is ever bumped) — separate finding, not a CRIT.

### [HIGH-006] [Correctness] core/ws_manager.py:159-195
  Description: Position list iterated while concurrent WS handlers modify it in-place
  Evidence: TP/SL enrichment loops over `app_state.positions` while `DataCache.apply_position_update_incremental()` can add/remove positions concurrently
  Impact: Positions skipped or duplicated during enrichment; risk state miscalculated
  Recommended fix: Copy position list before iteration: `for p in list(app_state.positions):`
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: NEEDS DEEP READ (Task 90.5b) — resolved in Task 91 (see below)
  Sweep evidence: ws_manager.py:159 confirms `for pos in app_state.positions`. The loop body (lines 159-195) has no `await` statements — pure attribute assignment — so within a single coroutine no other coroutine can interleave. Reachability depends on whether `apply_position_update_incremental` ever runs in a *different OS thread* (e.g., via asyncio.to_thread or a thread-pool callback) concurrently with this loop.
  Sweep note: The recommended fix (`list(app_state.positions)`) is a trivially safe defensive copy regardless of whether the bug is triggerable today, so the fix is cheap. But the "is this actually reachable" question requires tracing apply_position_update_incremental's caller chain. Defer to fix-task for that verification; apply the copy regardless.
  Status: VERIFIED FALSE in Task 91 — loop body is await-free; concurrent modification cannot interleave
  Verification note (Task 91):
    Deep-read traced the loop body (core/ws_manager.py:159-195) and confirmed
    zero `await` statements: pure attribute assignments, conditionals, and a
    `break` after the first match. Within asyncio's cooperative single-threaded
    event loop, no other coroutine can run during this loop.
    
    Modifier audit: grep `self\._positions\.|self\._positions =` in
    core/data_cache.py finds 4 sites — all inside async methods on the same
    event loop, three of them additionally gated by `async with self._lock`
    (data_cache.py:266 inside apply_position_snapshot, :366 + :386 inside
    apply_position_update_incremental, :813 init/reset). No `to_thread` or
    `run_in_executor` path mutates `_positions` from a separate OS thread.
    
    Conclusion: the audit's concurrent-modification failure mode is
    structurally unreachable. Combined with the `break` on first match
    (line 195), even if a mutation could interleave, this loop processes at
    most one position before exiting — the audit's "skip/duplicate" framing
    doesn't apply.
    
    Action taken: No production change. Regression pin
    test_apply_order_update_tpsl_loop_body_has_no_awaits in
    tests/test_task91_high006_position_iter_pin.py uses ast parsing to
    assert the loop body remains await-free. If a future contributor adds
    an `await` to the loop body, the pin fails with an explicit message
    pointing back to HIGH-006 and the audit's snapshot fix recommendation.
    Non-vacuous proof: temporarily inserted `await asyncio.sleep(0)` into
    the loop body, pin failed with "loop gained 1 await statement(s)";
    restored.

### [HIGH-007] [Reliability] core/adapters/base.py:109-110
  Description: Weight tracker exceptions caught and swallowed silently
  Evidence: `except Exception: pass` around weight tracking — requests proceed without cost estimation
  Impact: 429 rate-limit bans when budget was believed sufficient; no observability

      except RateLimitError:
          raise
      except Exception:
          pass  # tracker failure must not block requests
  Sweep note: Confirmed verbatim. Comment "tracker failure must not block requests" justifies pass-not-raise; audit's fix is just to add a log.warning before pass. Trivial.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 99 — observability fix matches the audit description directly. log.exception added at both swallow sites in core/adapters/base.py (reserve()'s except at lines 117-128 and _get_weight_tracker()'s lazy-init except at lines 66-75); preserves original "tracker failure must not block requests" control flow. AST regression pins in tests/test_task99_adapter_bundle.py::TestWeightTrackerSwallowLogs cover both branches.
  Framing note (Task 99.1): The Task 98 Top-5 slot-5 rationale that promoted HIGH-007 described it as "race-framed in the audit text" — that paraphrase was inaccurate. Re-reading the audit description above ("exceptions caught and swallowed silently" / "no observability") and the recommended fix ("Log at WARNING level"), the audit was never race-framed for this finding. The fix shape (log + preserve control flow) matches the audit recommendation exactly. No race-claim verification was needed; this finding does NOT count toward the race-framing false-positive calibration.

### [HIGH-008] [Reliability] core/schedulers.py:134-141
  Description: AuthenticationError not caught in background refresh tasks
  Evidence: `_account_refresh_loop` catches `RateLimitError` but not `AuthenticationError`
  Impact: Invalid API keys crash background tasks silently; positions not refreshed
  Recommended fix: Catch `AuthenticationError`; log CRITICAL and disable periodic refresh for affected account
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5b)
  Sweep evidence: grep `RateLimitError|AuthenticationError|except.*Error` in core/schedulers.py shows `from core.adapters.errors import RateLimitError` (line 17) and `except RateLimitError as e:` (lines 219, 516). No AuthenticationError anywhere. Background tasks at lines 125-140 fetch_account / fetch_positions without auth-specific catching.
  Sweep note: Confirmed. If invalid API key → auth error propagates → task crashes → MED-012's "log but don't restart" leaves the account stale forever. Pairs naturally with MED-012 (resurrection) as a bundled fix task.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 103 — narrow AuthenticationError catch + per-account auth_failed_accounts state + credential-update clears the flag.
  Audit-mechanism correction: the sweep note above describes the pre-fix path as "auth error propagates → task crashes." Tracing the actual code, the broad `except Exception` at line 222 ALREADY caught the AuthenticationError — the loop did not crash; it just logged a WARNING and retried on the next 15-30 s tick. The real harm pattern (closer to the audit's intent) is: every tick hammers the exchange with invalid credentials, which (a) generates log noise, (b) risks exchange-side IP-level bans for repeated auth failures, (c) gives the operator no visible signal that "credentials are wrong — fix them." Pattern matches the Task 102 calibration note: bug real, mechanism the audit described was off. Fix addresses the real harm (terminal-until-fix + visible-to-operator) rather than the described one (crashing loop).
  Implementation note:
    - core/state.py:285-291 — new `auth_failed_accounts: Set[int]` field on AppState. Mirrors the `dd_manually_unblocked` set pattern. NOT reset on account-switch (auth_failed is account-specific, not session-specific).
    - core/schedulers.py — imports `AuthenticationError` alongside `RateLimitError` (line 17); skip-guard at top of loop body checks `app_state.active_account_id in app_state.auth_failed_accounts` and `continue`s if true; new `except AuthenticationError as e:` clause between the RateLimitError and the broad Exception handlers logs.critical with account context, adds account_id to the set, and appends a WS log entry "AUTH FAILED for account {aid}: {e} — update credentials in Config." surfacing to the operator on next page load.
    - core/account_registry.py:update_account — when api_key OR api_secret is updated (non-None), the account_id is discarded from app_state.auth_failed_accounts so the scheduler resumes refresh on the next tick. Inside try/except so import failures in test paths don't propagate.
  Design choices:
    - Exception class: core.adapters.errors.AuthenticationError — adapter-neutral type already used at core/adapters/base.py:_run() to translate ccxt.AuthenticationError. Clean. No exchange-specific catching needed.
    - UI surface: minimal — app_state.ws_status.add_log() entry. Visible on the existing WS log fragment without new UI work. A dedicated badge / banner is deferred (out of scope per task spec).
    - Recovery: auth flag clears only on credential update via account_registry.update_account. No automatic retry. Matches audit intent ("disable periodic refresh until manual reconnect").
  Light arch check: scheduler is read-side. `_account_refresh_loop` calls `fetch_account`, `fetch_positions`, and `fetch_user_trades` — all read endpoints. Does not place / modify / cancel orders. Active-side concern does not apply.
  Tests (tests/test_task103_scheduler_auth.py, 10 cases): TestAuthFailedAccountsField (2) — field exists, fresh init empty; TestSchedulerCatchesAuthError (3) — AST pin verifies `except AuthenticationError` precedes `except Exception` in the loop's try chain, skip-guard references auth_failed_accounts, module imports AuthenticationError; TestCredentialUpdateClearsAuthFailedFlag (3) — api_key update clears, api_secret update clears, name-only update does NOT clear (anti-over-correction); TestAuthExceptionHandlerBehavior (2) — AuthenticationError sets flag + CRITICAL log + WS-log entry; ConnectionError (transient) does NOT set the flag.

Snapshot double-counting concern (in-memory state updates twice) is the real residual risk. Bounded blast radius; medium severity correct.

### [MED-012] [Reliability] core/schedulers.py:54-56
  Description: Background task crash logs exception but does not restart task
  Evidence: `_on_done` callback logs but doesn't signal lifespan or attempt restart
  Impact: Crashed scheduler (regime_fetcher, etc.) runs once and never recovers

  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5b)

      def _on_done(t: asyncio.Task) -> None:
          if not t.cancelled() and t.exception() is not None:
              log.error("Background task '%s' crashed: %r", name, t.exception())
  Sweep note: Confirmed verbatim. No restart, no event_bus signal. Pairs naturally with HIGH-008 (auth-error not caught) since the joint failure mode is: auth-error crashes scheduler → schedulers.py:_on_done logs but doesn't restart → account stays unrefreshed indefinitely.

## 12. Order Lifecycle Edge Cases

### [HIGH-009] [Correctness] core/order_manager.py:654-666
  Description: No validation that total_close_qty <= total_open_qty (overfill)
  Evidence: `total_close_qty = sum(f["quantity"] for f in close_fills)` with no cap. Proportional entry fee `entry_fees * (total_close_qty / total_open_qty)` exceeds entry_fees if overfilled.
  Impact: Fee calculation inflated, net_pnl understated for overfilled positions
  Recommended fix: `total_close_qty = min(total_close_qty, total_open_qty)` before fee calc
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5c)
  Sweep evidence: lines 654 and 666 unchanged from the audit:
      total_close_qty = sum(f["quantity"] for f in close_fills)
      ...
      prop_entry  = (
          entry_fees * (total_close_qty / total_open_qty)
          if total_open_qty else 0.0
      )
  Sweep note: Confirmed. If total_close_qty > total_open_qty (overfill scenario), the ratio exceeds 1.0 and proportional entry fee inflates. Trivial cap fix per audit recommendation.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 92 — overfill cap added before fee-proportional calc.
  Implementation note (Task 92):
    Light verification confirmed the audit's claim. `total_close_qty` is used
    downstream at two sites with semantic meaning that depends on the
    uncapped value:
      - core/order_manager.py:656 — denominator for exit_price (VWAP of close fills)
      - core/order_manager.py:699 — persisted `"quantity"` field on closed_positions
        (audit record must reflect actual close size, not capped value)
    The audit's literal recommendation (`total_close_qty = min(...)`) would
    corrupt both. Chose the separate-variable form instead:
      close_qty_for_fees = total_close_qty
      if total_open_qty and total_close_qty > total_open_qty:
          log.warning(...overfill...)
          close_qty_for_fees = total_open_qty
      prop_entry = entry_fees * (close_qty_for_fees / total_open_qty) ...
    Cap fires only on overfill (close > open). Normal close and partial close
    paths produce identical output to pre-fix.
    Tests in tests/test_order_manager.py::TestClosePositionOverfillCap:
      - test_overfill_caps_prop_entry_at_100pct (failure-before-fix verified:
        pre-fix total_fees=7.5 vs post-fix 5.0 with close_qty=1.5, open_qty=1.0,
        entry_fees=5.0)
      - test_underfill_no_cap_no_warning (anti-over-correction)
      - test_exact_fill_no_cap_no_warning (boundary)

### [HIGH-010] [Security] core/crypto.py:54-58
  Description: Decryption silently returns "" on failure (wrong master key, corruption)
  Evidence: `except (InvalidToken, Exception): return ""` — callers get empty credentials. WS auth sends empty API key, silently fails to connect.
  Impact: Engine runs with no exchange connectivity after key rotation; no user-visible error
  Recommended fix: Raise on decryption failure or log CRITICAL + set app_state flag to disable trading
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5c)
  Sweep evidence (crypto.py:48-56):
      def decrypt(ciphertext: str) -> str:
          if not ciphertext: return ""
          try:
              return _fernet().decrypt(ciphertext.encode()).decode()
          except (InvalidToken, Exception) as e:
              log.error("Decryption failed (key mismatch or corruption): %s", type(e).__name__)
              return ""
  Sweep note: The `return ""` silent fallback is present. A `log.error` was added (line 55) that partially mitigates the "no user-visible error" half of the audit's claim — but callers still receive "" and proceed with empty credentials. Recommended fix (raise OR set app_state.disable_trading flag) is still warranted. Duplicate finding: HIGH-016 cites the same lines verbatim. Audit should consolidate.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 105 — decrypt() now raises CredentialDecryptionError on InvalidToken / corrupted ciphertext; all three production call sites updated to catch and route to Task 103's auth_failed_accounts state.
  Implementation note:
    - core/crypto.py: new CredentialDecryptionError class. decrypt() preserves the empty-input fast-path (legitimate "no credential stored") but raises CredentialDecryptionError on the InvalidToken / generic-Exception branch instead of silent-fallback-to-empty. Error log line + exception message both reference ENV_MASTER_KEY so the operator knows where to look.
    - core/account_registry.py:load_all — per-account try/except wraps the two decrypt calls. On failure: log.critical with account_id + name, add account_id to app_state.auth_failed_accounts (Task 103's set; scheduler already skips refresh for accounts in that set), load the account with blank credentials so the rest of the engine doesn't crash on missing-account lookups. One bad credential no longer poisons the whole load.
    - core/connections.py:load_all — per-row try/except. On failure: log.critical with provider, load with blank credential AND is_active=0 so the connections page shows the provider as not-configured (operator sees both the log line and the UI state).
    - core/ws_manager.py — try/except around the decrypt pair in the post-connect-auth path. On failure: log.critical with account_id, add to auth_failed_accounts, append a WS log entry, RETURN from the WS task rather than sending empty-credential auth to the exchange (which would have looped through the reconnect cycle without surfacing the cause).
  Caller-state decision: reused Task 103's `app_state.auth_failed_accounts` rather than introducing a separate decryption-failed set. Rationale: the remediation blocker is operationally the same (no operations possible until the operator intervenes); the log line distinguishes "credential decryption failed" from "exchange rejected API key" so the operator can diagnose the difference. Keeps the state surface small.
  Tests (tests/test_task105_crypto_decryption.py, 12 cases):
    - TestDecryptRaises (6): round-trip succeeds with correct key; empty/None input returns '' (legitimate fast-path preserved); wrong master key raises with ENV_MASTER_KEY in the message; corrupted ciphertext raises; error message guides operator; ERROR log fires with the exception type.
    - TestAccountRegistryLoadAllHandlesDecryptionError (1): end-to-end mock of load_all with a decrypt that raises — confirms blank credentials in cache AND account_id in auth_failed_accounts.
    - TestConnectionsLoadAllHandlesDecryptionError (1): same for the connections path — confirms is_active=0 on the broken connection.
    - TestSourcePins (4): decrypt() source contains "raise CredentialDecryptionError" AND preserves the empty-input fast-path; all three caller modules import CredentialDecryptionError.

### [HIGH-011] [Correctness] core/db_trades.py:57-59
  Description: Filter column names in `_paginated_query()` are f-string interpolated without whitelist
  Evidence: `clauses.append(f"{col} = ?")` where `col` comes from `filters` dict keys. Currently callers pass hardcoded keys ("ticker", "direction") but no enforcement.
  Impact: SQL injection if future code passes user-controlled filter keys. Defense-in-depth gap.
  Recommended fix: Add `_ALLOWED_FILTER_COLS` set and validate `col in allowed` before interpolation
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5c)
  Sweep evidence (db_trades.py:57-60):
      for col, val in filters.items():
          if val:
              clauses.append(f"{col} = ?")
              params.append(val)
  Sweep note: Confirmed verbatim. No whitelist on `col`. Pairs with MED-003 (sort whitelist at route level) and MED-022 (LIKE prefix not parameterized) as the "defense-in-depth SQL-injection-hygiene" bundle.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 101 — class-level _ALLOWED_FILTER_COLS frozenset on TradesMixin + per-iteration validation in _paginated_query.
  Implementation note: Added `_ALLOWED_FILTER_COLS = frozenset({"ticker", "side", "direction"})` at core/db_trades.py:27-34 (covers all current callers — query_pre_trade_log uses ticker+side, query_execution_log uses ticker+side, query_trade_history uses ticker+direction). The filter loop at db_trades.py:67-77 now raises ValueError when a filter key is not in the whitelist, before any f-string interpolation. Existing callers pass only hardcoded keys, so the raise never fires in practice — it's a fail-fast guard against a future caller that might forward user-controlled filter dict keys.
  Tests (tests/test_task101_sql_injection_defense.py::TestPaginatedQueryFilterWhitelist, 4 cases): _ALLOWED_FILTER_COLS constant existence + required-keys check; concrete injection payload ("ticker; DROP TABLE pre_trade_log--") rejected; known-safe filter ("ticker") passes; empty value short-circuits without spurious raise.

### [MED-016] [Correctness] core/risk_engine.py:285
  Description: Position size can be negative when est_slippage >= 1.0 (100%+ slippage)
  Evidence: `est_size = base_size * (1.0 - est_slippage)` — if est_slippage = 1.2, est_size = base_size * -0.2
  Impact: Negative size passed to exchange API — rejected or interpreted as opposite-side order
  Recommended fix: `est_size = max(0.0, base_size * (1.0 - est_slippage))`
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5c)

      est_size = base_size * (1.0 - est_slippage)

(Note: est_slippage from estimate_vwap_fill is computed as `|est_fill_price - average| / average` — ≥1.0 means fill deviated more than 100% from entry, which would indicate catastrophic book conditions. Rare but the audit's defensive fix is still warranted.)

### [MED-018] [Correctness] core/data_cache.py:778
  Description: Negative equity not clamped; can cause downstream issues
  Evidence: `acc.total_equity = acc.balance_usdt + acc.total_unrealized` — if unrealized losses exceed balance, equity is negative. Downstream exposure calc divides by equity.
  Impact: Undefined risk metrics when equity is negative (near-liquidation scenarios)
  Recommended fix: Clamp to `max(0.0, ...)` or trigger liquidation warning
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5c)
  Sweep evidence (data_cache.py:767-768):
      if acc.balance_usdt > 0:
          acc.total_equity = acc.balance_usdt + acc.total_unrealized
  Sweep note: Line drift — audit cited :778, actual :768. Computation only runs when balance > 0 (so equity ≥ unrealized but unrealized can be negative). Audit's claim of negative-equity reachability is real. The `if acc.balance_usdt > 0` guard prevents the path on zero-balance but doesn't prevent negative total_equity.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 102 — clamp negative computed equity (and derived available_margin) to 0 at the apply_mark_price boundary.
  Implementation note: In core/data_cache.py apply_mark_price (current line ~767), raw_equity = balance + unrealized is computed once, checked, and clamped: if raw_equity < 0, log.warning + acc.total_equity = 0.0; else apply raw. available_margin is then computed as max(0.0, total_equity - total_margin_used) — the prior `equity - margin_used` would have produced a negative value whenever margin_used > 0 and equity was clamped. Both clamps are at the same boundary so internal-state invariants stay coherent.
  Downstream-consumer analysis: pre-fix, negative equity produced (a) `pf.drawdown = (max_eq - total_equity) / max_eq` reading > 100 % drawdown display; (b) `pf.total_exposure` skipped via the `if total_equity > 0` guard, leaving stale exposure data; (c) risk_engine.py:351's `total_equity if > 0 else 1.0` fallback gave 1.0 USDT phantom equity for sizing. The audit's "undefined risk metrics" is real but mostly cosmetic (display + sizing fallback) — no division-by-zero crash. Clamping makes the state coherent and explicit (near-liquidation surfaces as equity=0 + warning rather than as silent oddities downstream).
  Tests (tests/test_task102_trust_but_verify.py::TestApplyMarkPriceClampsNegativeEquity, 5 cases): equity clamp branch + warning fires; equity clamp via end-to-end mark_price drop on a real position; available_margin also clamps to 0; positive equity passes through; raw_equity == 0 exactly does NOT trigger warning (boundary condition).

## 15. DB Layer Defense-in-Depth

### [MED-022] [Security] core/db_orders.py:mark_stale_orders_canceled
  Description: LIKE clause prefix built via f-string, not parameterized
  Evidence: `scope_clause = f" AND exchange_order_id NOT LIKE '{exclude_prefix}%'"` — single quotes not escaped
  Impact: SQL injection if prefix params are ever user-controlled. Currently called with hardcoded prefixes only.
  Recommended fix: Use `?` placeholder: `scope_clause = " AND exchange_order_id NOT LIKE ?"` with `params.append(f"{prefix}%")`
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5c)
  Sweep evidence (db_orders.py:362,364):
      scope_clause = f" AND exchange_order_id NOT LIKE '{exclude_prefix}%'"
      ...
      scope_clause = f" AND exchange_order_id LIKE '{only_prefix}%'"
  Sweep note: Confirmed at both lines. Both f-string interpolated with single-quote literal wrapping. Bundles with MED-003 and HIGH-011 as the SQL-injection-hygiene defense-in-depth task.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 101 — both LIKE-clause branches use `?` placeholder; prefix value passed in params list.
  Implementation note: scope_clause now sets " AND exchange_order_id NOT LIKE ?" (or " LIKE ?") and appends `f"{prefix}%"` to a `scope_params` list, which is concatenated into both UPDATE-execute params tuples. SQL string no longer contains the raw prefix value. LIKE wildcard escaping decision: % and _ in the prefix are LEFT UNESCAPED (wildcards remain LIKE-feature, not literal-match). Today's callers pass only "algo:" (no wildcards); any future caller wanting literal-match semantics for user-supplied prefixes must escape themselves — documented in the function's body comment.
  Tests (tests/test_task101_sql_injection_defense.py::TestStaleOrdersLikeParameterized, 4 cases): source-pin asserts `LIKE '{` (the vulnerable shape) is not in the function source and that `LIKE ?` IS in it; capture-execute-args test fires `mark_stale_orders_canceled` with prefix `"' OR 1=1 --"` and confirms the raw payload appears in params but NOT in the executed SQL string (for both exclude_prefix and only_prefix branches); functionality-preservation test confirms "algo:" prefix still passes through unchanged.

## 17. Account Management

### [HIGH-012] [Correctness] api/routes_accounts.py + core/order_manager.py:607
  Description: Account switch while a deferred close-row build is pending can write old account's position to new account
  Evidence: `loop.call_later(2.0, lambda f=fill: asyncio.ensure_future(self._build_close_row_for_fill(account_id, f)))` — the lambda captures `account_id` from the outer scope which changes on account switch. If switch happens during the 2s delay, the close row may use the old account's data in a mixed context.
  Impact: Cross-account contamination of closed_position records during rapid account switches
  Recommended fix: Capture `account_id` as a value in the lambda, not a closure reference; verify it matches active account before insert
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: NEEDS DEEP READ (Task 90.5c)
  Sweep evidence (order_manager.py:594-599):
      if fill.get("is_close"):
          loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
          loop.call_later(
              2.0,
              lambda f=fill: asyncio.ensure_future(
                  self._build_close_row_for_fill(account_id, f)
  Sweep note: Subtler than the audit suggests. `account_id` is a function parameter of process_fill — Python function parameters are bound once at call entry and don't mutate from external account-switches. The lambda's closure captures the parameter's binding, which is stable. So the "outer scope changes on account switch" race-description is questionable as written. BUT the recommended fix (explicit value capture + active-account verification before insert) is still defensible defense-in-depth, especially the "verify it matches active account before insert" half. Defer to fix-task for a careful trace of: (1) is account_id ever rebound inside process_fill? (2) is the audit's race actually triggerable via some path I haven't traced? (3) should the verify-active-before-insert half be split into a separate finding?
  Status: VERIFIED FALSE in Task 96 — lambda captures function parameter; race structurally impossible.
  Verification note (Task 96):
    Deep-read of OrderManager.process_fill at core/order_manager.py:565-601:
        async def process_fill(self, account_id: int, fill: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
            ...
            if fill.get("is_close"):
                loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
                loop.call_later(
                    2.0,
                    lambda f=fill: asyncio.ensure_future(
                        self._build_close_row_for_fill(account_id, f)
                    ),
                )

    Capture analysis:
      - `self` and `account_id` are free vars; `f=fill` is explicit-value-captured already (correct).
      - `account_id` is the function parameter of process_fill. Each process_fill
        call has its own frame with its own account_id local variable.
      - Grep of process_fill body confirmed no reassignment of account_id (uses
        only: line 569 fill.setdefault, 576 _snapshot_and_fix_isclose, 587
        get_position_fees, 599 lambda body).
      - The lambda outlives process_fill (call_later 2.0s), but the captured
        cell stays pinned to the value passed at call time. Python frame-locals
        are not reachable from outside the function — no `app_state.something =
        ...` mutation can affect this binding.

    The audit's "outer scope changes on account switch" mechanism is incorrect:
    process_fill's local scope is private and immutable from outside.
    Each call has its own account_id; concurrent process_fill calls with different
    account_ids each have their own correctly-pinned closures.

    The "verify-active-account-before-insert" half of the audit's recommended fix
    was defensible only under the (incorrect) assumption that the captured value
    could go stale. Since it can't, the verify is redundant — the deferred close
    inserts to the correct account by construction.

    Action: no production change. Regression pin in
    tests/test_task96_high012_closure_pin.py uses AST inspection to assert the
    lambda references account_id as a bare ast.Name (function parameter), not
    an ast.Attribute (which would be self.account_id, app_state.active_account_id,
    or similar mutable path). Non-vacuous proof: temporarily replaced the
    lambda body's `account_id` with `self.account_id`; pin failed with explicit
    "HIGH-012 closure pattern changed... captured value can now be mutated by
    external code... apply audit's recommended fix" message. Restored.

    This is the 8th VERIFIED FALSE in the race/concurrency-framed cluster
    (CRIT-003, CRIT-005, HIGH-004, HIGH-005, HIGH-006, HIGH-019, HIGH-025,
    now HIGH-012). The audit consistently assumed Python runtime semantics
    permit concurrent mutation where in fact local-variable / GIL / cooperative-
    asyncio semantics provide structural protection. Future audit batches
    targeting "race" findings should default to verify-before-assume.

## 19. Exchange Adapter Robustness

### [HIGH-013] [Reliability] core/adapters/binance/rest_adapter.py:72-79
  Description: Missing account fields from Binance API response silently default to 0
  Evidence: `total_equity=float(info.get("totalWalletBalance", 0) or 0)` — if Binance omits field or returns null, equity becomes 0.0
  Impact: Account equity reported as 0 → risk engine allows excessive position sizing (thinks no capital at risk)
  Recommended fix: Validate critical fields (equity, balance) are present and > 0; raise if missing
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5c)
  Sweep evidence (binance/rest_adapter.py:72-79):
      total_equity=float(info.get("totalWalletBalance", 0) or 0),
      available_margin=float(info.get("availableBalance", 0) or 0),
      unrealized_pnl=float(info.get("totalUnrealizedProfit", 0) or 0),
      ...
  Sweep note: Confirmed verbatim across 8 fields (lines 72-79). Each defaults missing/null to 0 silently. Audit's risk-engine-allows-excessive-sizing impact is real — pairs with MED-018 (negative-equity clamp) for a "trust-but-verify exchange responses" hardening bundle.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 102 — Shape A (raise on missing critical fields) for the two equity-gating fields; Shape B (default + log) for the three non-critical numeric fields.
  Implementation note: After info/comm unpack in core/adapters/binance/rest_adapter.py:fetch_account (current line ~112), the two critical fields (`totalWalletBalance` → total_equity, `availableBalance` → available_margin) are validated via explicit `info.get(...) is None` checks. Either being missing or null raises ValueError with a "phantom-zero" message identifying the offending field. The three non-critical numeric fields (totalUnrealizedProfit / totalInitialMargin / totalMaintMargin) keep their `info.get(..., 0) or 0` default but a log.warning fires when each is absent. Behavior trace: schedulers._account_refresh_loop's existing `except Exception` catches the ValueError and logs "Periodic account refresh failed" — internal state (`acc.total_equity`, `acc.balance_usdt`) keeps its prior known-good value rather than being overwritten by a phantom zero.
  Downstream-consumer analysis: pre-fix, equity=0 propagated to (a) risk_engine.py:351's `total_equity if > 0 else 1.0` guard — sizing reverted to a phantom 1.0 USDT equity (micro-positions, not "excessive" as the audit phrased it); (b) data_cache._recalculate_portfolio's exposure / drawdown calcs (all guarded by `if > 0` — silently zeroed); (c) BOD/SOW equity init (line 670-676) — would silently freeze at 0 forever. The audit's "excessive sizing" framing is wrong-direction (sizing actually collapses to micro), but the silent corruption of prior state is real. The fix preserves prior state by failing the refresh cycle cleanly.
  Tests (tests/test_task102_trust_but_verify.py::TestBinanceFetchAccountCriticalFieldValidation, 6 cases): missing totalWalletBalance raises; missing availableBalance raises; null value raises (not just absent key); complete response applies normally; missing non-critical field logs+defaults (no raise); zero value valid (not treated as missing).

### [HIGH-014] [Reliability] core/adapters/base.py:88-110
  Description: Weight tracker never reconciles estimate with exchange response headers
  Evidence: Tracker estimates request cost upfront but never reads `X-MBX-USED-WEIGHT-1M` (Binance) or equivalent response headers to update actual usage
  Impact: Tracker estimate diverges from server truth over time; may under/over-throttle
  Recommended fix: Read response headers post-call and reconcile tracker state
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5c)
  Sweep evidence: grep `X-MBX-USED-WEIGHT|response\.headers|reconcile|update_actual` across core/adapters/ returns 0 hits. Tracker has no response-header reconciliation path.
  Sweep note: Confirmed by absence. Reconciling against exchange-reported usage requires intercepting CCXT's HTTP response — moderate effort. Pairs with HIGH-007 (weight tracker exceptions swallowed) for a "tracker observability + accuracy" bundle.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 99 (Binance only) — reconciliation fix matches the audit description directly. `_reconcile_from_response()` hook added on BaseExchangeAdapter (no-op default) and called from `_run()` after each successful executor return, wrapped in try/except so reconciliation failure cannot corrupt the call result. BinanceUSDMAdapter overrides the hook to parse `X-MBX-USED-WEIGHT-1M` (case-insensitive) from `self._ex.last_response_headers` and call the pre-existing tracker.reconcile(). Bounds-check rejects negative or implausibly-large values so a malformed header cannot poison the budget. Bybit override deferred — tracked separately as HIGH-028 (filed Task 99.1). weight_tracker.py module docstring updated to reflect partial v2.4 coverage. Tests: tests/test_task99_adapter_bundle.py::TestBinanceHeaderReconciliation pins the wiring (4 cases: header parsed, lowercase variant, missing header leaves estimate intact, garbage header ignored).
  Framing note (Task 99.1): The audit's description ("never reconciles estimate with exchange response headers") and Impact ("Tracker estimate diverges from server truth over time") explicitly describe drift, not concurrent state corruption. The fix is a reconciliation wire-up, not a concurrency primitive — which matches the audit recommendation exactly. No race-claim verification was needed; this finding does NOT count toward the race-framing false-positive calibration.
  Cross-reference: HIGH-028 (Bybit parallel — deferred from this task because the response-header schema differs).

## 20. Analytics & Regime

### [MED-027] [Correctness] core/analytics.py:209-212
  Description: `profit_factor` returns 999.0 sentinel when all trades are winners (no losses)
  Evidence: `else 999.0` — consumers can't distinguish "infinite profit factor" from "very high profit factor"
  Impact: Misleading analytics display; consumers may treat 999.0 as actual computed ratio
  Recommended fix: Return `None` or `float('inf')`; let UI render "N/A" or "∞"
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5c)
  Sweep evidence (analytics.py:209-213):
      profit_factor = (
          abs(sum(pos) / sum(neg))
          if neg and sum(neg) != 0
          else 999.0
      )
  Sweep note: Confirmed verbatim. The 999.0 sentinel is real magic-number-as-stand-in-for-inf.

  Impact: Event loop blocked 1-2s during VIX fetch; stalls order processing and WS message handling
  Recommended fix: Wrap in `asyncio.to_thread()` or replace with async httpx call to Yahoo Finance
  Effort: medium

### [MED-029] [Correctness] core/regime_classifier.py
  Description: No hysteresis band on regime transitions; signal oscillation near thresholds causes rapid flipping
  Evidence: If VIX oscillates around a threshold (e.g., 20±0.5), regime flips on every check cycle
  Impact: Regime-dependent position sizing multipliers jitter; trade signals unstable
  Recommended fix: Add hysteresis bands (e.g., enter at 22, exit at 18) or cooldown timer
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5c)
  Sweep evidence: grep `hysteresis|cooldown|prev_regime|previous_state` in core/regime_classifier.py returns 0 hits. No hysteresis/cooldown pattern present.
  Sweep note: Confirmed by absence. Real correctness concern; oscillating regimes would jitter sizing multipliers as audit describes.

### [MED-030] [Correctness] core/regime_fetcher.py (~fetch_fred_series)
  Description: FRED API response structure not validated before key access
  Evidence: Code assumes `"observations"` key exists; error response (e.g., invalid series ID) causes KeyError crash
  Impact: Regime fetcher crashes on FRED API error; regime stale until next successful fetch
  Recommended fix: Validate response structure: `if "observations" not in data: log.warning(...); return`
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: SCOPE-SHIFTED (Task 90.5c)
  Sweep evidence (regime_fetcher.py:153):
      observations = data.get("observations", [])
  Sweep note: Audit's KeyError failure mode is WRONG — the code uses `.get("observations", [])` with default empty list, which cannot KeyError. BUT the secondary impact (regime stale on API error) is real: if FRED returns an error response without "observations" key, `observations = []` silently → no rows recorded → regime stays at last-known value. The recommended fix (`log.warning + return`) is still defensible to surface the silent failure. Re-spec needed: severity may be lower than MED since failure is silent-degraded not crash; fix is observability, not correctness.

## 22. Drawdown Enforcement

### [HIGH-017] [Correctness] core/dd_gate.py:46-47
  Description: DD gate fails OPEN on database read error — allows trading when enforcement mode can't be determined
  Evidence: `except Exception: return True, None` — if `get_account_settings()` raises (DB corruption, connection lost), the gate allows order placement even for accounts configured as "enforced"
  Impact: Enforced drawdown limits bypassed during DB outages; trader can violate limits unknowingly
  Recommended fix: Fail closed: `return False, "unable_to_read_enforcement_settings"`. Or cache last-known enforcement mode.
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence (dd_gate.py:46-47):
      except Exception:
          return True, None  # can't read settings → don't block
  Sweep note: Confirmed verbatim including the "don't block" comment which captures the fail-open semantic. Real reliability concern for trading installations under DB stress.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 98 — fail-closed default on settings-read failure.
  Implementation note (Task 98):
    Calling convention: dd_gate_allows_new_entry returns (True, None) = ALLOW
    entry, (False, reason) = BLOCK. The audit's "fail-open" was `return True,
    None` on the except path; the fix inverts to `return False,
    "dd_gate_settings_unreadable"`. Trading-block gates default to BLOCK on
    unknown state — the alternative (allow trading because we can't determine
    enforcement mode) silently bypasses configured DD limits during DB stress,
    the exact scenario the limit is meant to protect against. Added log.error
    on the except path describing the fail-closed action so operators can
    correlate against DB-outage logs.
    3 tests in TestDDGateFailClosed: load-bearing pin (settings-read raise →
    blocked + logged), normal-path sanity (dd_state="ok" → allowed),
    advisory-mode sanity (dd_state="limit" + mode="advisory" → allowed).
    Anti-over-correction confirmed: normal paths pre/post-fix unchanged.

## 23. PubSub Internals

### [HIGH-018] [Reliability] core/pubsub/in_process_bus.py:46
  Description: Any exception during publish (not just QueueFull) permanently disconnects the subscriber queue
  Evidence: `except Exception: dead.append(q)` followed by `queues.discard(q)` — a transient error (e.g., payload serialization failure) permanently removes the subscriber
  Impact: UI SSE streams or internal event consumers silently stop receiving updates; no recovery without restart
  Recommended fix: Only disconnect on QueueFull; log other exceptions without removing the queue
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence (in_process_bus.py:41-49):
      except asyncio.QueueFull:
          log.debug("InProcessBus: dropped message ...")
      except Exception:
          dead.append(q)
      for q in dead:
          queues.discard(q)
  Sweep note: Confirmed verbatim. QueueFull is logged + kept; any other Exception adds to dead list and the discard at line 49 permanently removes. Audit's fix recommendation (narrow the except to specific exception types, log unknowns without disconnecting) is the right shape.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 98 — broad-except disconnect replaced with log.exception + keep-subscriber.
  Implementation note (Task 98):
    In InProcessBus.publish (core/pubsub/in_process_bus.py:35-51), the broad
    `except Exception: dead.append(q)` path is replaced with `log.exception(
    "InProcessBus: unexpected error delivering to subscriber on %s; keeping
    subscription", channel)`. The post-loop `dead` discard machinery is
    removed entirely.
    
    Disconnect-on-error analysis: the broad-except disconnect was NOT the
    only cleanup path. subscribe()'s `finally` block at lines 62-68 already
    handles normal subscriber cleanup when the iterator generator ends
    (GeneratorExit / CancelledError, or the consumer simply stops iterating).
    The removed path was a "subscriber misbehaving, kick them" mechanism —
    appropriate response to a corrupt subscriber but inappropriate response
    to a transient payload-serialization error or a recoverable bug. No
    resource leak risk: queues that never get cleaned up are still bounded
    by maxsize and will get GC'd when the subscriber generator is collected.
    
    QueueFull path unchanged (already correctly handled — log + drop event,
    keep subscriber).
    
    3 tests in TestPubsubKeepsSubscriber: load-bearing pin (faulty queue
    raises RuntimeError → subscriber kept + log.exception), QueueFull
    pin (sanity), normal-delivery anti-over-correction.

## 24. Order Lifecycle Gaps

### [HIGH-019] [Correctness] core/order_manager.py (TP/SL placement)
  Description: Position added to state before TP/SL orders are placed; if TP/SL placement fails, position has no protection
  Evidence: Entry fill → position created in DataCache → then TP/SL placed in separate API calls. If TP/SL call fails (rate limit, invalid price, network), position sits unprotected.
  Impact: Trader's position exposed to unlimited loss without stop-loss during the failure window
  Recommended fix: Queue TP/SL retry with exponential backoff; mark position as "awaiting protection" in UI until TP/SL confirmed
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence: grep `place_tp_sl|tp_sl_order|awaiting_protection|TP/SL.*queue|place_tpsl` in core/order_manager.py returns 0 hits. No retry queue, no "awaiting protection" state machine. Position-update flow does not gate on TP/SL placement success.
  Sweep note: Confirmed by absence. Currently slot 3 in Top 5; remains there until fix. Pairs with HIGH-026 (silent-swallow in close path) as the "position-protection state machine" cluster — both about making the post-fill lifecycle fail-loud and recoverable.
  Status: VERIFIED FALSE in Task 93 — architectural mismatch (engine is passive observer, not order placer)
  Verification note (Task 93):
    Grep of core/ for `place_tp_sl|place_tpsl|create_tpsl|create_order|place_order` found
    zero matches. `individual_tpsl=True` is set only inside _apply_order_update
    (core/ws_manager.py:166, 174, 261, 265) reacting to externally-observed
    ORDER_TRADE_UPDATE WS events. The engine is fundamentally a passive observer;
    the Quantower plugin (or user-side workflow) places orders directly to the
    exchange, and the engine reads the resulting events.
    
    The audit's "retry queue + awaiting-protection flag" recommendation assumed
    engine-side placement. That assumption does not match this codebase. No
    place_tp_sl function exists to wrap with a retry queue.
    
    The sweep note's pairing claim with HIGH-026 ("position-protection state
    machine cluster") was also based on the same incorrect assumption. HIGH-026
    is about silent-swallow in close-row build, which IS engine-side work;
    HIGH-019 is about TP/SL placement, which is NOT engine-side. The pairing
    doesn't actually hold.
    
    Action taken: No production change. Regression pin
    test_engine_does_not_place_tp_sl_orders in
    tests/test_task93_tpsl_protection.py walks core/*.py and asserts no
    place_tp_sl-style function exists. If a future contributor adds engine-side
    TP/SL placement, the pin fails with explicit guidance to re-evaluate.
    Non-vacuous proof: temporarily appended `async def place_tp_sl(*a, **k):
    pass` to core/log_formatter.py; pin failed with the architectural-invariant
    violation message; restored.
    
    Related residual concern (calc-to-fill matching window in core/exec_link.py
    — stale pretrades can retroactively match fresh fills) filed separately
    as HIGH-027.

### [HIGH-020] [Correctness] core/exchange.py:144
  Description: `fetch_account()` accesses `app_state._data_cache` without null check (inconsistent with `fetch_positions()` which guards at line 177)
  Evidence: `await app_state._data_cache.apply_account_update_rest(na, ts_ms)` — if DataCache not yet initialized (startup race), crashes with AttributeError
  Impact: Startup crash if account fetch fires before DataCache init; silent background scheduler failure
  Recommended fix: Add `if app_state._data_cache is None: log.warning(...); return` (matching fetch_positions pattern)

      exchange.py:177-179 (fetch_positions):  if app_state._data_cache is None: log.warning(...); return

### [HIGH-021] [Reliability] core/contract_validation.py:38-53
  Description: Contract specs cached for 24h; exchange spec updates (min_qty, lot_step) cause false rejects until cache expires
  Evidence: Specs stored with 24h TTL. If exchange changes min_qty mid-day, cached old value rejects valid orders for up to 24h. No admin endpoint to force refresh.
  Impact: Valid orders silently rejected; no visibility into cache staleness
  Recommended fix: Reduce TTL to 1-4h; add admin endpoint to force refresh; log cache age in rejection reason
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence (contract_validation.py:20,46):
      _CACHE_TTL_HOURS = 24
      ...
      if cached and (now - cached.fetched_at).total_seconds() < _CACHE_TTL_HOURS * 3600:
          return cached
  Sweep note: 24h TTL confirmed. No force_refresh admin endpoint anywhere in the file. Pairs naturally with MED-017 (mark price freshness) and MED-032 (TP/SL cache age) as the "cache-freshness hardening" bundle.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 94 — TTL reduced (24h → 4h) + force-refresh admin endpoint added.
  Implementation note (Task 94):
    - Replaced `_CACHE_TTL_HOURS = 24` with `CONTRACT_SPEC_TTL_SECONDS = int(os.getenv("CONTRACT_SPEC_TTL_SECONDS", "14400"))` at core/contract_validation.py:20-25. Default 4h; operator-tunable via env.
    - Added `force_refresh_specs() -> int` at core/contract_validation.py:60-69. Clears the in-memory cache and returns the count of cleared entries.
    - Added admin endpoint POST /admin/contract-specs/refresh at api/routes_admin.py:388-401. No auth — matches existing admin endpoints (auth is HIGH-001's scope).
    - 4 regression tests pin: TTL ≤ 8h ceiling, 4h default when env unset, force_refresh_specs clears cache + returns count, admin endpoint registered.
  TTL rationale: 4h chosen as balance between freshness (crypto-futures listings/spec-changes happen within a day) and rate-limit cost (refetching specs for active symbols isn't free). Operator can lower for fast-moving symbol sets or raise for stable ones via env. 8h ceiling enforces the "audit's 24h was too long" intent — any future bump past 8h needs to come with a test update + a documented reason.

## 25. Analytics & Fetcher Edge Cases

### [MED-031] [Correctness] core/calc_correlation.py:232-239
  Description: Drift percentage divides by near-zero TP/SL prices, producing huge drift values that filter out legitimate matches
  Evidence: `t_drift = abs(tp_price - tp) / tp if tp and tp_price else 1.0` — if `tp = 1e-6` (micro-cap token), drift = 1000+ even for small absolute differences
  Impact: Legitimate order-to-calc correlations missed for low-priced tokens
  Recommended fix: Use absolute difference threshold as fallback when prices < 0.01: `if tp < 0.01: t_drift = abs(tp_price - tp)`
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence (calc_correlation.py:233-235):
      e_drift = abs(entry_price - eff) / eff if eff else 1.0
      t_drift = abs(tp_price - tp) / tp if tp and tp_price else 1.0
      s_drift = abs(sl_price - sl) / sl if sl and sl_price else 1.0
  Sweep note: Confirmed verbatim. Same epsilon-vulnerability pattern flagged in MED-002 (R:R overflow) and MED-007's note — divisions guarded against 0 but not against near-zero. Family of "epsilon hardening" findings.

### [HIGH-024] [Security] api/routes_connections.py:55
  Description: API keys may appear in tracebacks if exception occurs during connections upsert
  Evidence: No `Sensitive` wrapper on key values; full key in exception traceback if DB write fails
  Impact: Credential exposure in error logs
  Recommended fix: Wrap keys in object that masks `__repr__`; sanitize tracebacks
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence (routes_connections.py:128-131):
      api_key: str = Form(...),
      ...
      await connections_manager.upsert(provider, label, api_key)
  Sweep note: Line drift — audit cited :55 (which is HTMX attribute string), actual handler at :128. `api_key: str` parameter receives raw key from form; passed unwrapped to upsert. If upsert raises during DB write, FastAPI's traceback includes function arguments. Real credential-leak vector. Trivial fix: wrap in a SensitiveStr class with `__repr__` returning "***".
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 100 — SensitiveStr wrapper + entry-point wrapping (narrow scope: HIGH-024's literal site only).
  Implementation note:
    - New SensitiveStr class in core/security.py — str subclass; __repr__ / __str__ / __format__ mask the value, while .encode() goes through a C-level slot that preserves real bytes (verified experimentally: hmac.new(SensitiveStr(k).encode(), ...) matches hmac.new(k.encode(), ...) exactly).
    - api/routes_connections.py:128 wraps the form-supplied api_key into SensitiveStr before calling connections_manager.upsert. Any exception in encrypt() or db.upsert_connection() now shows <SensitiveStr len=N masked> in traceback frame locals rather than the raw key.
    - core/connections.py ConnectionsManager.upsert unwraps before storing in the in-memory cache. Reason: the cache value is read by _test_provider and passed into httpx URL params / headers, and urlencode/httpx URL builders serialize values via str() — which is the masked override. Leaving SensitiveStr in the cache would break authentication on the live HTTP test path.
  Tests (tests/test_task100_sensitive_str.py, 17 cases):
    - TestSensitiveStr (10): repr/str/format mask; encode bypasses mask; HMAC signing unchanged; traceback message masked; failure-before-fix contrast with plain str; unwrap returns raw plain str; startswith/endswith/in/slicing/len transparent.
    - TestRoutesConnectionsWiring (2): source-pin asserts upsert_connection wraps api_key; module imports SensitiveStr.
    - TestConnectionsManagerUnwraps (3): cache stores plain str (not SensitiveStr); plain str input still works (anti-over-correction); encrypt() sees real bytes via SensitiveStr.
    - TestTracebackLocalsMasked (1): simulated encrypt-raises scenario — repr of api_key local in the raising frame is masked.
    - TestUrlencodeBreakageDocumented (1): documents urlencode would emit "<masked>" if SensitiveStr leaked past the unwrap boundary, explaining the unwrap's necessity.
  Wiring pin (Task 100): inspect-based pin on upsert_connection asserts source contains "SensitiveStr"; second pin asserts module imports SensitiveStr from core.security. Catches regression where future refactor drops the wrapping.
  Scope note: Audit cited only api/routes_connections.py. Parallel exchange-credential leak vectors exist in api/routes_accounts.py (5 entry points: create_account, update_account, add_account_modal, test_account_preview, update_account_detail) and in core/account_registry.py post-decryption — these are NOT covered by this fix. urlencode/httpx breakage (verified: `urlencode({"k": SensitiveStr("v")})` produces `k=%3Cmasked%3E`) means a broader wrap would require .unwrap() at every httpx URL/header serialization site (ccxt instantiator, _test_provider, etc.). Flagging as latent HIGH-029 for a follow-up task that takes the manager-chokepoint approach (wrap at core/crypto.decrypt + every form route + explicit .unwrap() at HTTP serialization sites).

### [HIGH-025] [Correctness] core/order_enrichment.py:47-89
  Description: Partial fills don't trigger parent order re-enrichment — TP/SL trigger prices stale after first partial
  Evidence: `enrich_fill()` propagates calc_id and classifies fill_type but does NOT re-run `_populate_tp_sl_trigger_prices()` on the parent order
  Impact: Subsequent partial fills have stale enrichment data; slippage measurement incorrect for multi-fill entries
  Recommended fix: Re-enrich parent order on each partial fill when TP/SL may have shifted
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence (order_enrichment.py:35-41 + 47-89):
      def enrich_fill(fill: Dict[str, Any], db_path: str) -> None:
          """Propagate calc_id from parent order to fill, classify fill_type, compute slippage."""
          try:
              _propagate_calc_id_to_fill(fill, db_path)
              _classify_and_compute_slippage(fill, db_path)
          except Exception:
              log.warning("fill enrichment failed for %s", ...)
  Sweep note: `enrich_fill` (line 35) does not call `_populate_tp_sl_trigger_prices` (line 47). The function only updates the fill itself, not the parent order's TP/SL trigger snapshot. Currently slot 5 in Top 5. Real correctness gap for multi-fill positions.
  Status: VERIFIED FALSE in Task 94 — literal claim true, but stale-snapshot impact does not materialize.
  Verification note (Task 94):
    The audit's literal observation is correct: enrich_fill does not call
    _populate_tp_sl_trigger_prices. But the IMPACT chain "stale enrichment
    data → slippage measurement incorrect" doesn't hold because the parent's
    trigger snapshot is refreshed via a different event and slippage reads
    fresh from DB anyway.
    
    Mechanism 1 — parent re-enrichment on child arrival:
      core/order_manager.py:204 `_re_enrich_parent_on_child_arrival(account_id, order)`
      runs whenever a child TP/SL order is persisted. It calls enrich_order
      on the parent, which re-runs _populate_tp_sl_trigger_prices. So if a
      user moves their TP/SL mid-trade, the parent's snapshot updates on the
      child-order event, not the fill event. Fills don't need to trigger
      this refresh because fills don't change TP/SL state — child order
      modifications do.
    
    Mechanism 2 — fresh DB read on every fill's slippage computation:
      core/order_enrichment.py:289-299 in _classify_and_compute_slippage
      does `SELECT * FROM orders WHERE account_id=? AND exchange_order_id=?`
      on every fill, then uses that parent row for slippage. There's no
      in-memory cached snapshot that could go stale between fills.
    
    Combined: multi-fill entries get correct per-fill slippage. The audit's
    "stale enrichment data" framing assumed an in-memory cache that doesn't
    exist; the implementation reads fresh per-fill.
    
    Action: no production change. Two regression pins:
      - test_re_enrich_parent_on_child_arrival_exists: inspects
        OrderManager._re_enrich_parent_on_child_arrival source, asserts it
        calls enrich_order. Pins mechanism 1 against future removal.
      - test_classify_and_compute_slippage_reads_parent_fresh_from_db:
        regex-scans _classify_and_compute_slippage source for
        `SELECT * FROM orders WHERE ... exchange_order_id` query. Pins
        mechanism 2 against future replacement by a stale cache lookup.
    Both pins use inspect.getsource + string/regex check, same pattern as
    Task 93's HIGH-019 architectural pin (already proven non-vacuous).

## 27. Order State Machine

### [MED-034] [Correctness] core/order_state.py:21-41
  Description: Transitions from terminal states (FILLED/CANCELED) silently dropped — rare exchange corrections ignored
  Evidence: `OrderStatus.FILLED: set()` — no outbound transitions. If exchange sends FILLED→CANCELED correction, update is silently skipped.
  Impact: Order state diverges from exchange; stale filled orders remain in local state
  Recommended fix: Allow FILLED→CANCELED transition; log as WARNING + emit engine_event
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence (order_state.py:26-29):
      OrderStatus.FILLED:           set(),
      OrderStatus.CANCELED:         set(),
      OrderStatus.EXPIRED:          set(),
      OrderStatus.REJECTED:         set(),
  Sweep note: Confirmed verbatim. Terminal states cannot transition. Audit's recommended fix (allow FILLED→CANCELED with WARNING + engine_event) handles the exchange-correction edge case. Pairs with MED-035 (no engine_event on dropped transition) as the "order-state-machine fail-loud" bundle.

### [MED-035] [Correctness] core/order_manager.py:58-63
  Description: Invalid state transitions logged as WARNING but produce no audit trail (no engine_event)
  Evidence: `log.warning("Invalid transition..."); continue` — order skipped without event_log entry
  Impact: No forensic trail for dropped order updates; DD gate counters may be inaccurate
  Recommended fix: Emit `engine_event("order_transition_dropped", ...)` before continuing
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence (order_manager.py:58-63):
      if not validate_transition(current_status, new_status):
          log.warning(
              "Invalid transition %s->%s for order %s, skipping",
              current_status, new_status, eid,
          )
          continue
  Sweep note: Confirmed verbatim. Trivial 2-LOC fix to emit engine_event before continue. Bundles with MED-034 (terminal-state corrections) for "order-state-machine fail-loud".

### [MED-036] [Correctness] api/routes_params.py:35
  Description: Parameter updates applied to live state without checking impact on open positions
  Evidence: `app_state.params.update(new_params)` — if `max_position_count` drops below current open count, risk engine throws on next cycle
  Impact: Mid-trade parameter change can crash risk engine
  Recommended fix: Validate `new_max >= current_open_count`; require confirmation for breaking changes
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence (routes_params.py:63-68):
      errors = validate_params(new_params)
      if errors:
          return HTMLResponse(...)
      app_state.params.update(new_params)
  Sweep note: validate_params runs at line 63 but doesn't check against runtime state (len(app_state.positions) etc.). app_state.params.update at :68 commits the new max_position_count regardless. Confirms audit's concern. Trivial fix: add cross-check before update.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 97 — runtime-state check added inline in update_params route.
  Implementation note (Task 97):
    Added inline check at api/routes_params.py:64-72: if max_position_count <
    len(app_state.positions), append a Validation error message and return without
    applying the update. Inline (rather than in validate_params) because the check
    requires app_state access; keeping validate_params pure avoids the
    import-circularity from core.state → app_state.
    3 unit tests in TestMaxPositionCountRuntimeCheck cover: validate_params alone
    doesn't catch runtime state (confirms separation), rejection path (3 open,
    new max=2), acceptance path (2 open, new max=5).
  Wiring pin (Task 97.1): AST-based pin asserts update_params contains a Compare node where one side is the Name `max_position_count` and the other references `len(app_state.positions)` (or equivalent runtime marker / known local alias), AND the literal source contains a runtime-marker string. Two-part check guards against both (a) dropping the comparison entirely and (b) replacing the runtime side with a hardcoded constant. Failure-before-fix verified both ways.

## 28. Monitoring & Gap Detection

### [MED-037] [Correctness] core/order_enrichment.py:231-274
  Description: Slippage calculation uses planned entry price (from pre_trade_log) not actual entry order fill price
  Evidence: `compute_slippage_actual(fill_type='entry')` compares fill against `effective_entry` from plan, not against the limit price of the entry order
  Impact: Slippage metric measures plan-vs-execution gap, not order-vs-fill gap — misleading for trades where the limit order itself was at a different price than the plan
  Recommended fix: Document that this is "shortfall vs plan" not "slippage vs order"; or add second metric for order-vs-fill comparison
  Effort: small (doc) / medium (new metric)
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence (order_enrichment.py:237-241 docstring):
      """Compute slippage_actual for a fill.
      - entry: (fill_price - pre_trade_log.effective_entry) / effective_entry
      - tp/sl: (fill_price - parent_order.stop_price) / parent_order.stop_price
      ...
      """
  Sweep note: The function's own docstring confirms the semantic is "fill vs plan's effective_entry" for entries — exactly what the audit calls misleading nomenclature. Cheapest fix is the doc-only rename to "shortfall_vs_plan"; new metric for "order-vs-fill" is the more substantive option.

### [MED-038] [Reliability] core/monitoring.py:404-413
  Description: DB health check blocks entire monitoring coroutine for up to 5s if DB is stuck
  Evidence: `await asyncio.wait_for(cur.fetchone(), timeout=5.0)` — other checks delayed while this blocks
  Impact: Monitoring responsiveness degraded during DB contention
  Recommended fix: Run checks concurrently with `asyncio.gather()` or individual task isolation
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence (monitoring.py:404-413):
      async def _check_db_health(self) -> None:
          try:
              async with db._conn.execute("SELECT 1") as cur:
                  await asyncio.wait_for(cur.fetchone(), timeout=_DB_HEALTH_TIMEOUT)
              self.resolve("db_unreachable")
          except Exception:
              if not any(...):
                  self.emit("db_unreachable", "critical", ...)
  Sweep note: Confirmed verbatim. The sequential-await pattern in the monitoring loop means each check holds the coroutine for its timeout. asyncio.gather() of independent checks is the recommended fix shape.

### [MED-039] [Correctness] core/equity_gap_detector.py:53
  Description: Gap threshold (2x interval = 20 min default) too lenient — misses 5-15 minute downtime windows
  Evidence: `threshold_ms = expected_interval_seconds * 2 * 1000` — 10-min interval → 20-min threshold. 15-min restart not flagged as gap.
  Impact: Analytics marked as valid despite data gaps from short downtimes
  Recommended fix: Reduce to 1.5x or make configurable; add startup gap check
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5d)
  Sweep evidence (equity_gap_detector.py:53):
      threshold_ms = expected_interval_seconds * 2 * 1000
  Sweep note: Confirmed verbatim. Audit's 1.5x suggestion is the simplest fix; making it configurable is the cleaner path.
