## Seventh Pass — Final (2026-05-17, same session)

Final audit of base template, SSE streaming, static assets, service worker,
and end-to-end position close data flow. This pass confirms several areas
as safe and identifies 5 remaining findings.

Confirmed SAFE (no findings):
- SSE backpressure: bounded queue (100 max), non-blocking publish, QueueFull drops with log
- Service worker: network-first strategy for API/fragments — trading data never served stale
- _paginate_list(): safe in-memory pagination, no edge case bugs
- All global JS functions: no unchecked user input flows to dangerous sinks
- Position close flow: REPLACE logic prevents duplicate rows; reconciler catches gaps

## 29. Frontend Security Hardening

### [MED-040] [Security] templates/base.html:18-21
  Description: CDN scripts loaded without Subresource Integrity (SRI) hashes
  Evidence: `<script src="https://unpkg.com/htmx.org@1.9.12"></script>` — no `integrity` attribute. Same for idiomorph@0.3.0 and echarts@5.5.1.
  Impact: CDN compromise (unpkg, jsdelivr) would inject malicious code into every page load
  Recommended fix: Add `integrity="sha384-..."` and `crossorigin="anonymous"` to all CDN script tags
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5e)
  Sweep evidence (base.html:18-21):
      <script src="https://unpkg.com/htmx.org@1.9.12"></script>
      <script src="https://unpkg.com/htmx.org@1.9.12/dist/ext/sse.js"></script>
      <script src="https://unpkg.com/idiomorph@0.3.0/dist/idiomorph-ext.min.js"></script>
      <script src="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/echarts@5.5.1/dist/echarts.min.js"></script>
  Sweep note: Four CDN scripts confirmed with no `integrity` or `crossorigin` attribute. Real CDN-compromise vector. Pairs with MED-041 (no CSP) as the "frontend security hardening" bundle.

### [MED-041] [Security] main.py (no CSP)
  Description: No Content-Security-Policy header configured on any response
  Evidence: No CSP middleware in main.py; no `<meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy">` in base.html
  Impact: Inline scripts and CDN scripts are unrestricted; XSS exploitation easier if a vector is found
  Recommended fix: Add CSP middleware: `script-src 'self' https://unpkg.com https://cdn.jsdelivr.net; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5e)
  Sweep evidence: grep `Content-Security-Policy|CSP|add_middleware` in main.py returns 0 hits. grep `Content-Security-Policy` in templates/base.html returns 0 hits.
  Sweep note: Confirmed by absence. No CSP at HTTP-header level or meta-tag level. Bundles with MED-040 (no SRI) as the "frontend security hardening" bundle — single FastAPI middleware addition covers both directions.

### [MED-042] [Reliability] core/order_manager.py + core/db_orders.py (position close path)
  Description: No explicit timeout on DB operations in the critical position-close path
  Evidence: `await self._conn.execute(sql, params)` in `insert_closed_position()` and `upsert_order_batch()` — no `asyncio.wait_for()` wrapper
  Impact: Slow DB (disk contention, WAL checkpoint) stalls position close for unbounded time; UI shows stale state
  Recommended fix: Wrap critical DB operations in `asyncio.wait_for(op, timeout=5.0)` with fallback logging
  Effort: medium
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5e)
  Sweep evidence: grep `asyncio\.wait_for|insert_closed_position|upsert_order_batch` in core/db_orders.py returns the method definitions at lines 36 + 157 and their log.exception sites at 105 + 233, but no `asyncio.wait_for` wrapper anywhere in the file. Same for core/order_manager.py's call sites (line 785 invokes insert_closed_position bare).
  Sweep note: Confirmed by absence. Pairs naturally with HIGH-026 (silent-swallow in close path) — both about position-close path reliability. Both are Phase 4 candidates.

### [LOW-020] [Quality] templates/base.html (static assets)
  Description: Static files served without explicit Cache-Control headers
  Evidence: FastAPI `StaticFiles` mount uses defaults — no `max-age` or `immutable` directives
  Impact: Browser uses heuristic caching; mitigated by `?v=N` query strings on versioned assets
  Recommended fix: Add `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400` for static assets
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5e)
  Sweep evidence (main.py:126):
      app.mount("/static", StaticFiles(directory="static"), name="static")
  Sweep note: Bare StaticFiles mount, no cache_control kwarg or wrapping middleware. Audit's own framing ("mitigated by `?v=N` query strings") is right — this is a hygiene fix, not a correctness one.

### [LOW-021] [Reliability] api/routes_streams.py
  Description: No maximum concurrent SSE connections limit
  Evidence: Each `EventSourceResponse` creates an asyncio.Queue subscriber; no cap on concurrent subscriptions
  Impact: Hundreds of open browser tabs could exhaust memory (~5KB each, but asyncio tasks add overhead)
  Recommended fix: Track active SSE connections; reject new ones above threshold (e.g., 50)
  Effort: small
  Sweep classification: VERIFIED REAL (Task 90.5e)
  Sweep evidence: grep `EventSourceResponse|max_connections|active_sse|connection_limit|MAX_SSE` in routes_streams.py returns only the two `EventSourceResponse(` instantiation sites at :52 and :78. No connection-counting state, no limit gate.
  Sweep note: Confirmed by absence. Single-user deployment makes the DoS-via-tabs less likely than the multi-user case, but adding a cap is cheap insurance.

### [LOW-023] [Test Infrastructure] tests/test_task101_sql_injection_defense.py:TestRoutesRejectInjection
  Description: Class-scoped `client` fixture builds a second `TestClient(app)` against the FastAPI app singleton. In full-suite runs `tests/test_routes.py` constructs the same TestClient(app) earlier under a module-scoped fixture; when this second TestClient's `with`-context calls lifespan startup again on the already-initialized app, the startup hangs indefinitely. Symptom matches Task 101's original ~1-hour test-suite hang exactly.
  Evidence: `pytest tests/` (full suite) hangs at TestRoutesRejectInjection's first test method (test_injection_in_sort_by_returns_400). pytest-timeout 2.4.0 (Task 101.1) catches the hang at the 30s mark with the stack frame pointing to tests/test_task101_sql_injection_defense.py:381 — the `with TestClient(app) as c:` line. Running the test file in isolation (`pytest tests/test_task101_sql_injection_defense.py`) completes in ~4s.
  Impact: 3 e2e injection-rejection tests (test_injection_in_sort_by_returns_400, test_injection_in_sort_dir_returns_400, test_valid_sort_request_returns_200) excluded from full-suite runs via @pytest.mark.skip. Coverage for the route-level wiring is preserved by TestRouteWiring's 4 source-pin tests in the same file; the wiring is verified by source inspection rather than HTTP. The injection rejection behavior is verified at the validator level by TestValidateSortParams (7 tests including concrete injection payloads).
  Recommended fix: Two options — (a) hoist the `client` fixture to `tests/conftest.py` with module or session scope so all callers share one TestClient/app instance; (b) investigate the lifespan re-entry deadlock (likely DB init, WS startup, or scheduler thread). Option (a) is the smaller change and resolves the immediate symptom; option (b) addresses the root architectural issue and would also help any other future test that needs a TestClient.
  Effort: small for (a), medium for (b).
  Status: OPEN — skip marker in place with reference to this finding. The skip's `reason=` text instructs how to re-enable.
  Discovered-during: Task 101.1 (pytest-timeout 30s caught the hang on first full-suite run after install — exactly the failure mode the guardrail was designed to surface).
  Cross-reference: HIGH-011 / MED-003 / MED-022 (parent fix in Task 101); Task 101.1 commit (guardrail that exposed the hang).
  Target phase: Phase 4 follow-up or test-infrastructure cleanup pass.
  Filed-during: Task 101.1

# Frontend Findings (audit-01, 2026-05-18)

Source: `docs/audits/2026-05-18-v2.4-frontend-audit-01.md` (full evidence, screenshots, methodology notes).

Audit conducted via Claude-in-Chrome session, ~25-30 min wall time, against engine at commit `c2bd969`. Coverage: Dashboard, Calculator, History (Position / Order / Trade), Analytics Overview, Backtest Run, Regime Overview, Config (Accounts + Connections), Add Account modal. Coverage gaps acknowledged — see audit-01 doc Section 14 ("Coverage Gaps (Honest)") below.

All 28 findings carry the `FE-` prefix to coexist with backend IDs. None are resolved yet — all Status: OPEN. Phase 5 scope.

## 30. Frontend Critical

### [FE-CRIT-001] [UX/Data] Configuration → Accounts: detail panel returns HTTP 500
  Description: Clicking the only existing account card on `/config` (Accounts tab) does nothing visible. The right-side detail panel stays at "Select an account..." placeholder.
  Evidence:
    - Console: `Response Status Error Code 500 from /fragments/account-detail/1` (htmx error handler, fired on both attempts at 18:14:23 and 18:14:39 local).
    - Network: `GET http://localhost:8000/fragments/account-detail/1` → statusCode 500.
    - Screenshot: ss_43293men4 (post-click, panel still shows placeholder).
  Impact: User cannot view or modify their account settings — including the `link_window_seconds` setting that v2.4's HIGH-027 work depends on. The fragment swap fails silently because htmx doesn't surface the 500 to the user. No toast, no error banner, no console output for a non-developer to notice.
  Recommended fix: (1) Get the server-side traceback (engine logs would have it — auditor couldn't isolate from sandbox because of log file path differences). (2) Add an htmx `htmx:responseError` handler that surfaces 4xx / 5xx swaps as a visible toast or banner — silent failure on a critical interactive is unacceptable.
  Effort: Small (probably a simple template-rendering bug on the server side) + small (htmx error handler is a one-time global add).
  Screenshots: ss_43293men4
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 108 (v2.4.1.1 hotfix).
  Root cause: `templates/fragments/account_detail.html:226` had `{% set lw_is_preset = lw_current in [p[0] for p in lw_presets] %}` — a Python list comprehension, which Jinja2 does not support. Render raised `jinja2.exceptions.TemplateSyntaxError: expected token ',', got 'for'`, surfacing as `GET /fragments/account-detail/{id}` → 500 silently (htmx aborts the swap with no UI signal). The bad line was introduced in Task 104b (commit `0d1fa93`); the Task 104b template-wiring tests grep'd template source for substrings but never actually rendered the template, so the syntax error slipped past CI for the entire Phase 4 window.
  Implementation note: Replaced the comprehension with a namespace-based loop pattern (Jinja2-idiomatic for "set a flag inside `{% for %}`"): `{% set ns = namespace(is_preset=false) %}{% for p in lw_presets %}{% if p[0] == lw_current %}{% set ns.is_preset = true %}{% endif %}{% endfor %}{% set lw_is_preset = ns.is_preset %}`. The htmx `htmx:responseError` + `htmx:sendError` global handlers were also added (see FE-MED-015 filed + resolved this task) so any future silent-500 surfaces as a visible toast immediately.
  Verification: Direct-render test against the actual template file with a synthetic context passes both preset and custom-value branches (`tests/test_task108_hotfix_v2_4_1_1.py::TestAccountDetailTemplateCompiles`, 2 cases). TestClient-based end-to-end tests (3 cases) pin the route's 200-response shape but are `@pytest.mark.skip`-ed in full-suite due to LOW-023 (double-TestClient lifespan deadlock); they pass when the file is run in isolation.

### [FE-CRIT-002] [Data/Architecture] Seven of eight Analytics sub-tabs return HTTP 503
  Description: Clicking Calendar PnL, Traded Pairs, MFE/MAE, R-Multiples, Risk Metrics, Funding, or Beta Exposure triggers an htmx GET to the corresponding `/fragments/analytics/*` endpoint. All seven endpoints return HTTP 503 Service Unavailable. The panels stay on a permanent "Loading..." indicator. Only Analytics → Overview renders successfully — the audit surface for the other 7 sub-tabs is non-functional.
  Evidence: Browser console captured 7 distinct htmx errors during audit-02 Session A, one per sub-tab — `503 from /fragments/analytics/{calendar,pairs,excursions,r_multiples,var,funding,beta}?period=monthly&offset=0` (all observed 2026-05-18 8:12 PM, retested 8:16 PM, persistent both times). Engine log shows a recurring `EventBus run loop error: <Queue ...> is bound to a different event loop` at high frequency around the failures. May or may not be related; backend triage needed to confirm root cause.
  Screenshots: ss_6727jupq1 (Calendar PnL Loading state), ss_2049l2aha (Traded Pairs), ss_8754rp8we (MFE/MAE), ss_17647y784 (R-Multiples), ss_0499id7to (Risk Metrics), ss_84212dktp (Funding), ss_3351087t7 (Beta Exposure).
  Impact: Most of the Analytics surface is non-functional. User has no access to Calendar PnL, Traded Pairs ranking, MFE/MAE statistics, R-Multiples distribution, VaR / Risk Metrics, Funding rate analysis, or Beta Exposure — these are the analytical surfaces a trader uses after-hours to review performance. Analytics → Overview alone is not sufficient. Also blocks audit-02 Sessions B and C: the prompt's per-sub-tab visual / data-quality / formatting checks cannot be performed while the panels return 503. Re-audit pass on the 7 sub-tabs after fix would surface a substantial second wave of findings (estimated 10-20 based on audit-01's depth on Overview alone).
  Recommended fix:
    1. **Backend triage first** — establish whether the 503 is one shared dependency (likely, given the uniform pattern) or seven independent handler bugs. The EventBus event-loop binding error in the engine log is the strongest root-cause candidate: handlers may all wait on the event bus (publish query intents / subscribe to downstream computations); a bus bound to the wrong asyncio loop would 503 every handler.
    2. **Decision after root cause** — operator decides whether this gates v2.5 or ships with broken sub-tabs documented as known-down. v2.4.3 was assembled before this finding surfaced; push-to-origin decision may want to wait for hotfix.
  Effort: Unknown without backend investigation. If event-loop binding (single root cause): likely small (asyncio.Queue creation order in startup). If seven separate broken handlers: larger.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A (2026-05-18).
  Filed-during: Task 127 (audit-02 reconciliation).
  Status: **VERIFIED-FALSE** in Task 128 (2026-05-19, post-operator-verification). The engine was stopped during audit-02 Session A — the 503 responses were an audit-time artifact (browser hitting an unreachable engine), NOT a real backend bug. Operator verified Analytics sub-tabs render correctly when engine is running. Retracted. The EventBus event-loop binding error in the engine log was also an artifact of the stopped/restarting state, not a real runtime fault.
  Calibration note: this is the second audit-time-artifact false positive in the ledger (after audit-01's race-framing FPs and the audit-impact-imprecision pattern). New discipline added to CLAUDE.md: browser-driven audit sessions must verify engine reachability before filing findings (pre-flight check on `/` returning 200 + valid HTML; halt + surface to operator if 503s appear mid-session).

## 31. Frontend High

### [FE-HIGH-001] [UI] Header "Plugin OFF" indicator is misread as "engine offline"
  Description: The top-right area of the header reads `Account 1 (Binanc...) ▾  |  Standalone ▾  |  ◯ Plugin  ● OFF  +437ms  |  ⚙`. The word "OFF" floats free of the word "Plugin" — visually it parses as a separate column. The engine owner read this during the audit as "the engine is offline" before realising it was the plugin indicator. The empty circle before "Plugin" is a decorative separator, but the red filled dot before "OFF" is a real status light, so the eye trains itself to read "two status lights" when there's really one.

  Secondary aspect surfaced by Aryo: the `+437ms` latency number appears stuck on the OFF state — the indicator was supposed to be showing a live ping/latency value the way the dashboard's `LATENCY 95ms` does, but it's currently overwritten by "OFF" text. The latency number and the connection state are competing for the same display slot.
  Evidence: Zoomed screenshot of header (ss_5224rm25o region 930-1308 × 0-40). Two distinct latency numbers visible on every page: `LATENCY 127ms` in status bar AND `+437ms` near Plugin OFF — different values, no documented relationship.
  Impact: Misreading "engine offline" on a trading dashboard is the worst class of UX bug. Even if the user knows the plugin is intentionally off, the visual primes anxiety. And the duplicated latency display means the user has to mentally reconcile two competing numbers every time their eye lands on the header.
  Recommended fix: Treat the plugin status as a single labeled component. Options:
    - "Quantower: Disconnected" (clear label, no ambiguity, no separate "OFF" word floating).
    - When plugin is OFF, suppress the latency value entirely (or grey it out — the value is meaningless when there's nothing to ping). When plugin is ON, show "Quantower: 43ms" as a single coherent unit.
    - Unify with the status-bar `LATENCY` reading: pick one metric, display it once, label it clearly.
  Effort: Medium (touches the StatusIndicator primitive and possibly backend metric exposure).
  Screenshots: ss_5224rm25o (zoom)
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 121 — first Bundle A primitive (StatusIndicator) built; FE-HIGH-001 site migrated to it. **Re-confirmed RESOLVED in Task 141** (post-Task-138 reopen-vs-new-finding decision): Session A' verification noted "Quantower · Disconnected · OFF +678ms" — the substantive HIGH-tier concern ("engine offline misread") is fully resolved by the StatusIndicator migration. The remaining trailing-redundancy concern ("OFF +Xms" alongside "Disconnected" is redundant) is LOW-tier polish, filed separately as FE-LOW-025 rather than reopening FE-HIGH-001 as PARTIAL. Reasoning per Task 141 decision: HIGH severity reflected the misread-engine-offline ambiguity; that's gone. Keeping FE-HIGH-001 open at HIGH for a polish concern would inflate the HIGH count. Split-with-new-LOW preserves severity accuracy.
  Implementation note: The free-floating dot + "Plugin" + adjacent WS-status "OFF" + "+437ms" composition that the eye misread is replaced by a single StatusIndicator showing `Quantower : Connected` (green) / `Quantower : Disconnected` (red) / `Quantower : Connecting...` (neutral grey). The latency / clock-offset display in the WS-status bar to its right stays — those are now visually distinct (separate primitive, separate CSS namespace, separate JS lifecycle). The eye no longer groups them into a composite.
  Architectural decisions (precedent-setting for subsequent Bundle A primitives — Card, TableRow, EmptyState, PeriodSelector):
    - **Directory**: `templates/primitives/` for reusable stateless component macros. Distinguishes from `templates/fragments/` which holds HTMX response-target fragments. Convention documented in `templates/primitives/README.md`.
    - **Pattern**: Jinja2 `{% macro %}` for stateless primitives (no slot content); `{% include %}` with blocks reserved for primitives that need slots (Card likely). StatusIndicator is the macro reference; Card will decide when its API is needed.
    - **CSS organisation**: inline in `base.html` `<style>` block under a `── Primitives (Bundle A — Task 121) ──` section. Class prefix per primitive (`.si-*` here; future `.card-*`, `.tr-p-*`, `.es-*`, `.ps-*`). When primitive CSS crosses ~500 lines, extract to `static/css/primitives.css`. Inline-for-now matches the codebase's existing inline-style convention; extraction is opportunistic.
    - **Severity vocabulary**: 5 words — `success / warning / error / info / neutral` — mapped to existing CSS vars (`--green / --amber / --red / --blue / --muted`). All future primitives that signal state reuse this vocabulary; do not introduce custom severity words.
    - **JS state-transition pattern**: macro renders the initial state via parameters; client-side JS uses `el.classList.remove('si-success', ...)` + `classList.add('si-' + sev)` to swap severity. The `setSeverity(el, sev)` helper in `base.html`'s plugin-polling block is the reference; future state-driven primitives reuse the shape.
  StatusIndicator API: `status_indicator(severity, label, value="", dot=true, id="")`. Unknown severity falls through to `neutral` (defensive). HTML-escapes the label (autoescape env). Value-present sets `data-has-value="1"` for JS / selector targeting. See `templates/primitives/status_indicator.html` macro docstring + `templates/primitives/README.md` for the full convention.
  Tests (tests/test_task121_status_indicator.py, 21 cases):
    - TestStatusIndicatorRendering (10) — severity × value × dot × id matrix; defensive bogus-severity fallthrough; HTML escape; absent-when-empty for value, id, and the `data-has-value` marker.
    - TestFeHigh001HeaderMigration (6) — base.html imports the macro, calls it with `label="Quantower"` + `id="plugin-indicator"`, the old `◌ Plugin` / `● Plugin` glyph text is removed, JS sets value text via `.si-value`, JS uses `setSeverity` + `si-{success/error/neutral}` class swap, primitives CSS block present.
    - TestPrimitivesReadme (3) — README exists, documents all 5 severities, references StatusIndicator + Task 121.
    - TestPrimitiveTemplateCompiles (2) — explicit MED-047 compile pins for the macro file + base.html post-migration.
    Plus 2 updates to pre-existing `tests/test_time_sync.py::TestHeaderUIPolish` — `test_plugin_indicator_always_visible` (loosened block window from 200 → 400 chars to span the macro call) and `test_plugin_js_shows_on_off_states` → `test_plugin_js_shows_connected_and_disconnected_states` (pins the new JS behaviour: 'Connected'/'Disconnected' strings + si-success/si-error class swap).
  FBF: Stashing `templates/base.html` + `tests/test_time_sync.py` only (the new primitive macro file + new test file remain untracked) → 6 of the 21 tests fail (all 6 FE-HIGH-001 wiring pins). 10 macro-rendering tests pass (they exercise the macro directly, independent of base.html). 3 README pins pass. 2 compile pins pass. Restoring → 21/21 pass. Full suite: 1623 passed, 6 skipped, 24 s — no regressions.
  Latent observations (informational, not filed):
    - **Other status displays that could migrate** but deferred: `ws_status.html` fragment (the WS / REST / OFF indicator with latency + age + clock offset — Task 121 explicitly left out-of-scope per task spec). It's the closest analog and would benefit from StatusIndicator, but its multi-field shape (latency + age + offset) maps to a future composite primitive, not bare StatusIndicator. Worth a future task once Bundle A's Card primitive lands (so the multi-field composite can be expressed cleanly).
    - **Constraint surfaced for Card primitive design**: nested Jinja2 `{# ... #}` comments don't nest — an inner `{# #}` closes the outer. Encountered while writing the StatusIndicator macro's example-usage docstring (line 13 `{# no value #}` inside the outer docstring broke template parsing). Card's docstring will likely have multi-line examples; pre-flight test compilation BEFORE committing the macro file. Recorded in MED-047 as "template-touching tasks: compile-render before commit, not after."
    - **JS pattern for class-swap is already a small primitive**: `setSeverity(el, sev)` is reusable across any StatusIndicator caller. When the second consumer lands (Card status badges, e.g.) it should be lifted to a global `window.statusIndicator.setSeverity(...)` helper or a small inline `<script>` block in `base.html`'s primitives section. For now it's scoped to the plugin-polling IIFE.
  Cross-reference: This task establishes the Bundle A precedent. Subsequent primitive tasks (Card / TableRow / EmptyState / PeriodSelector) reference the conventions in `templates/primitives/README.md`. The severity vocabulary is locked — any future task that needs a 6th severity word triggers an audit-doc / README update, not a silent additional class.

### [FE-HIGH-002] [Architecture/UX] Add Account modal: EXCHANGE dropdown only offers Binance
  Description: Calibration finding #1 confirmed. The EXCHANGE select in the Add Account modal contains exactly one `<option>`: `value="binance"` / label "Binance". Bybit and MEXC adapters exist in the backend (`core/adapters/`) but are not exposed in the UI.
  Evidence: `read_page` on the EXCHANGE combobox returned a single option. Screenshot ss_937403uft.
  Impact: User cannot add a non-Binance account through the UI. Architecturally inconsistent — backend supports multi-exchange, frontend doesn't. Also blocks meaningful Phase-5 verification of HIGH-028 (Bybit reconciliation), HIGH-030 (Bybit account-fields), HIGH-031 (MEXC account-fields) — those parallels can only be tested end-to-end with a non-Binance account, which the UI currently cannot create.
  Recommended fix (updated 2026-05-18 per operator decision): Populate the EXCHANGE dropdown from the registered adapter list. Non-Binance options carry a `(Beta)` suffix on the label — `"Binance"`, `"Bybit (Beta)"`, `"MEXC (Beta)"`. The tag indicates "adapter is built and exposed but not yet operator-verified in production trading." Bybit and MEXC don't get hidden or warning-banner-gated; the inline label tag is enough. Audit-01 Open Question #5 ANSWERED.
  Effort: Small.
  Screenshots: ss_937403uft
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 111.
  Implementation note:
    - `core/adapters/registry.py`: new `list_rest_exchanges(market_type="linear_perpetual")` helper. Returns `[{"value", "label", "is_beta"}, ...]`. Sort: canonical exchanges first (alphabetical), then Beta (alphabetical). Canonical set governed by module constant `_CANONICAL_EXCHANGES = frozenset({"binance"})` — graduating Bybit / MEXC out of Beta is a one-line change here. Label override for `mexc` → `MEXC` (uppercase acronym; title-case would render `Mexc`).
    - `api/helpers.py`: `_ctx` adds `available_exchanges` key from `list_rest_exchanges()`. Every page render now carries the dropdown data — the Add Account modal lives in `base.html` (included everywhere via template inheritance).
    - `templates/base.html:430-444`: hardcoded `<option value="binance">Binance</option>` replaced with `{% for ex in available_exchanges %}<option value="{{ ex.value }}">{{ ex.label }}</option>{% endfor %}`. Comment block in template references this finding ID for traceability.
  Tests (`tests/test_task111_exchange_dropdown.py`, 13 cases): 6 helper unit tests (registered exchanges list, Binance unsuffixed, Bybit "(Beta)", MEXC uppercase override, canonical-first sort, market_type filter); 5 template-render tests (compile + render via Jinja2 with all 3 options present, anti-revert pin against the old hardcoded option, source pin on the for-loop, render ordering check, empty-list defensive render); 2 wiring pins (_ctx references `available_exchanges` + imports `list_rest_exchanges`). Template-render tests follow MED-047 discipline — compile + render, not source-string grep.
  Failure-before-fix verification: pre-fix, source-pin `test_template_does_not_hardcode_single_binance_option` greps the literal `<option value="binance">Binance</option></select>` line that existed before. Reverting the template change would re-introduce that exact string and fail the pin. The render tests would fail with KeyError on `available_exchanges` if `_ctx` revert dropped the key.
  Cross-reference: unblocks Phase 5 Bundle B (HIGH-028 Bybit reconciliation + HIGH-030 Bybit account-field + HIGH-031 MEXC account-field) — those three need a non-Binance account to exercise end-to-end, which the modal now supports.

### [FE-HIGH-003] [Data] Trade Events Log: entries show entry_price 0.0 for BTCUSDT closures — DEMOTED to FE-MED-017
  Description: On History → Trade Events Log tab, every visible `position_closed` event for BTCUSDT (278 entries total) expands to JSON like `{"symbol": "BTCUSDT", "direction": "LONG", "entry_price": 0.0, "exit_price": 110.0, "realized_pnl": 0.0, "net_pnl": -0.5, "exit_reason": "manual"}`. BTCUSDT prices in 2026 are tens of thousands. `entry_price: 0.0` and `exit_price: 110.0` are clearly wrong — these are synthetic / test / replay artifacts that have polluted the events log.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_2916mizx9. 278 BTCUSDT entries visible across multiple timestamps, all the same shape.
  Impact (revised): Cosmetic + log-readability, not real-data corruption. Operator confirms (2026-05-18) no BTCUSDT trading in the relevant timeframe — these are residual test pollution from earlier development. No money-at-risk; downstream calculations are NOT silently corrupted (the events are not consumed by the live pipeline).
  Recommended fix: see FE-MED-017 (renumbered entry below) for the revised remediation plan.
  Effort: Medium.
  Screenshots: ss_2916mizx9
  Status: SCOPE-CONFIRMED + DEMOTED to MED 2026-05-18 (Task 110) — audit-01 Open Question #4 ANSWERED. Real-data risk was overstated; severity reduced HIGH → MED. Tracked going forward as FE-MED-017.

### [FE-MED-017] [Data/Log-Hygiene] Trade Events Log: BTCUSDT test-pollution rows (renumbered from FE-HIGH-003)
  Description: 278 BTCUSDT `position_closed` events with `entry_price: 0.0` exit residual from pre-v2.4.1 development testing. Operator confirms no real BTCUSDT trading occurred in the relevant period — these are not corrupted live data. Pollution makes the events log harder to read but does not corrupt downstream analytics.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_2916mizx9. Operator confirmation 2026-05-18.
  Impact: Log-hygiene + UI clutter. Operator scanning the events log for forensic purposes sees nonsense rows mixed with real entries. No analytics or PnL impact (the events are not part of the live trade-attribution pipeline).
  Recommended fix:
    1. One-off cleanup: `DELETE FROM trade_events WHERE symbol = 'BTCUSDT' AND entry_price < 1 AND timestamp_ms < <cutoff>` where `<cutoff>` is before the operator's last real trading session (safety margin).
    2. Write-time guard rejecting events with `entry_price <= 0` (or below a per-symbol minimum). Defense-in-depth against re-pollution.
    3. Optional: add an `event_origin` column (`'live' | 'test' | 'replay'`) and a UI filter so synthetic events stay in DB for debug but don't clutter the default view.
  Effort: Small (cleanup) + small (write-time guard) + medium (origin column + UI filter, if pursued).
  Status: OPEN — Phase 5 mini-phase 5.4 (data quality + performance bundle).
  Filed-during: Task 110 (renumbered from FE-HIGH-003 on demotion).

### [FE-HIGH-004] [Performance/Network] Dashboard polls /fragments/ws_status every ~1 second
  Description: Network monitor captured 206 requests on the Dashboard during a short observation window. ~85% were `GET /fragments/ws_status` — i.e., roughly 1 polling request per second. `GET /api/platform/connection` is interleaved every 5–6 ws_status pings (every ~5 s).
  Evidence: `read_network_requests` output: requests 1–50 show the pattern; full 206-request set is in the saved tool-results file.
  Impact: 1 Hz polling for what's typically a static-ish boolean (WS up/down) is wasteful. On a battery-powered laptop it adds visible CPU churn; on a server-side render it adds template-rendering load. Over 24 h it's ~86 000 requests for a single dashboard session.
  Recommended fix: Either move WS status to SSE (push instead of pull — the engine already uses SSE per the PRD), or back off the poll rate (5 s minimum, 10 s comfortable), or both. The current 1 s pulse should be reserved for genuinely fast-moving values (clock, latency).
  Effort: Small to medium depending on SSE integration cost.
  Status: RESOLVED-AS-INTENTIONAL 2026-05-18 (Task 110) — operator clarification confirms the 1 Hz cadence is BY DESIGN. The endpoint carries live latency data (visible as `+437ms` in the header), not just boolean connection state. Audit-01's "wasteful for static boolean" framing was an instance of audit-impact-imprecision (now 5th example of that pattern, joining HIGH-019 / HIGH-026 / HIGH-013 / HIGH-008). Other polling endpoints are already wider per design.
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-016 carries forward the SSE-migration optimization angle, separated from the (incorrect) bug framing. Audit-01 Open Question #3 ANSWERED.

### [FE-MED-016] [Performance/Network] /fragments/ws_status polling could migrate to SSE as an optimization
  Description: The 1 Hz cadence on `/fragments/ws_status` (FE-HIGH-004 above) is intentional for live-latency display, but ~86 000 requests / 24 h dashboard session is a sizable HTTP round-trip count. SSE-push could replace HTMX-pull for the latency stream as an optimization (reduces request count, allows sub-second updates without bumping load further). NOT a correctness fix — the current behavior is by design.
  Evidence: Carried forward from FE-HIGH-004's observation; reframed.
  Impact: Performance optimization opportunity, not a bug. Current load is well within engine capacity.
  Recommended fix: OPTIONAL — migrate `/fragments/ws_status` to SSE push if SSE infrastructure already exists (audit-01 noted it does per PRD). Defer indefinitely if cost > benefit.
  Effort: Small if SSE infra wires cleanly, medium otherwise.
  Status: OPEN — Phase 5 mini-phase 5.4 (data quality + performance bundle), low priority.
  Filed-during: Task 110 (reframed from FE-HIGH-004).

### [FE-HIGH-005] [UI/Data] Regime page: CURRENT REGIME shows "As of undefined"
  Description: The CURRENT REGIME card on `/regime` displays `Risk-On Choppy` with subtitle `As of undefined / full mode`. The string "undefined" appears literally where a timestamp should be.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_15579nr60.
  Impact: Looks like a JS / template bug leaking a JS `undefined` into rendered output. On a page where a trader needs to know "is this regime stale or fresh?", showing "undefined" is worse than showing nothing.
  Recommended fix: Find the template variable that's coming back undefined (likely `last_updated_ts` or similar). Either render a real timestamp (e.g., "As of 2026-05-18 10:53 UTC") or, if no data exists, render an explicit "—" or "no data". Never let a JS `undefined` reach the user.
  Effort: Small.
  Screenshots: ss_15579nr60
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 108 (v2.4.1.1 hotfix).
  Root cause: field-name mismatch between JS and API. `templates/regime.html:433` read `data.date`, but `api/routes_regime.py:42` returns the timestamp as `computed_at` (ISO string). `data.date` was always `undefined`, so the page rendered literal "As of undefined".
  Implementation note: JS rewritten to read `data.computed_at`, format as `"YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM UTC"`, and fall back to `"—"` when the value is null/undefined. Pinned by `TestRegimeAsOfRendering` (2 cases: source contains `data.computed_at`; null fallback exists).

### [FE-HIGH-006] [UX] Trade Events Log expansion is unformatted raw JSON on one line
  Description: Clicking a Trade Events Log row expands to show the detail. The expansion is the raw JSON string on a single line, truncated at row width. Example: `{"symbol": "BTCUSDT", "direction": "LONG", "entry_price": 0.0, "exit_price": 110.0, "realized_pnl": 0.0, "net_pnl": -0.5, "exit_reason": "manual"}` — all one line, monospace, no key-value layout, no formatting.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_2916mizx9 (row expanded after click).
  Impact: The whole point of an expand-on-click is to give users a readable detail view. Inline raw JSON is not readable for non-developers and barely faster than clicking through to a separate detail page.
  Recommended fix: Render the JSON as a key-value grid (label: value, one row each, monospace value column). Or pretty-print indented JSON in a `<pre>` block. Either way, the row should expand to several rows of height, not one.
  Effort: Small.
  Screenshots: ss_2916mizx9
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 113.
  Approach decision: KEY-VALUE GRID over pretty-printed JSON. Reasoning: operator scanning for a specific field anchors on the label column rather than text-searching JSON; consistent with the compact detail column (lines 76-86 of the same template) which already extracts specific fields by event_type; nested values stay rare (most payloads are flat), so the cost of inline-JSON-encoding for the rare nested case is acceptable.
  Implementation note: `templates/fragments/history/trade_events_table.html:89-114` — the expansion `<tr>` body. CSS grid with `grid-template-columns: max-content 1fr` (label column auto-sizes, value column fills). Iterates `p.items()` (the parsed `_payload` dict already prepared by the route handler at `api/routes_history.py:213`). Three render branches per value:
    - Mapping or non-string sequence → `tojson` filter (handles the rare nested case).
    - None → muted italic `null` (distinct from the Python `'None'` repr that would result from naive `{{ value }}`).
    - Otherwise → raw `{{ value }}` (scalar render).
  Empty-payload path renders a muted "No payload data." hint rather than an empty grid (defensive: parse failure in the route handler produces `_payload = {}`).
  Tests (`tests/test_task113_events_log_expansion.py`, 9 cases): every payload key appears as a label (with values rendered alongside); anti-revert pin against the literal raw single-line JSON string; CSS-grid source pin; empty-payload muted placeholder; null-value styled marker (not Python `None` repr); nested dict via `tojson`; compact-detail-column non-regression (the lines 76-86 logic still runs); explicit MED-047 template compile pin; empty-rows path still shows "No trade events found".
  Failure-before-fix verification: reverting the template to the prior `<pre>{{ r.payload_json }}</pre>` form would re-introduce the literal raw-JSON string in the rendered HTML, failing the `test_no_raw_payload_json_pre_block_for_populated_event` pin. The CSS-grid source pin would also fail (no `display:grid` in the expansion).
  Latent (don't fix here): the key-value grid is an extractable primitive — could become `EventDetail` / `KeyValueGrid` per audit-01's Bundle A "missing primitives" angle. Other future event-detail surfaces (exec_link panel, position-close drawer, etc.) would benefit. Flagged for Bundle A consideration but not pulled out now to keep this task narrow.

### [FE-HIGH-007] — REFRAMED in Task 128 → see FE-MED-019
  Originally filed in Task 127 as HIGH-tier driven by the FE-CRIT-002 503 cluster. Task 128 reframe: the underlying gap (htmx target-element not swapped on 5xx) is a real architectural concern, but the evidence basis (the 503 cluster) was an audit-time artifact (engine stopped during Session A — see FE-CRIT-002 VERIFIED-FALSE). Without a real failure surface demonstrating the gap, HIGH severity is not warranted. Reframed as MED-tier finding FE-MED-019 (smaller scope: design discipline for future failure paths, not an operational fix).
