## 32. Frontend Medium

### [FE-MED-001] [Design] Analytics overview cards have wider spacing than rest of engine — calibration #4 confirmed
  Description: The 5 cards on Analytics → Overview each span the full page width with metrics distributed across, generously spaced. Compare to History where rows are tight and information-dense. The visual rhythm breaks when navigating from History → Analytics.
  Evidence: Screenshots ss_42238ocm7 (Analytics overview), compared to ss_11017eioe (History).
  Impact: Inconsistency. Wastes vertical space on Analytics. Suggests the page was designed in isolation from the design language of the rest of the engine.
  Recommended fix: Build a Card primitive with documented padding tokens. Migrate Analytics and History to the same primitive. Aim for tighter rhythm matching History.
  Effort: Medium (Card primitive plus migration of 2+ pages).
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 122 — Card primitive built (Bundle A.2); Analytics Overview's 5 section cards migrated; rhythm now matches History (tight 8px × 10px via existing `.card-p8` utility class).
  Implementation note:
    - Built `templates/primitives/card.html` as Bundle A's first slot-content primitive. Slot pattern: `{% call card(...) %}body{% endcall %}` with Jinja2's `caller()` for the body. Header (title + optional subtitle) and footer remain keyword params; richer header content (action buttons, etc.) defers to a future open/close primitive pair when a real consumer needs it.
    - Default padding `"tight"` emits `<div class="card card-p8">` — the existing utility class History uses. The original loose default is opt-in via `padding="loose"` for backward-compat with non-migrated callers.
    - Title class composes `.sec-lbl` (existing section-label chrome) — primitive doesn't invent a new title style; reuses what the rest of the engine already does. Subtitle is small mono text alongside.
    - New CSS in `base.html` primitives section: `.card-header`, `.card-title` (overrides `.sec-lbl`'s margin so the header row controls spacing), `.card-subtitle`, `.card-body` (no rules — body content drives layout), `.card-footer` (8px top margin + 6px border-top).
    - Footer accepts raw HTML via `{{ footer | safe }}` — caller is responsible for escaping user-derived input. Body slot is autoescape'd by the calling template's autoescape config (default ON for `.html` extensions).
  Analytics Overview migration:
    - `templates/fragments/analytics/overview_stats.html`: 5 `<div class="card">` blocks replaced with `{% call card(title=..., subtitle=...) %}body{% endcall %}`. The "Volume & Activity — October 2025" pattern split into title (`"Volume & Activity"`) + subtitle (`period_label`) — visual separation between section name and period label.
    - End-to-end render confirms 5 wrappers all use `class="card card-p8"` post-migration (vs the loose `class="card"` pre-migration).
  Architectural decisions documented in `templates/primitives/README.md`:
    - Macro vs include vs call/caller decision tree expanded with Card's case (slot-content via call/caller; defer open/close pair until needed).
    - Jinja2 nested-comment gotcha (carried forward from Task 121 latent) added with explicit "compile-render before committing" discipline.
    - Card added to the prefix table (`card` prefix; class collisions managed by composing on the existing `.card` utility).
  CLAUDE.md updated with the Jinja2 nested-comment gotcha alongside MED-047 template-wiring discipline.
  Tests (`tests/test_task122_card_primitive.py`, 21 cases):
    - TestCardRendering (10) — title-only / title+subtitle / no-header / body / footer present + absent / tight + loose padding / HTML escape on title / safe-passthrough on footer.
    - TestAnalyticsOverviewMigration (4) — source-pin Card import; count 5 `{% call card(` blocks; anti-revert pin against bare `<div class="card">`; end-to-end render confirms 5 `class="card card-p8"` wrappers + section titles rendered.
    - TestCardCssInBase (1) — CSS block present in base.html with FE-MED-001 anchor.
    - TestDocsUpdates (3) — README documents Card + Jinja2 nested-comment gotcha; CLAUDE.md has gotcha cross-referenced to MED-047.
    - TestTemplateCompiles (3) — explicit MED-047 compile pins for Card macro + Analytics overview + StatusIndicator anti-regression.
  FBF: Stashing `templates/base.html` + `templates/fragments/analytics/overview_stats.html` (CSS + migration) → 5 of 21 tests fail (4 Analytics migration pins + 1 CSS-block pin). The 16 passes exercise the macro directly + README + CLAUDE.md + compile pins (independent of base.html / Analytics). Restoring → 21/21 pass. Full suite: 1644 passed, 6 skipped, 24 s — no regressions.
  Scope decision — related card findings:
    - **FE-LOW-003** (Connections cards different layouts Connected vs Not Set): NOT addressed by Card migration. The drift is *within-card content layout* (one-row condensed vs two-row expanded), not padding rhythm. Card primitive locks padding; content design is separate. Deferred to a future content-layout task.
    - **FE-LOW-006** (Backtest sessions card empty on right half): NOT addressed by Card migration. The issue is page-level layout (two-column vs full-width when right side is empty), not card padding. Deferred.
    - **FE-MED-014** (account card preview): already RESOLVED in Task 108 (v2.4.1.1 hotfix, collapsed by FE-CRIT-001 fix). Not in scope.
  Latent observations for the next primitive (TableRow):
    - Card composes on existing utility classes (`.card`, `.card-p8`). TableRow likely composes on `<tr>` + existing cell-styling classes (`.td-symbol`, `.td-ts`, `.td-sub`, `.td-dim`, `.mono`). Primitive should add structure (action-cell slot, hover state, selection state) without reinventing the cell typography.
    - Card's `{% call %}` + single-body-slot pattern works because the body is one contiguous block. TableRow may need multiple slots (one per cell) — `{% call %}` doesn't support that natively. Options: per-cell macros (`{% call tr_p() %}{% call cell(...) %}…{% endcall %}{% endcall %}`), or an open/close pair (`{{ tr_p_open() }}<td>…</td><td>…</td>{{ tr_p_close() }}`). My weak prior for TableRow: open/close pair, since `<tr>` body is a list of `<td>` siblings (not one block) and per-cell macros add nesting noise.
    - The "compose on existing utility classes" pattern from Card is the convention going forward — don't reinvent typography or spacing utilities, build slot structure atop what exists.

### [FE-MED-002] [Architecture] Row styling diverges across history tables — calibration #2 confirmed
  Description: Position History rows and Order History rows have consistent treatment. Trade History rows diverge: the SIDE cell renders **two** colored words inline (`Close LONG` / `Open SHORT` etc.) where Position History uses a single colored word (`LONG` / `SHORT`) and Order History uses a single colored word (`BUY` / `SELL`). The Trade History SIDE cell is visually noisier than the other two by design.
  Evidence: Screenshots ss_4313zsgql (Trade History) vs ss_11017eioe (Position History) vs ss_84879w5e5 (Order History).
  Impact: Calibration finding — Aryo's eye already flagged this. The row component is rolled per-table; that's why drift happens. The drift isn't fatal but adds cognitive load when scanning between tabs.
  Recommended fix: Build a TableRow primitive with consistent typography, spacing, color tokens, hover / selection states. Migrate all 3 history tables to use it. For the Open / Close + LONG / SHORT case in Trade History, decide whether it belongs in one cell (it currently does) or two (cleaner). Recommendation: split into two columns, "ACTION" (Open / Close) and "SIDE" (LONG / SHORT), each single-color. Calibration #3 covered.
  Effort: Medium.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 123 — Bundle A.3. TableRow primitive built (first sibling-list-slot primitive); all 3 history tables migrated + Trade History SIDE column split into ACTION + SIDE per calibration #3.
  Audit-text clarification: the "Trade History" referenced in the audit screenshot ss_4313zsgql maps to `templates/fragments/history/fills_table.html` (whose section label is `Trade History (N entries)`), not `trade_history_table.html` (which is the closed-trades summary view and uses the single-word LONG/SHORT pattern already). The audit's path name was implicit; the fix is in `fills_table.html`.
  Implementation note:
    - Built `templates/primitives/table_row.html` as Bundle A's first sibling-list-slot primitive. Pattern: **open/close pair** (`tr_open` + `tr_close`) rather than `{% call %}` + `caller()`. Reason: `<tr>` body is N sibling `<td>` cells, not one contiguous block — `caller()` would force callers to wrap cells in an opaque blob, losing per-cell structure.
    - API: `tr_open(state="default", on_click="", id="", extra_class="")` + `tr_close()`. Ergonomic shortcut: `on_click=...` alone implies `state="clickable"` so callers don't repeat themselves.
    - States composed via class: `.tr-p` (base, faint hover), `.tr-p-clickable` (cursor:pointer + stronger hover), `.tr-p-selected` (dynamic — JS-toggled, not initial-state).
    - Design tokens reuse existing palette: hover `rgba(255,255,255,.02)`, clickable hover `rgba(59,130,246,.04)`, selected `rgba(59,130,246,.08)`. No new color tokens.
    - Cell typography classes (`.td-symbol`, `.td-ts`, `.td-sub`, `.td-dim`, `.mono`, badges) keep working unchanged — TableRow only owns row-level state structure. Same "compose on existing utility classes" principle Card established (Task 122).
  Migrations:
    - **Position History** (`closed_positions_table.html`): `<tr style="cursor:pointer;" onclick="togglePosRow({{ r.id }})">` → `{{ tr_open(on_click="togglePosRow(" ~ r.id ~ ")") }}`. on_click implies clickable, so the explicit `style="cursor:pointer"` lives in CSS now via `.tr-p-clickable`.
    - **Order History** (`order_history_table.html`): bare `<tr>` → `{{ tr_open() }}`. Static rows, no on_click. Old bare `<tr>` inside the for-loop body removed; the `<thead><tr>` header row keeps its bare `<tr>` since it's a column header, not a data row.
    - **Trade History / fills** (`fills_table.html`): bare `<tr>` → `{{ tr_open() }}` plus the **SIDE column split** (calibration #3). Pre-fix: single compound cell `<td class="{{ side_class }}">{{ side_label }}</td>` where `side_label = "Close LONG"` / `"Open SHORT"` etc. Post-fix: two columns, `<td class="{{ 'pos-short' if r.is_close else 'pos-long' }}">{{ 'Close' if r.is_close else 'Open' }}</td>` followed by `<td class="{{ 'pos-long' if r.direction == 'LONG' else 'pos-short' }}">{{ r.direction }}</td>`. ACTION column header `<th>Action</th>` added. Action coloring: Open → green (entering exposure), Close → red (exiting). SIDE coloring: LONG → green, SHORT → red — matches Position History exactly.
  Convention doc: `templates/primitives/README.md` adds TableRow section + the open/close pattern alongside Card's `{% call %}` pattern. Decision tree codified:
    - Body is one contiguous block (Card) → `{% call %}` + `caller()`.
    - Body is a list of sibling elements (TableRow) → open/close pair.
    - Body is parameter-driven stateless (StatusIndicator) → plain macro.
  Tests (`tests/test_task123_table_row.py`, 29 cases):
    - TestTableRowRendering (8) — default open, clickable state, on_click ergonomic shortcut, id presence/absence, on_click absence, extra_class append, close returns just `</tr>`.
    - TestTableRowCssInBase (1) — `.tr-p` / `.tr-p-clickable` / `.tr-p-selected` rules present + FE-MED-002 anchor.
    - TestPositionHistoryMigration (3) — primitive import pin, `tr_open(on_click=` usage, anti-revert against bare `<tr style="cursor:pointer;">`, end-to-end render confirms `class="tr-p tr-p-clickable"` + `onclick="togglePosRow(1)"`.
    - TestOrderHistoryMigration (4) — primitive import, default `tr_open()`, anti-revert (no bare `<tr>` in loop body), render confirms `class="tr-p"` without clickable.
    - TestTradeHistoryMigrationAndColumnSplit (4) — primitive import, anti-revert (`side_label = "Close "` / `"Open "` gone), `<th>Action</th>` header present, render confirms `>Open<` / `>Close<` / `>LONG<` as single-coloured cells AND old `Open LONG` / `Close LONG` compound labels absent.
    - TestReadmeDocumentsTableRow (3) — README has Task 123 section, open/close pattern documented, decision tree present.
    - TestTemplateCompiles (6) — explicit MED-047 pins for TableRow macro + all 3 migrated tables + sibling primitives (StatusIndicator, Card) anti-regression.
  FBF: Stashing `base.html` + all 3 migrated templates → 12 of 29 tests fail (4 migration pins per table = 12). The 17 passes exercise the primitive directly + README + compile pins (independent of base.html / migration sites). Restoring → 29/29 pass. Full suite: 1673 passed, 6 skipped, 25 s — no regressions.
  Out-of-scope items considered:
    - ~~`position_fills.html` (Position History expansion sub-table) has the same compound `"Open " ~ dir` / `"Close " ~ dir` pattern. Not migrated in this task — it's an inline expansion sub-table, smaller in operational visibility than the 3 primary tables. Flagged as latent for opportunistic future migration if it surfaces or if a future task touches that fragment.~~ **Migrated in Task 129** (TableRow + ACTION/SIDE split + EmptyState with `padding="tight"`). The Task-123 calibration #3 closure is now complete across both primary and inline sub-table consumers.
    - Other tables (Pre-Trade Log, Events Log, Open Orders, Open Positions, Exchange Trade History) all have `<tr>` markup that could adopt TableRow. Not migrated; defer per task spec out-of-scope.
  Latent observations for EmptyState (next primitive):
    - **Three of the migrated tables share an empty-state pattern**: each ends with `{% else %}<div style="color:var(--muted);padding:20px 0;text-align:center;font-size:.78rem;">No <X> found for this period.</div>` — duplicated styling, message-only customization. Natural EmptyState consumer: `{% empty %}{{ empty_state(message="No closed positions found for this period.") }}` or similar. The audit's FE-MED-001 + FE-LOW-006 may both touch on empty-card patterns; EmptyState resolves the shared shape.
    - The 3 messages vary only by data type ("No closed positions" / "No order history" / "No trade history"). EmptyState's API needs a message slot but no other configurability — minimal primitive. Probably plain macro (like StatusIndicator), not call/caller or open/close.
    - **Icon support**: EmptyState in other apps often pairs with an icon. The codebase already has SVG-inline icons (the standalone-banner triangle in base.html). EmptyState could accept an `icon` param taking either an SVG string or a named glyph; defer until a consumer needs it (matches Card's "defer open/close pair" reasoning).
    - **Empty-state vs full-card-empty (FE-LOW-006)**: there are two different empty patterns. (a) Empty *inside* a card (the table-rows-empty case) — what EmptyState handles. (b) Card that's *entirely* empty when its parent feature is unused (FE-LOW-006 Backtest sessions) — that's a page-layout decision, not a primitive. EmptyState handles (a) only.
    - **Constraint surfaced by TableRow's open/close**: when EmptyState is rendered inside a Card or replacing a table body, callers need to position it without the table's `<table>` structure. EmptyState's wrapper element (probably a `<div>`) shouldn't carry table semantics. Worth noting before designing the API.

### [FE-MED-003] [UX] Trade Events Log shows duplicate consecutive timestamps for the same position
  Description: Trade Events Log has 6 entries at `2026-05-18 10:41:32`, all BTCUSDT, all `position_closed`, same `order_manager` source. Followed by 4 more at `10:40:40` and another batch at `10:19:08`.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_4027esh0w.
  Impact: Either real (one position closed in multiple fill events, each emitting a closure event — over-emitting) or buggy (duplicate event emission). Either way the log is hard to read.
  Recommended fix: If these are legitimately multiple fills closing one position, group them visually under one parent row (chevron expand, like Position History does). If they're duplicates, fix the emission. Either way the user shouldn't see N near-identical rows for one logical event.
  Effort: Medium.
  Status: OPEN

### [FE-MED-004] [Data] Trade Events Log: CALC ID always "—"
  Description: Every visible entry in Trade Events Log has CALC ID = "—". The whole point of the v2.4 link-window work (HIGH-027) is to associate fills with their originating calculator session via `calc_id`. The Events Log is the natural place to verify that link is working — and it's empty for every row.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_4027esh0w (rows 1–18 all show `—` in CALC ID column).
  Impact (revised): Audit-01's "the link feature doesn't work" framing was driven by the same BTCUSDT pollution as FE-HIGH-003 / FE-MED-017. Operator confirms (2026-05-18) those rows are synthetic test artifacts with no calc backing — by construction they have CALC ID = `—`. There may still be a real concern (no live calc-backed events present for visual confirmation of the linkage), but the empty CALC ID column is NOT a bug per se.
  Recommended fix (revised): Pair with FE-MED-017's remediation. Once the test-pollution rows are cleaned and at least one calc-backed event lands in the log (operator runs a calc → fill → closure), this column should self-validate. The structural fix is the UI filter from FE-MED-017 option 3 (`event_origin` + filter): synthetic events hidden by default, the CALC ID column then meaningfully shows real link state.
  Effort: Small (absorbed by FE-MED-017's UI filter work).
  Status: SCOPE-CONFIRMED 2026-05-18 (Task 110) — not a bug in isolation; collapses into FE-MED-017's remediation. Stays OPEN as a tracker for "verify CALC ID populates correctly after pollution cleanup."
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-017.

### [FE-MED-005] [UX] Calculator "Recent" card shows time-only timestamps, no date
  Description: The Recent calculations card on `/calculator` shows entries with timestamps `16:07` and `22:37` — time only, no date. If the entries are from yesterday or earlier, the user has no way to know.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_58390jsag (Recent card top of Calculator page).
  Impact: Ambiguity. Time-only formatting works if the assumption is "always today", but the Pre-Trade Log on History shows `2026-05-18 09:07:36` — full date+time. Inconsistent.
  Recommended fix (updated 2026-05-18 per operator decision): Relative format for entries < 24 h old (`2h ago`, `Yesterday 22:37`), absolute format for older entries (`2026-05-15 22:37`). Drop time-only display entirely. Audit-01 Open Question #2 ANSWERED.
  Effort: Small.
  Screenshots: ss_58390jsag
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 112.
  Implementation note: `templates/calculator.html` — `rec.ts` now stores `now.getTime()` (epoch ms) rather than the prior `HH:MM` string. New JS helper `formatCalcTs(ts)` renders four buckets: < 60 s → `Just now`; < 1 h → `Nm ago`; < 24 h → `Nh ago`; 24-48 h → `Yesterday HH:MM`; older → `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`. Backward-compat branch (`typeof ts === 'string' → return ts`) keeps pre-Task-112 localStorage entries renderable as-is rather than crashing to `undefined`. The render path now calls `formatCalcTs(h.ts)` instead of concatenating `h.ts` directly.
  Tests (`tests/test_task112_calculator_ux.py::TestRecentCardTimestampRelative`, 4 cases): ts stored as epoch ms (anti-revert against the `HH:MM` shape); helper has all four format buckets; legacy-string backward-compat branch present; render-path invokes the helper, not raw `h.ts`.

### [FE-MED-006] [Design] Multiple empty-state treatments — calibration #5 root cause
  Description: 7 distinct empty-state treatments across the app: centered "No open positions.", centered "No sessions yet.", placeholder "Select an account...", inline "Not yet backfilled — use Backfill tab" (with different layouts for BTC Market Cap card vs Aggregate OI Change card), "—" filler in calculator preview, "awaiting calculation" right-side text on calculator. No consistent visual treatment.
  Evidence: Screenshots: ss_11017eioe (No open positions), ss_4567eh8la (No sessions yet), ss_6052aajhr (Select an account), ss_15579nr60 (Not yet backfilled — two cards with different layouts).
  Impact: Each empty state had to be invented by the page author. Some are useful (call to action: "use Backfill tab"), some are stub text (Select an account...). User-facing: small frustration. Engineering: more cost to maintain.
  Recommended fix: EmptyState primitive with slots for icon / message / action button / tone (informational vs actionable vs error).
  Effort: Medium.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 124 — Bundle A.4. EmptyState primitive built; 9 template-side sites + 1 JS-side site migrated.
  Implementation note:
    - Built `templates/primitives/empty_state.html` as Bundle A's second plain-macro primitive (StatusIndicator was first). API: `empty_state(message, tone="info"|"action", action_label="", action_url="", action_attrs="")`. Defensive bogus-tone fallthrough to `info`, parallel to StatusIndicator's bogus-severity handling.
    - Two tones: `info` (muted grey — the 90% case) and `action` (slightly brighter, paired with CTA button). The audit's "error" third tone deferred until a real consumer surfaces (matches Card's "defer open/close pair" reasoning).
    - Action button shape: `<a class="es-action" href=...>` when `action_url` is set; `<button class="es-action">` when only `action_label` + `action_attrs` are set (HTMX shape). Caller-provided `action_attrs` rendered via `| safe` — caller is responsible for escaping.
    - Wrapper semantics constraint (load-bearing — documented in README): EmptyState renders as `<div class="es es-{tone}">`, **NOT safe inside `<tbody>`** because browsers drop non-`<tr>` children of `<tbody>`. Use in the parent container's empty branch (the `{% else %}` of the row loop's `{% if rows %}` block, OUTSIDE the `<table>`), or inside a Card body slot, or as a standalone block.
    - CSS in base.html primitives section: flex column, center-aligned, 20px vertical padding (matches the dominant 20px shape in the inventory), `.es-msg` styled per tone, button has hover blue tint matching existing chrome accent.
  Migrations (10 sites total):
    - Position History (`closed_positions_table.html`): `<div style="color:var(--muted);padding:20px 0;...">No closed positions found...</div>` → `{{ empty_state(message="No closed positions found for this period.") }}`.
    - Order History (`order_history_table.html`): same shape; "No order history found for this period."
    - Trade History / fills (`fills_table.html`): "No trade history found for this period."
    - Pre-Trade Log (`pre_trade_table.html`): "No pre-trade entries found for this period."
    - Trade Events Log (`trade_events_table.html`): "No trade events found for this period."
    - Dashboard open positions (`dashboard_body.html`): "No open positions." (was 24px padding — normalized to 20px via primitive).
    - Analytics pairs_table: "No closed trades found for this period."
    - Analytics beta_exposure: "No open positions."
    - Analytics funding_tracker: "No open positions — no funding exposure."
    - Regime JS not-backfilled (`regime.html:472`): `chartEl.innerHTML = '<div class="es es-action" style="height:100%;justify-content:center;padding:0;"><div class="es-msg">Not yet backfilled</div><a class="es-action" href="#backfill" onclick="showPanel(\'backfill\');return false;">Use Backfill tab</a></div>'`. JS reuses the primitive's CSS classes — the primitive becomes the shared visual language across server- and client-rendered empty states.
  Out-of-scope items considered:
    - `config.html` "Select an account..." — placeholder before auto-load runs; auto-load was wired in Task 108. Not an empty state — just a brief initial-state placeholder.
    - `backtest.html` "No jobs yet" + "No sessions yet" — page-layout question (FE-LOW-006: right half empty when no sessions). Defer.
    - `calculator.html` "awaiting calculation" — different semantic; a value is reserved, not absent. Placeholder, not empty state.
    - ~~`position_fills.html` (10px padding), `exchange_table.html` (16px padding), `equity_ohlc.html` (32px padding) — different padding shapes. Defer for opportunistic migration when those files are touched for other reasons; primitive locks 20px.~~ **API extended in Task 129** then **fully migrated in Task 130**: `padding="default"` (20 px, dominant), `"tight"` (10 px, inline sub-tables), `"loose"` (32 px, full-page). `position_fills.html` migrated using `padding="tight"` (Task 129). `equity_ohlc.html` migrated using `padding="loose"` (Task 130). `exchange_table.html` migrated using default `padding="default"` (Task 130) — the prior 16 px was a single-consumer outlier; accepting the 4 px shift + left→center alignment to converge with primitive convention rather than adding a `medium` preset for one consumer.
    - Admin views (`admin/shadow_events.html`, `admin/trade_events.html`) — empty state inside `<td colspan="N">` (legitimate for table-row empty marker). Different structural placement; defer.
    - News empty state in `regime.html` (uses `<div class="card">` wrapping pattern) — different shape (full-card-empty vs inline-empty). Could migrate but defer.
  Tests (tests/test_task124_empty_state.py, 46 cases):
    - TestEmptyStateRendering (9) — both tones, bogus-tone fallthrough, HTML escape, no-action button omitted, action with URL → anchor, action without URL → button, HTMX attrs pass through, attrs on anchor when url present.
    - TestEmptyStateCssInBase (1) — CSS block present + FE-MED-006/008 anchor.
    - TestHistoryTableMigrations (11) — import pin + old inline div anti-revert for 5 history tables + render-pin on Position History empty path.
    - TestDashboardMigration (2) — import + old inline div gone.
    - TestAnalyticsMigrations (3) — import pin for 3 analytics fragments.
    - TestRegimeMigration (4) — .es-action class present, .es-info class present, old "Not yet backfilled — use Backfill tab" single-string gone, showPanel('backfill') CTA wired (not the fictional switchTab function).
    - TestReadmeDocumentsEmptyState (4) — README has Task 124 section, both tones documented, wrapper-semantics warning present, decision tree includes EmptyState.
    - TestTemplateCompiles (12) — MED-047 pins for all migrated templates + Bundle A.1/A.2/A.3 anti-regression.
  FBF: Stashing all 11 modified templates → 20 of 46 tests fail (CSS pin + 15 migration pins + 4 Regime pins). The 26 passes exercise the primitive directly + README + compile pins (independent of consumer-site state). Restoring → 46/46 pass. Full suite: 1719 passed, 6 skipped, 25 s — no regressions.
  Latent observations for PeriodSelector (last Bundle A primitive):
    - **EmptyState revealed that primitives can be JS-consumable too**: the Regime not-backfilled migration didn't change the JS structure — it just changed what classes the JS emits into `chartEl.innerHTML`. The primitive's CSS becomes a shared *visual language* even when no template-side macro call exists. PeriodSelector will face the same pattern: chart-card period dropdowns are currently JS-driven (via `loadSignalCard(sig)`); the primitive should provide both a macro for server-rendered cases AND class conventions for JS-rendered cases.
    - **PeriodSelector vs the existing preset-buttons pattern**: History page already has `.preset-btn` class for the date-range presets (Last 90 days / Last 30 days / etc.). PeriodSelector should compose on this OR replace it. If composing, primitive becomes a thin wrapper around existing chrome; if replacing, callers migrate. Audit FE-MED-007 is the relevant finding (each chart card has its own period selector + a global one — precedence ambiguity).
    - **PeriodSelector params likely**: `current` (selected value), `options` (list of period keys: "30d", "90d", "1y", "5y", "All"), `on_change_js` or `hx_get_template` for state transitions. Three of the migrated sites use existing buttons; the primitive consolidates them.
    - **Constraint surfaced by EmptyState's wrapper**: like EmptyState's `<div>` warning (don't use inside `<tbody>`), PeriodSelector will have its own placement constraint — segmented controls inside flex containers vs grid containers behave differently. Worth pinning before API design.
    - **EmptyState's defer-discipline reused**: action tone deferred without action_url to be a button (HTMX shape); error tone deferred until consumer. PeriodSelector should follow the same — minimal API, defer parameters until a real consumer surfaces.

### [FE-MED-008] [Data] Regime page: 2 of 7 macro signal cards are "not backfilled"
  Description: BTC Market Cap card shows `not backfilled` next to title and `Not yet backfilled — use Backfill tab` in the body. Aggregate OI Change shows the same but with different visual layout (the "not backfilled" sits next to "Aggregate OI Change" title differently from the BTC Market Cap version — wraps).
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_15579nr60.
  Impact: Two problems: (1) the feature is incomplete — 2/7 cards don't have data and the user has to know to go to Backfill. (2) The empty-state rendering differs between the two cards.
  Recommended fix: First-class empty state (per FE-MED-006) with consistent "Not backfilled" / "Backfill now" CTA button that takes the user directly to the Backfill tab with that signal pre-selected. Effort split between feature completeness work and the EmptyState primitive work.
  Effort: Medium (mostly feature/data completeness, not UI).
  Status: PARTIALLY RESOLVED in Task 124 — visual treatment normalized via EmptyState primitive (action tone). Feature work (pre-select the signal when navigating to Backfill tab) deferred — audit doc explicitly notes the split: "Effort split between feature completeness work and the EmptyState primitive work."
  Implementation note: Both not-backfilled cards (BTC Market Cap, Aggregate OI Change) now emit the same `.es .es-action` markup via `templates/regime.html:472`. The visual layout inconsistency (different wrap behaviour next to titles) is fixed by the shared primitive structure — centered flex column, identical message + CTA shape. The CTA navigates to the Backfill tab via the existing `showPanel('backfill')` JS function. **Feature gap remaining**: the Backfill tab does not pre-select the relevant signal (operator must scroll the signal list and find the one they came from). This is data/feature work, not UI primitive work — filed for future feature task or operator decision.

### [FE-MED-007] [UX] Regime page: each macro signal card has its own period selector AND there's a global one
  Description: The Macro Signals card on /regime has 7 mini-charts. Each chart card has its own `30d/90d/1y/5y/All` segmented control. The Macro Signals card header also has an `All: 30d/90d/1y/5y/All` control at top-right that presumably sets the period for all charts. Unclear which takes precedence; unclear what happens to individual selections when the global is changed.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_15579nr60.
  Impact: Precedence ambiguity is the worst kind of UX — every interaction makes the user re-learn the model.
  Recommended fix: Either (a) remove the global selector and let each card stand alone, or (b) make the global selector visually clearly "primary" and have each card show "(custom)" when overridden. Document precedence in a tooltip on the global selector.
  Effort: Small.
  Screenshots: ss_15579nr60
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 136 (fully, was PARTIAL after Task 125). Visual normalization closed Task 125; the deferred precedence-rule question was reframed in Task 136 by applying Task 135's orthogonality investigation lens (see FE-MED-018).
  Investigation outcome (Task 136): rejected the audit's "precedence ambiguity" framing. Empirical analysis:
    - Regime page has NO page-level period bar (different shape from Analytics). Sub-tabs (Overview / Backfill / News / Config) switch via client-side `showPanel(name)`; no fragment endpoints, no route-level `period` query consumption. Task 135's `_periodTabs` registry / dim+disable pattern does not apply.
    - The "global" selector is panel-scoped (lives inside the Macro Signals card on Overview only). Backfill / News / Config sub-tabs have no period selectors at all.
    - Both global "All:" and per-card selectors are functional (drive `loadSignalCard()` for each signal). Neither is a dead control.
    - The precedence MODEL already exists in code: `setAllCardRanges(days)` overrides every per-card range; `setCardRange(key, days)` clears the global's active state (visual signal that cards are no longer all-in-sync). This approximates audit recommendation (b).
    - BUT — orthogonality investigation surfaced a Task-125 MIGRATION REGRESSION: the PeriodSelector primitive renamed the button class from `.global-range-btn` to `.preset-btn` (under a new `.global-range-group` wrapper). Two JS callsites still queried the dead `.global-range-btn` selector, so the visual clear-on-override silently stopped working post-migration. Visible effects:
      1. Clicking "All: 30d" then "All: 90d" left BOTH active simultaneously.
      2. Clicking a per-card range did not clear the global's "active" highlight.
  Task 136 fix:
    - Replace `.global-range-btn` with `.global-range-group .preset-btn` in `setCardRange` and `setAllCardRanges` (2 lines).
    - Pass `id="signal-cards-range"` to the PeriodSelector call so the init IIFE can target the wrapper.
    - Init IIFE sets `title=` attribute documenting the precedence model: "Sets the range for all macro signal cards. Clicking a per-card range overrides it for that card only; the All: highlight clears when any card is overridden." (Audit recommendation b — "Document precedence in a tooltip on the global selector.")
    - No shared-utility promotion: Analytics's `_setPeriodBarEnabled` (cross-sub-tab dim/disable) doesn't fit Regime's panel-scoped shape. Keep utility scoped to Analytics — defer-discipline holds.
  Tests (`tests/test_task136_regime_period_precedence.py`, 14 cases):
    - TestNoPageLevelPeriodBar (2) — no Analytics-style page-period-bar id on Regime; no _periodTabs registry (different shape from Analytics).
    - TestSelectorRegressionFix (4) — dead selector removed from executing JS (but allowed in anchor comments), correct selector used in both callsites, setCardRange clears global, setAllCardRanges clears prior globals.
    - TestGlobalSelectorWrapperHasId (1) — period_selector call passes id="signal-cards-range".
    - TestInitSetsTitleAttribute (3) — init IIFE sets `.title`, text mentions all/card/override semantics, FE-MED-007 anchor comment present.
    - TestPrecedenceModelInvariants (3) — anti-regression: setAllCardRanges still iterates SIGNALS + reloads each; setCardRange only reloads the one clicked; both touch signalRanges state.
    - TestTemplateCompiles (1) — MED-047 compile pin.
  FBF: stashing `templates/regime.html` → 9 of 14 tests fail (regression-fix + wrapper-id + init-title-attr). 5 pass: anti-regression on precedence semantics + no-period-tabs-registry (correct — never had one) + compile pin. Restoring → 14/14 pass. Full suite: see below.
  Latent observations from this task:
    - **Post-primitive-migration audit pattern**: When primitive migrations rename classes, ALL JS that queried the old class must be audited. Task 125's PR shipped without checking `setCardRange`/`setAllCardRanges` — silent visual regression. Future primitive-migration PRs should grep for old class names in JS before merging. Adding to MED-047's template-wiring discipline: "Class-rename migrations need a JS-callsite sweep alongside the compile-render test."
    - **Other multi-selector pages worth orthogonality-checking**: Backtest, Calculator. Likely no period selectors of this shape (per Task 125's "out-of-scope sites considered" note — Calculator has no period controls, Analytics covered in T135), but worth a quick sweep on next audit pass if either page gains comparable UX.
    - **Pattern enrichment** for audit-impact-imprecision: orthogonality investigations can surface migration regressions (this task) in addition to dead controls (Task 135). Both are mechanism mismatches with the audit's stated framing. The general lens — investigate orthogonality first when finding describes "precedence ambiguity" — applies regardless of which mechanism the investigation surfaces.
    - **The Task-125 "feature gap remaining" entry (FE-MED-008 Backfill pre-select)** is unrelated to FE-MED-007; the precedence model is fully closed here. FE-MED-008 partial status unchanged.
  Implementation note:
    - Built `templates/primitives/period_selector.html` as Bundle A's third plain-macro primitive (alongside StatusIndicator + EmptyState). Stateless: server renders buttons with `.active` on the current option; JS or HTMX handles transitions.
    - API: `period_selector(options, current, on_change_template, hx_get_template, extra_btn_attrs, label_prefix, id, extra_class)`. Caller passes `options=[{value,label}, ...]` and `current` (matched via string-cast so int/string values both work).
    - Transition modes: `on_change_template` (JS expression with `{value}` interpolated raw — caller controls quoting) and `hx_get_template` (HTMX URL with `{value}` interpolated). Both may coexist (HTMX wins at runtime); caller should pick one.
    - Optional `label_prefix` renders inline `<span class="ps-label">` before buttons (e.g. "All:" for Regime global).
    - Every button emits `data-value="{value}"` for JS targeting via `b.dataset.value` (History's `highlightPreset` updated from `dataset.preset` → `dataset.value`).
    - Composes on existing `.preset-btn` utility class — no new button typography. Net-new: `.ps` wrapper (flex layout + gap) + `.ps-label` (inline prefix styling). Task 124's "compose on existing utilities" discipline applied.
    - **Placement-container decision**: `.ps` wrapper is `display:inline-flex` with `flex-wrap:wrap`. Works inside flex / grid / block parents. Same `<tbody>` warning as EmptyState — don't place inside table-row contexts.
  Migrations (3 reference sites across distinct option-set shapes):
    - **Regime global "All:" selector** (`templates/regime.html:90-100`): 5-option integer-value set (30/90/365/1825/0), `setAllCardRanges({value},this)` handler, `label_prefix="All:"`. Hand-rolled `{% for days, label in [...] %}` loop with inline `.preset-btn` buttons replaced by single `period_selector(...)` call.
    - **History page presets** (`templates/history.html:36-44`): 6-option string-value set ("90d"/"30d"/"15d"/"7d"/"yesterday"/"today"), `setPreset('{value}')` handler. Demonstrates string-value path with single-quoting in the JS template. `highlightPreset` JS updated from `b.dataset.preset` → `b.dataset.value` (commented).
    - **Regime per-card signal selectors** (`templates/regime.html:452`): JS-rendered inside `buildSignalCards`'s template literal — can't call macro from JS. JS emits the same `.ps` wrapper + `.preset-btn` buttons + `data-value` attr the primitive emits. Same shared-visual-language pattern as Task 124's EmptyState Regime migration. 7 chart cards × 5 buttons = 35 buttons all using the primitive's class convention.
  Out-of-scope sites considered:
    - Regime Timeline range picker (`regime.html:76-79`): inside a composite control alongside timeline-style buttons (Swim/Bars/Blocks/Heat/Stack). Composite primitive needed; defer.
    - Analytics period controls: opportunistic migration in a future task.
    - Calculator period controls: none currently present.
  Tests (tests/test_task125_period_selector.py, 33 cases):
    - TestPeriodSelectorRendering (14) — option buttons, active class, unknown-current defensive, int/string cross-match, on_change_template interpolation (string + integer values), hx_get_template, dual-mode emission, extra_btn_attrs, label_prefix, id/extra_class, HTML escape.
    - TestPeriodSelectorCssInBase (1) — CSS block present + FE-MED-007 anchor.
    - TestHistoryMigration (5) — import pin, primitive call, old `data-preset=` gone, JS reads `dataset.value`, end-to-end render with 6 presets + 30d active.
    - TestRegimeGlobalMigration (3) — import pin, old hand-rolled `global-range-btn preset-btn` gone, `label_prefix="All:"` used.
    - TestRegimePerCardJsMigration (2) — JS emits `class="ps"` wrapper, `data-value="${d}"` attr.
    - TestReadmeDocumentsPeriodSelector (3) — README section, decision tree mentions PeriodSelector, both transition modes documented.
    - TestTemplateCompiles (5) — MED-047 pins for primitive + 3 migrated templates + Bundle A.1/A.2/A.3/A.4 anti-regression.
  FBF: Stashing `base.html` + `history.html` + `regime.html` → 11 of 33 fail (CSS pin + 5 History + 3 Regime global + 2 Regime per-card). 22 pass: primitive direct + README + compile pins. Restoring → 33/33 pass. Full suite: 1752 passed, 6 skipped, 37 s — no regressions.
  Latent observations — Bundle A close shape + composite-primitive needs:
    - **All 5 Bundle A primitives now landed**: StatusIndicator (T121), Card (T122), TableRow (T123), EmptyState (T124), PeriodSelector (T125). Decision-tree complete: contiguous body → call/caller; sibling list → open/close; parameter-driven → plain macro. Primitives README is the authoritative convention doc.
    - **Bundle A close candidate: v2.4.3 tag**. The 5 primitives + 5 audit findings resolved (FE-HIGH-001, FE-MED-001, FE-MED-002, FE-MED-006, FE-MED-007 + partial FE-MED-008) close a coherent UX-primitive chapter. Natural shape: tag after this task, then Bundle B remnants / audit-02 takes over.
    - **Composite-primitive needs surfaced across the bundle (worth filing as new findings or tracking as latent)**:
      - **WsStatus composite** (flagged Task 121): `ws_status.html` fragment's multi-field display (latency + age + clock offset + connection state) is a composite of StatusIndicator + numeric readouts. Future task could build a `Stat` or `MetricRow` primitive and compose.
      - **Regime Timeline mode+period combo** (flagged this task): the Timeline section has Swim/Bars/Blocks/Heat/Stack toggle alongside the period picker. Two segmented controls that should visually coordinate. Future task either: (a) treat as two separate primitives in a flex container (current shape, just visually consistent now via PeriodSelector + the existing button shape); or (b) build a SegmentedToolbar composite primitive. (a) is simpler; defer (b) until a third consumer of the pattern appears.
      - **Card with action-cell header** (flagged Task 122): Card primitive currently has title + subtitle + body + footer. Some Card consumers (e.g., Backtest sessions card, Analytics period-selector-in-card) want action buttons in the header right corner. Card currently supports this only through footer or by embedding the action in the body via caller(). A `header_actions` slot or open/close pair is the natural follow-up. No finding filed — defer until a real consumer surfaces.
      - **Form-field cluster primitive** (newly observed): Config / Add Account modal has hand-rolled `<label> + <input>` clusters repeated ~15-20 times. Out of Bundle A scope but visible pattern. Could be Bundle G or a separate Bundle F.
    - **Migrations remaining (opportunistic, not Bundle A blockers)**: Analytics period selectors, equity_ohlc empty state (32px padding variant), position_fills/exchange_table empty states (different paddings), config.html account-name truncation, etc. None block Bundle A close — they're scattered MED/LOW cleanup for a future bundle.
    - **No new HIGH findings surfaced during Bundle A**. Top 2 has held stable since Task 121 (HIGH-001 auth Phase 8, HIGH-002 coupling Phase 6).

### [FE-MED-009] [UX] Dashboard equity curve y-axis tops at $180 but actual equity is $83
  Description: The Equity Curve OHLC chart on Dashboard has a y-axis ranging 0 to ~$180. Actual equity is $83. The lower half of the chart contains all the data; the upper half is empty whitespace. Hover and zoom controls on the chart suggest it's a real chart library; the autoscaling either isn't running or is hard-coded to a wider range.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_5224rm25o.
  Impact: Half the chart's vertical real estate is wasted. On a small account (live $83), small percentage moves should be visible; the current scale flattens them.
  Recommended fix: Auto-scale y-axis to the data range, with a small headroom (5-10%). Add a "Fit to data" / "Fixed scale" toggle if you want manual control.
  Effort: Small.
  Screenshots: ss_5224rm25o
  Status: OPEN

### [FE-MED-010] [UI] Dashboard "0/10.0" badge in positions area is unlabeled
  Description: In the Positions section of the Dashboard, top right shows `0/10.0`. From context auditor inferred "open positions / max positions" — but there's no label.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_5224rm25o (top-right of positions panel).
  Impact: Unlabeled progress metrics force the user to learn what each one means. Trivially fixable.
  Recommended fix: Either add a label ("Positions 0/10") or a tooltip on hover explaining the metric.
  Effort: Trivial.
  Screenshots: ss_5224rm25o
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 131 — `title="Open positions / max positions"` tooltip added to both capacity-indicator sites (`templates/fragments/dashboard_body.html` line 98, `templates/fragments/dashboard_positions.html` line 25). Tooltip is the least-invasive disambiguation: layout unchanged, indicator sits next to the Positions tab which provides spatial context.

### [FE-MED-011] [UI] Calculator labels use identifier-style with underscores
  Description: In the Calculator's right-side preview panel, several field labels are rendered with underscores instead of spaces: `_SIZE (CONTRACTS)`, `NOTIONAL / EST_SIZE (USDT)`. These look like raw code identifiers leaked into the UI.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_58390jsag (right column).
  Impact: Looks unfinished. Especially `_SIZE` (leading underscore is jarring) and `EST_SIZE` (should probably be `Est. Size` in display).
  Recommended fix: Translate identifier names to title-cased human strings in the template layer. Maintain a label-mapping dict if needed for unit suffix variants.
  Effort: Small.
  Screenshots: ss_58390jsag
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 112.
  Implementation note: `templates/fragments/calc_result.html` — two label renames at the template layer (underlying variables `c.size` / `c.notional` unchanged):
    - Line ~148: `_size (contracts)` → `Size (Contracts)`.
    - Line ~163: `Notional / est_size (USDT)` → `Notional / Est. Size (USDT)`.
  No label-mapping dict introduced — the two sites are static text, not derived from a code-identifier-to-display-name translation. Direct rewrite is simpler.
  Tests (`tests/test_task112_calculator_ux.py::TestCalcResultLabelsTitleCase`, 2 cases): render asserts title-case present + underscore-form absent for each label.

### [FE-MED-012] [UI] Calculator fee/depth labels are unclear
  Description: Calculator preview shows `1% DEPTH (USDT)` and `FEE (2X)` without explanation. 1% depth probably means orderbook depth 1% from mid; 2X probably means round-trip fee (entry + exit). Neither is obvious without insider knowledge.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_58390jsag.
  Impact: Confuses new users; even experienced users have to mentally translate.
  Recommended fix: Either tooltip-on-hover with explanation, or rename: `1% Depth from Mid`, `Round-trip Fee` / `Fee (Entry + Exit)`.
  Effort: Small.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 112.
  Approach decision: RENAME over tooltip-on-hover. Reasoning: no JS dependency (works under no-script / screen-reader / keyboard navigation), no hover-discovery requirement (the disambiguation is visible at-a-glance), and consistent with the rest of the calc_result.html label style. Tooltips deferred to a separate Bundle E follow-up if the operator wants progressive-disclosure UX later.
  Implementation note: `templates/fragments/calc_result.html` — two label renames:
    - Line ~123: `1% Depth (USDT)` → `1% Depth from Mid (USDT)` (clarifies the reference point — operators unfamiliar with orderbook semantics no longer have to guess what "1%" measures).
    - Line ~181: `{Taker|Maker} Fee (2×)` → `{Taker|Maker} Fee — Round-trip (Entry + Exit)` (removes the "2× = leverage?" misread; round-trip semantics now explicit).
  Tests (`tests/test_task112_calculator_ux.py::TestCalcResultLabelsDisambiguated`, 3 cases): depth label clarifies reference point; fee label clarifies round-trip semantics (covers both Maker and Taker branches via order_type variation); anti-revert pin against the old `1% Depth (USDT)` and `Fee (2×)` strings.

### [FE-MED-013] [Data] Analytics: Performance Ratios card has partial coverage
  Description: SHARPE = 2.18, SHARPE (MFE) = 9.55, SORTINO = 2.48, SORTINO (MAE) = —, PROFIT FACTOR = —, EXPECTANCY (R) = —. Three of six metrics empty.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_42238ocm7.
  Impact: User sees a card with mostly missing data. Either the metrics aren't implemented yet or they require data the engine doesn't have. Also: `SHARPE (MFE) = 9.55` is an unusually high Sharpe value — worth a tooltip explaining what's being measured because it'll otherwise look like a bug.
  Recommended fix: Implement the three missing metrics OR remove them from the UI until implemented. Don't ship empty cells.
  Effort: Medium (depends on metric complexity).
  Screenshots: ss_42238ocm7
  Status: OPEN

### [FE-MED-014] [UX] Configuration → Accounts: account card preview is empty until clicked, then it stays empty (see FE-CRIT-001)
  Description: Even ignoring the 500 error, the right-side detail pane defaults to "Select an account..." text while there's clearly only one account in the system. On a single-account install, the detail panel should auto-load on page enter.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_6052aajhr.
  Impact: Extra click for a 1-account user.
  Recommended fix: Auto-select the single account on page load, or auto-select the active account. The "Select an account..." placeholder only makes sense when there are >1 accounts and none are selected.
  Effort: Trivial.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 108 (v2.4.1.1 hotfix) — collapsed by FE-CRIT-001 fix. `templates/config.html:38-43` already had `hx-trigger="load"` on `#account-detail` against `/fragments/account-detail/{{ active_account_id }}` — the auto-load was wired correctly; the underlying 500 prevented it from rendering. Once FE-CRIT-001's syntax error was fixed, the auto-load behaves as designed. No template change needed in Task 108. Pinned by `TestConfigAutoLoadWiring::test_config_template_has_hx_trigger_load_on_detail_panel`.

### [FE-MED-015] [UX] HTMX fragment 4xx / 5xx responses fail silently without user surface
  Description: HTMX swap targets fail silently when the server returns an error status — the swap doesn't happen but no error appears to the user. Surfaced during the FE-CRIT-001 investigation: the underlying TemplateSyntaxError → 500 was invisible to the operator because htmx aborted the swap and gave no UI signal. Audit-01 surfaced this pattern in its FE-CRIT-001 recommended fix #2 ("add htmx:responseError handler").
  Evidence: FE-CRIT-001 itself is the audit-01 evidence — a release-blocker that hid for a full audit pass + a release tagging because the failure mode was silent.
  Impact: Any future HTTP-error path through HTMX has the same silent-abort behaviour. Operators see "click did nothing" with no diagnostic trail; developers don't get bug reports because the user can't even articulate the problem.
  Recommended fix: Global `htmx:responseError` + `htmx:sendError` listeners on `document.body` rendering a fixed-position toast for ~5 s with status + path. Logs full detail to console for developer access. Minimal — no notification library; the goal is "user knows it failed", not fancy UX.
  Effort: Small. ~50 LOC in base.html (style + element + listener).
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 108 (v2.4.1.1 hotfix) — added to `templates/base.html` immediately after the existing ECharts htmx-lifecycle handler. Pinned by `TestHtmxErrorHandler` (3 cases: response-error listener, send-error listener, toast element with `visible` class).
  Filed-during: Task 108.

### [FE-MED-018] [Architecture] Analytics → Equity Curve: page-level + chart-panel period selectors with undocumented precedence
  Description: Audit-02 Session A surfaced a second consumer of the period-selector duplication pattern (parent finding FE-MED-007 on Regime). On Analytics → Equity Curve, two distinct period selectors are visible:
    - **Page-level header** (visible across all Analytics sub-tabs): `< May 2026 >` then `Month / Week / Quarter / Year / 30D / 90D / All`.
    - **Equity Curve panel header** (inside the chart card): `1W / 2W / 1M / 3M / 6M / 1Y / All`, plus "Log scale" and "Drawdown %" checkboxes.
  Two control sets share semantic intent (pick a time range) but offer different options. Interaction is undocumented: does the chart-level selector override the page-level? Does selecting "1Y" in the chart panel sync the page header back? Neither is obvious.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session A screenshot ss_5579nji3m (Equity Curve sub-tab with both selectors visible at top).
  Impact: Identical to FE-MED-007 on Regime — precedence ambiguity, the worst kind of UX. Every interaction makes the user re-learn the model. With FE-MED-007 partially-resolved (PeriodSelector primitive built Task 125, visual normalization done) and FE-MED-018 now filed, the unresolved portion of both findings shares the same operator-UX question: page-level filters vs panel-level zooms, and how they coordinate.
  Recommended fix: Migrate both selectors to PeriodSelector primitive (Task 125 visual-normalization piece is straightforward — same as Regime global + History migrations). Then decide the precedence rule:
    1. Page-level filters the data set (date range); chart-level zooms within the filtered range.
    2. Chart-level overrides while overridden; page-level becomes inactive + shows "(custom)" or similar.
    3. They sync bidirectionally (most expensive, most magical).
  Operator-UX decision; visual primitive doesn't pick. Same precedence-rule question as FE-MED-007.
  Audit-text note: Session A originally numbered this `FE-MED-017`, which clashed with the existing FE-MED-017 (Trade Events BTCUSDT test pollution, reframed in Task 110). Renumbered to FE-MED-018 during Task 127 reconciliation.
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-007 (Regime — same root cause, different site). Task 125 (PeriodSelector primitive) — visual normalization solution; the precedence-rule UX work remains shared between FE-MED-007 and FE-MED-018.
  Effort: Small (migrate to PeriodSelector + apply operator's precedence-rule decision once made).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A.
  Filed-during: Task 127.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 135 — but **NOT** via the recommended precedence-rule path. Investigation reframed the underlying problem.
  Investigation outcome (audit-impact-imprecision — 6th example): the audit framed this as "precedence ambiguity between two selectors that share semantic intent." Source inspection of `templates/analytics.html` + `api/routes_analytics.py` revealed the actual root cause: **the page-level period selector is a DEAD CONTROL on the Equity Curve sub-tab** (and 3 others — Calendar, Funding, Beta). The handler `frag_analytics_equity` (line 188) takes only `tf`, `log`, `dd` query params — it **ignores `period` entirely**. Clicking Month / Week / Quarter / Year / 30D / 90D / All on Equity Curve has zero effect on the rendered data. No precedence rule exists because there's no actual conflict — one of the two selectors silently does nothing.
  Verified empirically:
    - Handlers that read `period`: overview, pairs, excursions, r_multiples, var (5 sub-tabs).
    - Handlers that ignore `period`: equity, calendar, funding (live), beta (4 sub-tabs).
  Picking a precedence rule (the task spec's models A/B/C) would have invented a contract for the page-level period to affect Equity Curve data — substantial design work with composition unknowns ("how does page-level 'monthly' compose with tf='1W'?"). Smaller correct fix: reveal the orthogonality.
  Fix shape — **Model D** (newly surfaced; rejected the audit's A/B/C framing): dim + disable the page-level period selector on sub-tabs whose handler doesn't consume `period`. Operator sees the selector is inactive → understands it doesn't apply here. Future operator decision can still choose to wire `period` into more handlers later (additive); current minimal fix prevents the confusion without inventing new semantics.
  Implementation note:
    - `templates/analytics.html`: wrapped the page-level period selector in `<div id="analytics-period-bar">` so JS can toggle disabled-state on it as a unit.
    - New JS const `_periodTabs = { overview:1, pairs:1, excursions:1, rmultiples:1, risk:1 }` — the 5 sub-tabs that consume `period` server-side. Empirically derived; enforced by a test that grep's `frag_analytics_*` signatures in `api/routes_analytics.py` and asserts the registry matches.
    - New JS helper `_setPeriodBarEnabled(enabled)` flips `opacity` (1 vs .4), `pointerEvents` (default vs 'none'), and each button's `disabled` property. Mirror-to-attribute means keyboard focus + screen readers also see the inactive state.
    - `title` attribute on the wrapper communicates WHY it's inactive: "Period filter doesn't apply to this sub-tab. Use the chart's own period buttons." Operator learns the model on hover without external docs.
    - `switchTab(tid)` now calls `_setPeriodBarEnabled(!!_periodTabs[tid])` after the tab-active class toggle.
    - Init IIFE also calls `_setPeriodBarEnabled` so the starting state matches (initial tab is 'overview' which DOES consume period — bar starts enabled).
  Tests (`tests/test_task135_analytics_period_bar.py`, 15 cases):
    - TestPeriodBarWrapper (2) — wrapper id present, FE-MED-018 anchor present.
    - TestPeriodTabsRegistry (2) — registry contains all 5 consuming sub-tabs; the 4 dead-control sub-tabs are absent (regex word-boundary check to avoid false matches).
    - TestDisableHelper (5) — helper defined; toggles opacity, pointerEvents, button.disabled, and title attribute.
    - TestSwitchTabHook (2) — switchTab calls helper with registry lookup; init IIFE also calls.
    - TestRegistryMatchesHandlerSignatures (1) — load-bearing drift detector: parses `api/routes_analytics.py` handler signatures and asserts the JS `_periodTabs` registry matches which handlers actually accept `period:` as a parameter. Future drift (adding/removing `period` from a handler without updating the registry) is caught.
    - TestAntiRegression (2) — `setPeriod()` and `loadTab()` unchanged; existing operator workflow on sub-tabs that DO consume period is preserved.
    - TestTemplateCompiles (1) — MED-047 compile pin.
  FBF: stashing `templates/analytics.html` → 12 of 15 tests fail (registry + helper + hooks + drift pin). 3 pass: anti-regression on setPeriod/loadTab (unchanged) + compile pin. Restoring → 15/15 pass. Full suite: 1852 passed, 6 skipped — no regressions.
  Cross-reference update: FE-MED-007 (Regime parent finding) and FE-MED-018 (this finding) were assumed to share a single operator-UX precedence-rule question. Task 135's investigation invalidates that assumption for the Analytics case — Equity Curve's apparent "duplication" is actually orthogonality (dead control on one side). The Regime case (FE-MED-007) may have the same shape underneath; worth re-investigating with the same lens during audit-02 Session B/C or as a follow-up task. **Recording as a new latent observation, not refiling FE-MED-007** — that finding is closed for visual normalization (Task 125); precedence-rule reopen would require operator direction.
  Audit-impact-imprecision pattern note: this is the **6th example**. Audit's "precedence ambiguity" framing was off-mechanism — the actual bug was a dead control, not competing controls. Pattern is now: race-framing FPs (8/15 ≈ 53%), audit-impact-imprecision (6 examples), template wiring compile-test discipline (MED-047), audit-time-artifact (1 example).
  Latent observations from this task:
    - **Other sub-tabs with the same dead-control problem**: Calendar PnL, Funding (live), Beta Exposure. The fix applies uniformly to all of them via the registry — operator gets consistent dim/disable behavior across all dead-control sub-tabs without additional code.
    - **Calendar PnL uses `month` + `all` params** instead of `period`. If a future task migrates Calendar to consume `period`, the registry update is one line. The current dimming is correct for the current handler signature.
    - **Future operator decision** to wire `period` into Equity Curve / Calendar / Funding / Beta would be additive: add the handler param, add the registry entry, the bar enables automatically. No design rework needed.
    - **Regime page (FE-MED-007 parent)** has the same pattern (global selector + per-card selectors). Whether Regime's global selector is also a dead control or actually drives signal data is worth checking — out of scope for this task, but a candidate for FE-MED-007 reopen if confirmed dead-control. Recorded for next audit pass.
    - **Pattern-level observation**: composite "operator-UX precedence" questions in audit findings should be **investigated for dead-control first** before designing precedence semantics. Adding to the audit-impact-imprecision calibration: precedence-framed findings default to investigate-orthogonality-first.

### [FE-MED-019] [UX/Design] htmx 5xx error path doesn't replace target-element content — EmptyState error tone deferred
  Description: Task 108 (v2.4.1.1 hotfix) added global `htmx:responseError` + `htmx:sendError` listeners that render a fixed-position toast for ~5s on every 4xx/5xx response (FE-MED-015 fix). The toast fires correctly. However, the htmx **target element** (panel body, fragment container, etc.) is never updated — its pre-swap "Loading..." text (if present) stays visible after the error. Once the toast auto-dismisses, the user sees a stuck "Loading..." with no signal anything failed.
  Reframe note: originally filed as FE-HIGH-007 in Task 127, driven by the FE-CRIT-002 503 cluster (Analytics sub-tabs). Reframed to FE-MED-019 in Task 128 after FE-CRIT-002 was VERIFIED-FALSE (engine stopped during audit was the actual cause of the 503s, not a real bug). The architectural gap is real (htmx target-element handling on 5xx is incomplete) but there's no real-world failure surface demonstrating it — severity HIGH was not warranted without evidence of a real bug exposing the gap. MED is more accurate: a design discipline concern for future failure paths, not an urgent fix.
  Evidence: Source review of `templates/base.html`'s htmx error handler (Task 108 addition). Handler logs to console + shows toast; does NOT call `htmx.swap` or modify the target element. Any genuine future 5xx on a fragment-load path would produce the stuck-Loading shape.
  Impact: When a real 5xx eventually occurs (server bug, dependency failure, etc.), users will see "Loading..." that never resolves. Operationally similar to the original silent-500 problem FE-MED-015 was supposed to fix, just shifted from "no signal at all" to "brief toast + stuck loading." Less urgent than originally filed (no current failure surface) but still a real gap.
  Recommended fix: Extend the htmx error handler in `templates/base.html` to also replace the target element's content with a visible error state. Two patterns work:
    - **Inline replacement**: swap loading text for `<div class="es es-error">Failed to load (HTTP {status}) · <button class="es-action" onclick="...retry...">Retry</button></div>`. EmptyState primitive (Task 124) has the action-tone shape ready; adding an `error` tone is the natural extension. Task 124 deferred error tone because no consumer existed — FE-MED-019 is that consumer trigger when work is scheduled.
    - **Toast + grayed panel**: keep panel but apply `.htmx-failed` gray-out class while toast shows.
  Bundle A pattern compatibility favors option 1 (EmptyState error tone).
  Effort: Small (one global hook + EmptyState `error` tone class).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A (originally as FE-HIGH-007).
  Filed-during: Task 127 (as FE-HIGH-007).
  Reframed-during: Task 128 (FE-HIGH-007 → FE-MED-019, post-FE-CRIT-002 VERIFIED-FALSE).
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-015 (Task 108 global toast — incomplete fix), FE-CRIT-002 (Task 128 VERIFIED-FALSE).
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 134 — EmptyState `tone="error"` shipped + global htmx error handlers extended to swap target-element content.
  Implementation note: chose **in-place JS swap** path (option 1 from the recommended-fix list). Server-side error templates per endpoint were considered and rejected — would require touching every 5xx-emitting route handler; in-place swap works uniformly for all htmx requests with zero backend changes. The handlers in `templates/base.html` (`htmx:responseError` line ~877, `htmx:sendError` line ~922) now do BOTH:
    1. **Toast** (FE-MED-015, unchanged) — global notification surface, ~5 s fade-out.
    2. **Target swap** (FE-MED-019, this task) — replaces the failed target element's innerHTML with `<div class="es es-error"><div class="es-msg">…</div><button class="es-action" onclick="…retry()">Retry</button></div>`. The JS-built fragment uses the exact same `.es-*` class structure the macro emits (shared visual language, same pattern as Tasks 124 + 125 Regime migrations).
  Retry semantics: each failed target gets a uniquely-named `window._htmxRetry[key]` function (counter-based key to avoid closure-over-loop bugs) wired into the Retry button's `onclick`. The function re-fires `htmx.ajax(verb, path, {target, swap})` with the original request's parameters preserved. **No automatic retry-attempt limits** — a persistent failure simply re-renders the same error state on each click; the user controls retry pace. Avoids infinite loops by being user-triggered, not automatic.
  Coexistence with toast: both handlers (`htmx:responseError` + `htmx:sendError`) call `showError()` (toast — global "something failed") AND `swapTargetWithError()` (target swap — local "this specific element couldn't load"). Different concerns; both apply. Verified by `TestHtmxHandlerTargetSwap::test_response_error_handler_still_calls_show_error_toast` (FE-MED-015 anti-regression pin).
  Operator-facing message is generic: `"Couldn't load this section."` for `htmx:responseError`, `"Network error. Engine offline?"` for `htmx:sendError`. Status code + path stay in the toast + console (developer diagnostic). Generic message because per-status-code messaging would clutter the UI (and many 5xx codes have similar operator implications anyway).
  XSS guard: the message string passes through a `_esc()` helper that escapes `&<>"'` before injection into `target.innerHTML`. Currently message is a constant string (not user input), but the helper is in place for future cases.
  EmptyState extension:
    - Third tone added: `error`. Visual = `--red` palette token (more pronounced than action's `--blue`, less aggressive than the full-bleed `#htmx-error-toast` that fires alongside).
    - `tone` allowed-list expanded from `("info", "action")` to `("info", "action", "error")`; defensive fallthrough to `"info"` on unknown values preserved.
    - CSS additions in `base.html` primitives section: `.es-error .es-msg` (red color), `.es-error a.es-action / button.es-action` (red border + text), red-tinted hover background.
    - `templates/primitives/README.md` updated to document the new tone. Note added: tone vocabulary considered complete; future tone additions follow consumer-driven defer-discipline (same pattern as Card's open/close-pair deferral).
  Tests (`tests/test_task134_htmx_error_target_swap.py`, 18 cases):
    - TestEmptyStateErrorTone (5) — error class application, action-url renders anchor, action-attrs only renders button, defensive fallthrough preserved, info/action tones unchanged.
    - TestEmptyStateErrorCss (2) — `.es-error` CSS present in base.html with `--red` palette token.
    - TestHtmxHandlerTargetSwap (7) — anchor present, response-error still toasts AND swaps, send-error swaps, buildErrorFragment emits `.es-*` classes, installRetry references `window.htmx.ajax` + preserves verb/path/target, `_esc()` HTML-escape helper present (XSS guard).
    - TestReadmeDocsErrorTone (1) — README documents error tone + htmx use case.
    - TestTemplateCompiles (3) — MED-047 pins on empty_state + base + Bundle A.1/A.2/A.3/A.5 sibling anti-regression.
  FBF: stashing `base.html` + `empty_state.html` + README → 9 of 18 tests fail (error-tone + handler-swap + CSS pins). 9 pass: info/action tones unchanged, defensive fallthrough, all compile pins, bundle siblings, README baseline (which already mentioned "htmx" elsewhere). Restoring → 18/18 pass. Full suite: 1837 passed, 6 skipped — no regressions.
  Latent observations during implementation:
    - **Timeout vs 5xx distinction**: htmx exposes `htmx:timeout` event separately from `htmx:responseError`. The current handler doesn't listen for timeout — a stuck request that never returns won't trigger the error path. If a future audit observes "request hangs forever, no error state ever appears," that's the gap. Not filed as a new finding (no current evidence of timeout-without-error in production); recorded here for future reference.
    - **Retry attempt limits**: no current limits. Operator-driven retry is simpler and safer than automatic backoff (avoids hammering a failing backend). A future enhancement could add visual feedback ("Retry attempt 2 of N…") if persistent failures become common, but no current observation justifies it.
    - **Target swap on non-htmx-loaded targets**: if a target element was server-rendered (not htmx-loaded) and an htmx request to refresh it fails, the swap replaces the original server content with an error fragment. The retry re-fires htmx.ajax which would re-render the server content on success. This is correct behavior for fragment-loaded targets but worth flagging if a target is dual-source (rare in this codebase).
    - **EmptyState tone vocabulary now considered complete.** Three tones (info / action / error) cover the documented use cases. Future tone proposals (e.g., `warning`, `success`) should require a concrete consumer pattern before filing — same defer-discipline as Card's open/close pair, composite primitives, etc.
    - **No new findings filed** during this implementation. The htmx error path is now operator-recoverable end-to-end; no adjacent gaps surfaced.
