## 33. Frontend Low

### [FE-LOW-001] [Data] Footer / startup banner says "v2.4" but tag is v2.4.1
  Description: Footer at the bottom of every page reads `QUANTAMENTAL ENGINE v2.4 — Binance USD-M Futures — UTC+7 — <timestamp>`. The release tag is `v2.4.1`. `config.py:8` has `PROJECT_VERSION_ = "v2.4"` unbumped.
  Evidence: Footer text in every screenshot; corroborated by code review (see prior v2.4.1 smoke verification report).
  Impact: Cosmetic.
  Recommended fix: Bump `PROJECT_VERSION_` to `"v2.4.1"` in `config.py`.
  Effort: Trivial.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 108 (v2.4.1.1 hotfix) — `config.py:8` bumped to `"v2.4.1.1"` (one step past the audit-recommended `v2.4.1` to reflect the hotfix release that closes FE-CRIT-001). Pinned by `TestVersionBump::test_project_version_no_longer_stale`.

### [FE-LOW-002] [UI] Account name truncated in card: "Account 1 (Binance Fu..."
  Description: The account card on Config → Accounts truncates "Binance Futures" to "Binance Fu...".
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_6052aajhr.
  Impact: Cosmetic.
  Recommended fix: Either wider card, or smaller font, or auto-truncate at the word boundary ("Binance" not "Binance Fu...").
  Effort: Trivial.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 131 — `templates/fragments/account_list.html` line 13: removed `white-space:nowrap;overflow:hidden;text-overflow:ellipsis;` so long names wrap at word boundaries instead of truncating mid-word. Added `word-break:break-word` to cover the rare case of a single-word name longer than card width. Added `title="{{ acct.name }}"` for hover tooltip showing the full name regardless of wrap state. Card height grows slightly for long names (acceptable trade-off; `.card` is variable-height).

### [FE-LOW-003] [UI] Connections cards have different layouts for Connected vs Not Set states
  Description: Connected provider cards (FRED, Finnhub) are one-row condensed (title + status + masked key + Test/Edit/Remove actions). Not Set provider cards expand to two rows (title + status / API Key input + Save & Test button). The vertical jump between adjacent cards looks unfinished.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_6066tdw9k.
  Impact: Inconsistent rhythm. The Not Set cards aren't bad on their own, but stacked above/below Connected cards the layout is jarring.
  Recommended fix: Unify card height. Either make Connected cards always tall enough to match (room for a hover-reveal input?), or make Not Set cards collapse to a single row with a click-to-expand for the input.
  Effort: Small.
  Status: OPEN

### [FE-LOW-004] [UX] Order History "ORDER ID" column mixes algo and exchange IDs
  Description: ORDER ID column shows a mix of `algo:3000001` (synthetic algo orders) and `504678492` (real Binance exchange IDs). Same column, different formats.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_84879w5e5.
  Impact: Cosmetic / informational. Could matter if the user clicks an ID expecting a specific shape.
  Recommended fix: Either prefix all IDs with origin tag (`algo:` / `bn:`) or split into two columns. Or leave alone — for an internal tool it's fine.
  Effort: Trivial.
  Status: **DEFERRED-PER-AUDIT-01** (Task 131 confirmation). Audit-01 explicitly recommended defer-indefinitely; internal-tool grade. Status word normalized in Task 131's polish-bundle pass — does not count toward active findings.

### [FE-LOW-005] [Data] Connections: provider names mix abbreviation styles
  Description: "Federal Reserve (FRED)" / "Finnhub" / "Binance Market Data (OI/Funding)" / "BWE News (Crypto)" / "CoinGecko". Three have parenthetical category hints, two don't.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_6066tdw9k.
  Impact: Cosmetic.
  Recommended fix: Add category hints to all (e.g., "Finnhub (Equities)") or remove from all.
  Effort: Trivial.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 131 — chose "add hints" path. `core/connections.py:KNOWN_PROVIDERS`: Finnhub → "Finnhub (Equities)"; CoinGecko → "CoinGecko (Crypto)". All 5 entries now have parentheticals. Note: Federal Reserve's `(FRED)` is an abbreviation rather than a category, and the other 3 carry category hints — taxonomy mix is acceptable; not rewriting all 5 to a single convention since FRED is the well-known abbreviation operators search for. Minimal-invasive fix per the audit's framing.

### [FE-LOW-006] [UI] Backtest sessions card occupies right half of page but is empty
  Description: Backtest page is two-column. Form on left (~40% width), Sessions list on right (~60% width). Sessions is empty: "No sessions yet." The right column dominates visual area with nothing to show.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_4567eh8la.
  Impact: Wasted real estate when no sessions exist.
  Recommended fix: When sessions list is empty, give the form full width. When sessions exist, switch to two-column.
  Effort: Small.
  Status: OPEN

### [FE-LOW-007] [UX] Backtest INITIAL EQUITY default is 10000 but live account is $83
  Description: Backtest form INITIAL EQUITY field defaults to `10000`. Live account on the engine is $83.29. There's an argument for either (run sims at meaningful size vs. run sims matched to actual capital), but mismatch is jarring.
  Evidence: Screenshot ss_4567eh8la.
  Impact: User has to remember to change it. Or doesn't, and gets sim results that don't reflect actual position sizing.
  Recommended fix: Default to active account's current equity, or last used backtest's equity. Persist last value.
  Effort: Small.
  Status: OPEN — audit-01 recommends defer indefinitely; legitimate-either-default tradeoff.

### [FE-LOW-008] [Data] Equity Curve panel: "Range" value doesn't match high-minus-low
  Description: Equity Curve panel header day-summary line shows `O $89.30 · H $100.72 · L $79.77 · C $80.50 · Chg +$0.20 (+0.25%) · Range $100.95 (126.55%) · CF +$0.00 · Adj Chg +$0.20 (+0.25%)`. Naive arithmetic: H − L = $100.72 − $79.77 = $20.95, but Range shows $100.95 — off by exactly $80 (about an order of magnitude). The 126.55% also seems implausible for the data range shown on the chart.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session A screenshot ss_5579nji3m.
  Impact: Either Range measures something other than H − L (label is wrong) or the calc has a bug. Either way it's a confusing number on a trader's performance dashboard. At best ambiguous, at worst misleading.
  Recommended fix: Identify what Range is supposed to measure (lifetime drawdown? range-from-initial-equity? range-vs-starting-balance?), then either fix the calc or fix the label. The 126.55% denominator looks like it might be against initial equity rather than the OHLC range itself; if so, the label should say so.
  Effort: Small (calc / label fix once intent is decided).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A.
  Filed-during: Task 127.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 133 — investigation outcome + label disambiguation + defensive atomic-snapshot pattern. No calc change.
  Investigation outcome: the JS calculation `var rng = c.h - c.l` IS mathematically correct (H minus L of the bar). Both render paths in `templates/fragments/equity_ohlc.html` (the inline stats `renderStats` at line ~104, and the chart hover tooltip's `formatter` at line ~228) use the same `c.h - c.l` formula and the same `rngPct = (rng / c.l) * 100` denominator. No backend computation; `var raw = {{ candles | tojson }}` serializes server candles directly, and `api/cache.py:_inject_live_equity` only mutates the LAST candle's c/h/l with current live equity before the JSON is sent.
  Why the audit-observed mismatch is **internally inconsistent within a single render snapshot**: rng=$100.95 / l=$79.77 → 126.55% (matches audit's rngPct), BUT rng=$100.95 cannot come from displayed h=$100.72 minus displayed l=$79.77 (which gives $20.95). For rng to equal $100.95 with l=$79.77, h must have been $180.72 at the moment rng was computed — but the displayed H read $100.72. Two reads of `c.h` in the same render produced different values. Most plausible cause: screenshot-timing artifact during the 1 Hz `QE.startRefresh` (line ~306) — that handler reassigns `raw`'s entries and immediately calls `renderStats`; an in-flight screenshot during the brief refresh window can capture partial-paint state with mixed-snapshot values. Less plausible but possible: an auditor misread of the screenshot. Verified-false would have been the wrong classification — the audit's observation was REAL, just not a calc bug.
  Fix shape (no calc change):
    1. **Label disambiguation**: "Range" → "Bar Range" in both render sites. Anchors the metric to the current bar's H-L explicitly (vs lifetime range, drawdown range, account range, etc.). Operators scanning the stats line now see exactly what the number means.
    2. **Defensive atomic-snapshot pattern**: cache `c.o`, `c.h`, `c.l`, `c.c` into locals (`o`, `h`, `l`, `cc`) at the top of both `renderStats` and the tooltip formatter. All subsequent computation (`rng`, `rngPct`, `chg`, `adjChg`, `chgPct`, `adjChgPct`) AND all HTML rendering (`fmt(o)`, `fmt(h)`, `fmt(l)`, `fmt(cc)`) read from these locals. Forecloses any theoretical between-reads mutation race during the 1 Hz refresh. Makes the rendered O/H/L/C labels guaranteed-consistent with the computed Range/Change/Adj Change. The bug-as-observed becomes literally impossible to reproduce — a screenshot during refresh now either captures the OLD bar fully or the NEW bar fully, never a mix.
    3. **No calc change.** `rng = h - l` is correct; `rngPct = (rng / l) * 100` is correct. The audit's third recommended-fix path ("calc has a bug") was investigated and ruled out.
  Sites changed: `templates/fragments/equity_ohlc.html` — `renderStats` (line 104-145) + tooltip `formatter` (line 228-285). ~25 net LOC including FE-LOW-008 anchor comments.
  Tests (`tests/test_task133_equity_curve_range.py`, 10 cases):
    - TestLabelDisambiguation (3): inline stats uses "Bar Range", tooltip uses "Bar Range", anti-revert on bare ">Range</span>" + "row('Range'," patterns.
    - TestDefensiveLocalCache (3): snapshot line `var o = c.o, h = c.h, l = c.l, cc = c.c;` appears at both sites (count==2); compute block uses locals not direct c.X reads (regex-based check across the compute block with comment-stripping); HTML build uses `fmt(o)` / `fmt(h)` / `fmt(l)` / `fmt(cc)` and not `fmt(c.o)` etc.
    - TestRangeComputationInvariant (3): Python equivalents proving rng=h-l math (audit's expected $20.95 from $100.72-$79.77); audit's reported 126.55% IS consistent with rng=$100.95 / l=$79.77 (proves the rendered values were internally consistent within themselves, just not with the displayed H/L); cross-check showing actual rngPct from displayed H/L would be 26.26%, not 126.55%.
    - TestTemplateCompiles (1): MED-047 compile pin.
  FBF: Stashing `templates/fragments/equity_ohlc.html` → 6 of 10 tests fail (label pins + cache pins). 4 pass: Python math invariants + compile pin (independent of source state). Restoring → 10/10 pass. Full suite: 1819 passed, 6 skipped — no regressions.
  Latent observations:
    - **126.55% denominator note**: the audit hypothesised "might be against initial equity rather than the OHLC range itself; if so, the label should say so." Investigation rules this out — the denominator IS `c.l` (the bar's low), not initial equity. The audit's intuition that 126.55% looks implausible is correct for a normal bar (single bars don't usually move 126% of their low), and that intuition was a legitimate pointer to the underlying timing artifact. Worth recording for future audit calibration.
    - **No other ambiguous metric labels** found during this investigation. The Equity Curve stats line has O/H/L/C (universal OHLC convention), Chg (could be expanded but matches Calculator's Task-112 short-vs-long-label split where mathematical-Δ stays short), and now Bar Range / Cash Flow / Adjusted Change (all explicit post-Tasks 131 + 133). Consistent.
    - **The atomic-snapshot pattern** is now a candidate convention for any future `c`-object-driven render functions across the codebase. Worth flagging in CLAUDE.md if a similar between-reads-mutation observation surfaces elsewhere. Not adding now — single-instance is below the discipline-doc threshold; wait for a second case.

### [FE-LOW-009] [UI] Equity Curve panel: "CF" and "Adj Chg" labels are unlabeled abbreviations
  Description: Same Equity Curve panel header line includes `CF +$0.00` and `Adj Chg +$0.20 (+0.25%)`. "CF" probably means Cash Flow or Carry Forward; "Adj Chg" probably means Adjusted Change. Neither is obvious without insider knowledge.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session A screenshot ss_5579nji3m.
  Impact: Cosmetic / minor cognitive load. Same family as FE-MED-011 / FE-MED-012 (resolved Task 112) — identifier-style labels leaking into UI — but lower-impact because these are acronyms, not snake_case identifiers.
  Recommended fix: Tooltip on hover, OR expand to full labels (`Cash Flow +$0.00`, `Adjusted Change +$0.20`). Tooltip is cheaper and preserves the dense single-line layout; expansion is clearer but takes more horizontal space.
  Effort: Trivial.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A.
  Filed-during: Task 127.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 131 — chose expansion path (matches Task 112's Calculator convention: full title-case labels, no tooltips). `templates/fragments/equity_ohlc.html`: stats line CF → Cash Flow, Adj Chg → Adjusted Change; hover tooltip lines updated to match (Cashflow → Cash Flow for consistency). Stats container is `flex-wrap:wrap` (line 56), so expansion wraps gracefully on narrow viewports.

### [FE-HIGH-008] [Data] Regime Backfill fails at 80% with `'RegimeFetcher' object has no attribute 'close'` AttributeError
  Description: On Regime Backfill, clicking "Start Backfill" with default form (MODE: Macro Only, FROM 01/01/2020, TO 19/05/2026) triggers a real backfill. Progress bar advances. At ~80% it errors with `'RegimeFetcher' object has no attribute 'close'` — surfaced in two places: (1) a red toast top-right, (2) below the form alongside the stuck 80% progress bar.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session B screenshots ss_0653fgbca (toast + inline progress), ss_36678ndsx (Overview check confirms BTC Market Cap / Aggregate OI Change still "Not yet backfilled" after failed run), ss_2630gbf8r (signal coverage table confirms 0 rows / Not backfilled for both).
  Impact: Backfill feature is broken. The two signals the Overview directs users to backfill via "Use Backfill tab" CTAs cannot be backfilled through the UI. Backend AttributeError — likely a missing `__exit__` or `close()` cleanup method on a context manager / resource handler. Blocks the FE-MED-008 follow-up work (Backfill pre-select feature).
  Recommended fix: Backend — add the missing `close()` method to `RegimeFetcher` (or remove the `.close()` call that the caller assumes exists). Frontend — once backend is fixed, existing toast + progress UI behaves correctly. After successful backfill, Overview's two "Use Backfill tab" cards should auto-update; if they don't, that's a follow-up.
  Effort: Small (single missing method or removed cleanup call) + verification pass.
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-031 (error toast wording — this finding's `'RegimeFetcher' object has no attribute 'close'` is one of the raw-exception toast instances).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session B.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 140 — one-line orphan call at `api/routes_regime.py:108`. `await fetcher.close()` was called on a `RegimeFetcher` instance that has no `.close()` method. RegimeFetcher holds only `self._adapter` (caller-owned); each `fetch_*` method uses its own `async with httpx.AsyncClient(...)` context manager. Nothing to clean up. **Fix shape: removed the orphan call** (NOT added a no-op method — that would obscure intent). Anchor comment in call site explains the removal. Tests: 8 cases in `tests/test_task140_regime_backfill_close.py`. FBF: 2/8 fail when fix stashed (source-pin tests); 6 pass (class-shape + AttributeError-reproducibility + create_task-pattern — all unchanged by stashing). Full suite: 1885 passed (+8), 6 skipped — no regressions. Event-bus-pattern investigation (Task 140 step 3): Regime backfill does NOT share Task 139's calculator race pattern. Different shape: `asyncio.create_task(_run())` + shared in-memory `_regime_jobs` dict, registered BEFORE the task fires. Status endpoint reads same dict. No "row not yet written" window. No new race-surface finding to file. Test class `TestBackfillUsesInMemoryJobDictNotEventBus` pins the pattern so a future refactor onto event_bus will trip a re-evaluation.

### [FE-HIGH-009] [UX/Data] Calculator countdown widget shows `✗ PLAN EXPIRED` on freshly-submitted ELIGIBLE calc
  Description: After submitting an eligible calc on the Calculator page, two parts of the right-side result panel show contradictory state:
    - Top banner of the result panel: `✗ PLAN EXPIRED — recalculate to link new fills` (red, terminal state).
    - Just below it, CALCULATED POSITION: `ELIGIBLE` (green badge).
  Reproduced on multiple submissions during the session — both immediately after submission AND ~8 seconds after submission (one full poll cycle past). Banner doesn't update to LINKABLE.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session C screenshots ss_6893mt6em (eligible BNB calc, banner says PLAN EXPIRED), ss_7078h1tr4 (re-submit confirms same state), ss_6259nnfgg (8-second wait, no update).
  Impact: **Critical UX bug for the link-window feature.** The whole point of v2.4.1's HIGH-027 work (Tasks 104a + 104b, 36 passing unit tests) was to give traders a clear signal that fills within the link window get auto-linked. If the countdown UI says EXPIRED for a fresh calc, the trader gets exactly the wrong message — they'd think their plan is already stale and either skip the trade or assume the auto-link won't work. The backend state machine is unit-tested correct; the frontend display layer contradicts it.
  Possible root causes (need backend dive to confirm):
    - Countdown widget rendering with a stale `calc_id` from a previous calc on the page. When a new calc is submitted, the htmx swap updates the calculated-position card but not the countdown widget's `calc_id` reference (different swap targets / hx-include scope).
    - Widget's poll-status endpoint returns EXPIRED for the new calc_id (backend issue — maybe a race where the calc is inserted but the link-window window calculation reads timestamp from a different source).
    - Widget renders with previous calc's calc_id and never re-resolves on subsequent submissions until full page reload.
  Recommended fix: Backend triage first — log the calc_id the widget polls vs. the calc_id from the most-recent calc submission, and confirm whether they match. If they match and EXPIRED is being returned legitimately, the bug is in the time-window computation. If they don't match, the bug is in how the widget's swap target updates on calc submission.
  Effort: Small to investigate; small-to-medium to fix once root cause is known.
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-030 (related widget-semantic issue — countdown tracks "last submitted calc" not "current form"). Strong suspicion these share root cause.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session C.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 139 — confirmed hypothesis 2 (event-bus-publish-vs-htmx-load race, not hypothesis 1 swap-target or hypothesis 3 stale-calc_id). POST `/calculator/calculate` calls `event_bus.publish()` which only enqueues to an asyncio.Queue; the actual `insert_pre_trade_log` runs on a separate task drained by `EventBus.run()`. The response embeds `<div hx-trigger="load,every 5s">` which fires the first poll within microseconds — frequently BEFORE the DB INSERT commits. Route handler's SELECT returns no row → `compute_link_window_status(pretrade_ts_ms=None)` correctly returns EXPIRED (conservative for unknown calc). But EXPIRED template branch is TERMINAL (no `hx-trigger`) — polling stops, widget stuck. Fix: new PENDING state at the route-handler layer. When `pretrade is None`, handler short-circuits with `status="PENDING"` (NOT a call to `compute_link_window_status`). Template gains PENDING branch with `hx-trigger="every 1s"` (fast retry — race window is sub-second). `compute_link_window_status` itself unchanged. Tests: 11 cases in `tests/test_task139_countdown_race.py` (PENDING branch presence + polling + fast cadence + EXPIRED-still-terminal anti-regression + compile-render of all branches + route-handler source-pin). FBF: 8/11 fail when fix stashed; 11/11 pass restored. Full suite: 1877 passed (+11), 6 skipped — 36 prior link-window unit tests still pass. FE-MED-030 auto-resolved by same fix (same root cause).

### [FE-MED-020] [UX] Funding & Beta Exposure show "May 2026" header on LIVE-only views
  Description: Funding and Beta Exposure tabs are inherently "live snapshot" views — Funding shows current funding-rate exposure for open positions (with "Auto-refreshes every 30s" indicator and "FUNDING RATE EXPOSURE — LIVE" card title); Beta Exposure shows current beta-weighted exposure. Neither has a historical/period dimension. Yet the page-level period selector at top still shows `< May 2026 >` with Month / Week / Quarter / Year / 30D / 90D / All buttons. Buttons appear grayed/inactive on these tabs (Task 135's `_periodTabs` registry dim+disable working correctly) but the date string remains, implying period scope the content doesn't have.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session A' screenshots ss_4163jzjjt (Funding "LIVE" card with period header still "May 2026"), ss_5684a1nt5 (Beta Exposure same pattern).
  Impact: User sees a "May 2026" framing for a card showing current state. The disabled buttons are a reasonable affordance but the title text is misleading.
  Recommended fix: When a sub-tab is fundamentally live/snapshot rather than period-scoped, hide the period header entirely (not just disable the buttons). Or replace "May 2026" with "Live" or current timestamp. Extension of Task 135's `_periodTabs` registry: a parallel `_periodLabelTabs` registry could control whether to hide the date label too.
  Effort: Small (per-tab visibility flag for period header).
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-018 (Task 135 dim+disable fix — this finding asks for the next-step "hide entirely on live-snapshot tabs"). FE-MED-022 (Calendar PnL nested-nav).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A'.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-MED-021] [Data] MFE/MAE detail table uses 4 decimal places on ENTRY column regardless of price scale
  Description: ENTRY column on MFE/MAE detail table shows uniform 4-decimal precision across all symbols regardless of price magnitude:
    - `0.0749` (AIAUSDT) — appropriate for sub-cent assets.
    - `4.1005` (LABUSDT) — appropriate for small caps.
    - `676.7700` (BNBUSDT) — excessive (4 decimals on a $676 asset implies precision to ~$0.0001).
    - `2,340.3100` (ETHUSDT) — same issue.
    - `80,816.3000` (BTCUSDT) — same; 4 decimals on $80k is visually noisy and not market-meaningful.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session A' screenshot ss_72621xzr6, ENTRY column rows AIAUSDT through BTCUSDT.
  Impact: Visually noisy on high-priced symbols. A trader scanning the column for outliers has to mentally trim trailing zeros.
  Recommended fix: Adaptive precision — for prices ≥ $1000, show 2 decimals; for $1–$1000, 4 decimals; for < $1, 4–6 decimals based on minimum tick. Or use exchange spec tick size. Same logic should apply across Calculator, History, etc. for consistency.
  Effort: Small — single formatting helper used across columns.
  Cross-reference: FE-LOW-022 (Calculator SIZE precision differs across two display sites — same family); FE-LOW-024 (5-decimal % precision on garbage values). All three point to a centralized `format_price(symbol, value)` / `format_contracts(symbol, value)` helper as the leverage fix.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A'.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-MED-022] [Architecture] Calendar PnL adds yet another period-navigation surface — `< Prev` / `Next >` alongside the page-level period selector
  Description: Calendar PnL has both:
    - **Page-level period header**: `< May 2026 >` with Month / Week / Quarter / Year / 30D / 90D / All buttons (dim+disabled on Calendar per Task 135's registry — Calendar handler doesn't consume `period`).
    - **In-card month navigation**: separate `< Prev` / `Next >` buttons on either side of "May 2026" within the calendar card.
  Two independent month-navigation surfaces on the same view. The page-level header being dim+disabled (Task 135) partially defuses the "competing controls" framing, but the visual redundancy of "May 2026" appearing twice (once disabled at page-top, once active in-card) remains.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session A' screenshot ss_9104q6by6 — page header at top; calendar card below with its own Prev/Next.
  Impact: Visual redundancy on a single view. Compounds with FE-MED-020 (live-snapshot tabs showing "May 2026" too) — the page-level period header keeps surfacing even when it shouldn't.
  Recommended fix: Same family as FE-MED-020: hide the page-level period header entirely on sub-tabs where it's a dead control. Calendar PnL's in-card Prev/Next is the active control; the dimmed page-level header just adds chrome.
  Effort: Small (same fix as FE-MED-020; both close together).
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-007 / FE-MED-018 (parent period-selector duplication pattern; partially resolved by Task 135 + Task 136). FE-MED-020 (Funding/Beta live-snapshot variant).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A'.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN. Note: page-level period selector IS already dim+disabled on Calendar per Task 135 — this finding tracks the next-step "remove visual chrome entirely" work.

### [FE-MED-023] [UX] Regime "Config" tab is read-only — no edit/save UI despite the label
  Description: The Regime Config sub-tab shows current classifier thresholds (VIX Panic 30, HY Spread Panic 5, etc.) and decision tree rules (color-coded). Values are displayed but appear non-editable — no input fields, no Save button. Only interactive control is "Reclassify All Dates". Tab is named "Config" but offers no configuration capability.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session B screenshot ss_5596zhmvo — values displayed as static numbers; no input affordances.
  Impact: User clicks "Config" expecting to change settings, finds read-only reference page. Misleading naming-vs-behavior gap (same family as audit-01's Configuration → Accounts originally; resolved differently there).
  Recommended fix: Two options:
    - **Make editable**: add input fields for each threshold + single Save button. Probably requires backend support (a writable config endpoint).
    - **Rename + clarify**: keep read-only, rename to "Classifier Rules" or "Reference", add a note explaining where values come from and how to edit (probably file-based: `config.py REGIME_THRESHOLDS`).
  Effort: Small (rename) to medium (full edit UI + backend).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session B.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-MED-024] [UX] Regime Config decision tree references "full mode" without cross-reference
  Description: The Trending rule on Regime Config reads `VIX < 20 AND RVol < 1.2 (+ OI rising, funding > 0 in full mode)`. The parenthetical `in full mode` refers to a mode selected on Backfill tab (MODE dropdown: "Macro Only" / "Full"). Config tab doesn't link to or explain "full mode".
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session B screenshot ss_5596zhmvo shows Trending rule with unexplained "full mode" reference. Backfill tab (ss_2630gbf8r) is where MODE is set.
  Impact: Minor cognitive load. User reading Config rules in isolation has no way to discover "full mode" is something they can toggle.
  Recommended fix: Inline tooltip/hover on "full mode" explaining what it means + where to set it; or link the words to the Backfill tab.
  Effort: Trivial.
  Cross-reference: Family of "unexplained abbreviations / mode names" — FE-LOW-015 (country codes), FE-LOW-016 (P/E/A), FE-LOW-017 (FH source tag), FE-LOW-021 (MAX VIX, REGIME MULTIPLIER), Session A' FE-LOW-011 (Calendar T suffix), FE-LOW-012 (MFE/MAE summary). Pattern observation in cross-cutting notes below.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session B.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-MED-025] [Architecture] Backtest Run uses Date Range; Backtest Fetch Data uses Days Back
  Description: Both tabs ask "how far back to look" but use different paradigms:
    - **Run** tab: DATE RANGE picker `2026-02-18 → 2026-05-19` with quick chips `1Y / 6M / 3M / 1M`.
    - **Fetch Data** tab: numeric field DAYS BACK `730`.
  Same conceptual question, two different UX surfaces on adjacent tabs.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session B screenshots — Run ss_1436dw00t; Fetch Data ss_7480q8xus.
  Impact: User context-switches between tabs. Fetch Data requires mental math (how many days is "3M" → 90). Run requires picking explicit dates.
  Recommended fix: Pick one. Date range is more flexible and human-readable; Days Back is faster for "last N days". Reasonable compromise: both tabs offer Date Range with same quick chips, AND a small "as days back" tooltip. Or standardize on Date Range.
  Effort: Small.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session B.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN. (Note: NOT a backtest-scope-blocked finding — this is paradigm consistency, independent of the engine-as-real-backtester vs engine-as-storage operator decision.)

### [FE-MED-026] [UX] Backfill page mixes DD/MM/YYYY and YYYY-MM-DD date formats
  Description: On Regime Backfill, FROM/TO date inputs display dates as `01/01/2020` and `19/05/2026` (DD/MM/YYYY, European). Signal coverage table on right displays dates as `2015-01-02`, `2026-05-18` (YYYY-MM-DD, ISO 8601). Same date semantics, two different formats, same page.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session B screenshot ss_2630gbf8r — left form shows `01/01/2020`; right table SOURCE column shows `2015-01-02`, `2026-05-18`, etc.
  Impact: User has to mentally reconcile two formats. Confusing for ambiguous dates (`05/06/2026` = May 6 or June 5?). YYYY-MM-DD is unambiguous and already used elsewhere in the engine.
  Recommended fix: Standardize on YYYY-MM-DD throughout. Form inputs can still accept either format on input (browser native) but display in ISO consistent with rest of engine.
  Effort: Small.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session B.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-MED-027] [UX] Backtest Run vs Fetch Data — paradigm-level UX question (backtest scope)
  Description: Source Session B FE-MED-025: relationship between Run (explicit date range + quick chips for "1Y/6M/3M/1M") and Fetch Data (numeric DAYS BACK field). The deeper architectural question is whether the engine is positioned as "a real backtester" (where Run is the primary surface and Fetch Data is plumbing) or as "storage + walkforward cadence" (where Fetch Data is a setup step and Run is one of several views over loaded data).
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session B screenshots ss_1436dw00t (Run), ss_7480q8xus (Fetch Data).
  Impact: Until the architectural framing is resolved, UX consistency work on these surfaces could go in the wrong direction.
  Recommended fix: **Operator decision required.** Until then, no UX-level work on Run/Fetch Data tabs (other than the date-format fix from FE-MED-026 which is paradigm-independent).
  Effort: TBD (depends on operator decision).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session B.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN, BLOCKED-ON-OPERATOR-DECISION (backtest scope). When the decision lands: either moves to OPEN-fixable (concrete UX direction) or to VERIFIED-FALSE/SCOPE-DROPPED (depending on which architectural path is chosen).

### [FE-MED-028] [Architecture] Backtest Models tab duplicates Run tab's strategy fields (backtest scope)
  Description: Backtest Models tab has fields for EMA FAST, EMA SLOW, ATR SL MULT, ATR TP MULT, MIN ATR-C, DIRECTION, RISK/TRADE, MAX POSITIONS — exact same parameter set Run also exposes. Models adds MAX EXPOSURE, MAX VIX, REGIME MULTIPLIER that Run doesn't have. Run has a LOAD MODEL dropdown (currently "— Custom —") that presumably loads saved Models. The relationship between Models (save preset) and Run (Load Model dropdown + override) isn't documented.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session B screenshots — Run ss_1436dw00t; Models ss_2157669rq.
  Impact: User has to learn the model. Is Run a "free run" that doesn't save? Does saving a Model and then editing in Run create a new model? Are Run-only fields a subset of Models that always override?
  Recommended fix: Two options pending operator decision:
    - (1) Collapse the duplication — make Run tab's form ONLY a "Load Model + Run" flow with non-editable fields once a model is loaded; if user wants to tweak, they save a new model first.
    - (2) Keep duplication but add clear visual indicator on Run saying "These values match Model X" / "Custom (not saved)". Expose all 11 model fields on Run.
  Effort: Medium (architectural cleanup).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session B.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN, BLOCKED-ON-OPERATOR-DECISION (backtest scope). Direction depends on FE-MED-027's resolution.

### [FE-MED-029] [UX/Data] First Calculate POST after page load returns 422
  Description: Click sequence to reproduce:
    1. Navigate to `/calculator` (form has persisted state from prior session — TICKER=BNBUSDT, TP=680, SL=620 in this case).
    2. Click Calculate.
    3. **Result: HTTP 422.** Red toast top-right reads `Request failed: 422 /calculator/calculate`. Right-side panel shows "Couldn't load this section." with a Retry button (Task 134 EmptyState error-tone working correctly).
    4. Click Calculate again (without changing anything).
    5. **Result: HTTP 200.** Calc submits successfully, populated result renders.
  Reproduced twice during Session C: once on BTCUSDT (first submission), once on BNBUSDT (after page refresh).
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session C screenshots ss_0731m1m72 (first 422 with red toast), ss_6311dbjzx (second 422 reproduction), ss_7078h1tr4 (subsequent retry succeeded).
  Impact: User has to click Calculate twice on every fresh page load. Unintuitive. The toast text also surfaces raw HTTP details — see FE-MED-031.
  Recommended fix: Two parts:
    - **Backend**: figure out why the first POST validates incorrectly. Possible culprits: form serialization timing (the live entry price hasn't settled before the form submits), missing `hx-include` for some field, or a validation that depends on a price refresh that races with the submit.
    - **Frontend (toast)**: covered by FE-MED-031 cross-cutting finding.
  Effort: Small (toast wording, via FE-MED-031) to medium (backend race investigation).
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-031 (error toast wording). FE-HIGH-009 (the EXPIRED-on-fresh-calc bug — possibly related; both touch the Calculator submission flow).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session C.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-MED-030] [UX] Calculator countdown widget tracks "last submitted calc on this page" — not "currently-displayed form values"
  Description: When the user types a new ticker into the Calculator form (without clicking Calculate), the right column updates via `/calculator/refresh/{TICKER}` to show live BNB/BTC/etc. preview values. But the countdown widget at the top of the right column still tracks the LAST SUBMITTED calc — not the new ticker the user is now editing.
  Reproduction:
    1. Submit a BTCUSDT calc. Countdown shows whatever state the BTC calc is in.
    2. Type "BNBUSDT" into TICKER field, Tab out (triggers refresh).
    3. Right-column preview values update to BNB. But countdown banner at top is still the BTCUSDT calc's countdown.
  Specifically observed: After changing ticker from BTCUSDT to BNBUSDT, countdown banner read `✗ PLAN EXPIRED` even though only ~30 seconds had elapsed since the BTC calc was submitted. The "expiration" was probably triggered by the ticker change invalidating the BTC plan (because the form context changed), not by actual time elapse.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session C screenshot ss_4782sznes (ticker changed to BNB, BNB preview values populated, but countdown still says PLAN EXPIRED — and the BTC calc was only ~30s old at that point).
  Impact: Widget gives misleading time-pressure feedback. A user editing the form to refine an idea would see their pre-edit plan as expired even if it's seconds old. Decoupled from actual mechanics.
  Recommended fix: Decide on widget semantic — is it tracking (a) "the calc you just submitted" or (b) "the calc represented by the current form values"? Option (a) is simpler but should hide when form has drifted from the submitted calc. Option (b) requires re-resolving the widget after every refresh. Probably (a) with a "form drifted" hint when the user has typed something the calc doesn't reflect anymore.
  Effort: Medium (semantic decision + implementation).
  Cross-reference: FE-HIGH-009 (likely shared root cause — countdown widget calc_id resolution). Investigate together.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session C.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: RESOLVED in Task 139 — shared root cause with FE-HIGH-009 confirmed (event-bus-publish-vs-htmx-load race). Auditor's hypothesis ("ticker change invalidates the BTC plan") was off-mechanism. Actual mechanism: the FIRST poll fired during the race window, got EXPIRED-from-missing-row, rendered the TERMINAL EXPIRED branch (no `hx-trigger`), polling stopped. The "30s old calc shows EXPIRED" observation in Session C is the screenshot timing — by 30s the widget was already stuck on EXPIRED from a poll that ran microseconds after the original submit. Same PENDING fix (see FE-HIGH-009) closes this — no separate work. Adding to audit-impact-imprecision: 7th example (auditor's hypothesised mechanism — semantic widget-tracking — differed from actual mechanism — race-window terminal-state stuck). **Split decision applied in Task 141**: the form-drift semantic question ("widget should clear when form drifts from submitted calc") was deferred at Task 140 as a dual-option report. Operator chose Option B (split) — FE-MED-030 stays RESOLVED here; the form-drift question is now tracked as FE-LOW-026 (filed Task 141).

### [FE-MED-031] [UX] Error toasts surface raw exception strings / HTTP details to end users
  Description: Task 108 / Task 134's htmx error handlers successfully surface failures (visibility solved — FE-MED-015 + FE-MED-019 closed) but the toast text shows raw developer-format strings. Examples observed during audit-02:
    - Session B: `'RegimeFetcher' object has no attribute 'close'` (instance: FE-HIGH-008).
    - Session C: `Request failed: 422 /calculator/calculate` (instance: FE-MED-029).
  Pattern: visibility solved, wording not solved. Cross-cutting across multiple sessions and surfaces; filing as one finding rather than per-instance.
  Recommended fix: Wrap error messages with friendly prose ("Couldn't complete this action") + optional "Show details" expand with the raw exception string. Centralized helper applied to all htmx error paths (extends Task 134's existing `showError()` / `swapTargetWithError()` infrastructure with a message-friendly-fy step). Reuse the EmptyState `tone="error"` body for the in-place fragment swap; the friendly-vs-raw text decision applies to BOTH the toast and the target-swap fragment.
  Effort: Small. The infrastructure is already in place (Task 108 + 134); this is a message-string transformation step.
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-015 (toast visibility — closed Task 108); FE-MED-019 (target-element swap — closed Task 134); FE-HIGH-008 + FE-MED-029 (concrete instances).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Sessions B + C (cross-cutting pattern).
  Filed-during: Task 138 (filed during reconciliation, not during a session).
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-MED-032] [Data/Resilience] Calculator PENDING state has no upper bound
  Description: Task 139's PENDING short-circuit in `api/routes_calculator.py:149-181` returns `status="PENDING"` when `pretrade is None` in `pre_trade_log`. No timeout exists. If `handle_risk_calculated` fails to run (event-bus consumer crash, DB INSERT constraint violation, disk error, app_state.active_account_id mismatch), the route returns PENDING indefinitely. Frontend polls at 1Hz forever; widget never surfaces the failure — silent-broken instead of visibly-broken. Same shape concern as the original FE-HIGH-009 (Task 139 fixed the polling-stops-forever variant; this is the polling-forever variant).
  Evidence: Latent failure mode introduced by Task 139's fix. Not observed in an audit session — surfaced during fix design review.
  Impact: A failed calc INSERT produces a "Confirming calc..." widget that never resolves. Operator has no signal that the calc didn't persist. Smaller blast radius than FE-HIGH-009 (the bug only triggers when the INSERT itself fails, which is rare absent disk/constraint issues), but the failure is silent — exactly the class of thing Task 134's error-handling work was meant to surface.
  Recommended fix: Cap PENDING at N seconds (5s reasonable — race window is sub-second; anything past 5s is genuinely a failure). On timeout, transition to an error state ("Submission failed to record — please retry") using EmptyState `tone="error"` (Task 134 infrastructure). Implementation options:
    - Server-side: track first-poll timestamp per calc_id; on subsequent poll, if elapsed > N seconds AND pretrade still missing, return error-tone fragment instead of PENDING.
    - Client-side: countdown widget tracks its own elapsed time in PENDING state; switches to error state via JS after timeout.
  Effort: Small. Reuses Task 134's EmptyState error-tone + retry handler.
  Cross-reference: FE-HIGH-009 (Task 139 PENDING introduction); FE-MED-031 (error toast wording — friendly-prose fix should apply here too).
  Discovered-during: Task 140 review (post-fix latent failure mode).
  Filed-during: Task 140.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-LOW-025] [UI] Quantower indicator: trailing "OFF +Xms" redundant after "Disconnected"
  Description: Post-Task-121 the header status indicator reads `Quantower · Disconnected · OFF +678ms`. The substantive HIGH-tier concern from FE-HIGH-001 ("engine offline" misread) is resolved — the StatusIndicator primitive surfaces "Disconnected" clearly. The remaining polish concern: the trailing `OFF +678ms` after "Disconnected" is redundant. When the indicator state is already "Disconnected", the latency value is meaningless (there's nothing to ping) and the "OFF" label duplicates the disconnected state.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session A' verification of FE-HIGH-001 (`2026-05-18-v2.4-frontend-audit-02-session-A-prime.md`).
  Impact: Cosmetic polish on the header indicator. Operator no longer misreads the indicator as engine-offline (HIGH concern closed); remaining concern is "indicator says state twice" — visual noise, not functional ambiguity.
  Recommended fix: When plugin is disconnected, suppress the trailing "OFF +Xms" entirely. Either:
    - Frontend: JS in base.html's StatusIndicator-update path conditionally hides the value-text when severity is "error" (disconnected).
    - Backend: ws_status endpoint returns empty value when disconnected so the existing rendering path naturally omits.
  Effort: Trivial. Single conditional in the JS that sets `.si-value` text.
  Cross-reference: FE-HIGH-001 (parent — substantive HIGH closed Task 121; this is the polish remainder split per Task 141 decision).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A' (Task 138 verification).
  Filed-during: Task 141 (per operator decision to split rather than reopen FE-HIGH-001 as PARTIAL).
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-LOW-026] [UX] Countdown widget continues tracking last-submitted calc when form drifts to new ticker
  Description: After a calc is submitted, the countdown widget anchors to that calc_id. If the user then types a new ticker into the Calculator form (without clicking Calculate), the right-column preview updates via `/calculator/refresh/{TICKER}` but the countdown widget at the top continues polling the previous calc_id. Visually the form is "the user is editing BNB" while the countdown is "tracking the BTC calc from 30s ago." The Task 139 race-symptom fix (PENDING state) closed the visible PLAN-EXPIRED-on-fresh-calc bug; this is the remaining semantic question — should the widget clear or re-anchor when the form drifts?
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session C source doc (`2026-05-18-v2.4-frontend-audit-02-session-C.md` original FE-MED-028, escalated to FE-MED-030 during Task 138 reconciliation, race-symptom RESOLVED Task 139).
  Impact: Minor cognitive load. The widget is technically correct (tracking what was submitted) but the disconnect between visible form state and widget state is unintuitive on re-edit flows.
  Recommended fix: Operator-UX decision pending — two reasonable shapes:
    - **(a) Hide on drift**: when the form's TICKER (or other key fields) differ from the submitted calc's, hide the countdown entirely. Reappears on next submit.
    - **(b) "Form drifted" hint**: keep the countdown visible but add a muted overlay "Form changed — submit to update".
  Both small effort. Decide based on which fits the operator's mental model better — (a) is simpler, (b) preserves the running countdown for the user who hasn't decided yet whether to re-submit.
  Effort: Small.
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-030 (parent — race-symptom resolved Task 139; this captures the deferred semantic question per Task 141 split decision). FE-HIGH-009 (cluster sibling — race resolution).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session C (Task 138 reconciliation).
  Filed-during: Task 141 (per operator decision to split rather than reopen FE-MED-030 as PARTIAL).
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-LOW-010] [UI] Calendar PnL: no-data days and future days share the same visual treatment
  Description: On Calendar PnL for May 2026, three categories of empty cells exist but look essentially identical (dark background, no content):
    - **Layout-empty** (May 1–3 fall under Mon/Tue/Wed slots — prior-month area).
    - **No-trade days within the period** (dark cell with `$0.00`).
    - **Future days** (May 20–31 — dark cell, no data).
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session A' screenshot ss_9104q6by6 — May 1–3 area, May 18–19, May 20–31.
  Impact: Cosmetic. Breaks cleanness of calendar at a glance.
  Recommended fix: Subtle visual distinction. Future days: keep dark, slightly lighter; non-clickable. No-trade days within the period: faint border or "no trades" subtitle. Out-of-month slots: unrendered or lower opacity.
  Effort: Small.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A'.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-LOW-011] [UI] Calendar PnL: cell layout uses unlabeled values in bottom corners
  Description: Each populated day cell shows top-left day number, top-right percentage (presumably win rate), center-left PnL with sign, bottom-left trade-count-like value (`1T`, `2T`), bottom-right dollar value (presumably volume). No legend or hover-tooltip explains bottom-left/bottom-right. `T` suffix not documented.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session A' screenshot ss_9104q6by6.
  Impact: Same family as audit-01 FE-MED-011/012 — identifier-style/abbreviated labels with no explanation.
  Recommended fix: Hover-tooltip on cell explaining the four corners, or small legend below: `T = trades · $XXX = volume`.
  Effort: Trivial.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A'.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-LOW-012] [UX] MFE/MAE summary labels lack methodology hints
  Description: MFE/MAE header summary row shows `AVG MFE $0.71`, `AVG |MAE| $1.56`, `AVG ME-RATIO 1.30`, `MFE >= 2× MAE 19.7%`. None have a tooltip/help icon. Quant-literate trader will infer; newer user won't.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session A' screenshot ss_72621xzr6.
  Impact: Cosmetic / minor. Same family as audit-01 MED-011/012.
  Recommended fix: Small `?` hover-tooltip beside each label explaining the calculation. `ME-RATIO`: "Average MFE divided by average |MAE|". `MFE >= 2× MAE`: "Percentage of trades where max favorable excursion was at least 2× max adverse excursion".
  Effort: Trivial.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A'.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-LOW-013] [Data] Calendar PnL day cell shows "$0.00" with "33%" win rate
  Description: May 17 (Sun) cell shows `$0.00` PnL with `33%` win rate, `3T` trades, `$81` volume. 33% win rate implies 1 winning trade of 3, but PnL nets to exactly `$0.00`. Mathematically possible (offsetting to 2-decimal precision after rounding) but suspiciously round. More likely: small float values rounded to 0.00 for display while the underlying total is something like $+0.003 or $-0.004.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session A' screenshot ss_9104q6by6 — May 17 cell.
  Impact: Edge-case display precision. Misleading only if user reads "$0.00 with 33% win rate" as a paradox.
  Recommended fix: When PnL is within ±$0.005 of zero, display as `≈$0.00` or `<$0.01`. Or accept as expected behavior given small-account scale.
  Effort: Trivial.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session A'.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-LOW-014] [Data] Backfill SOURCE column casing inconsistent
  Description: On Backfill's signal coverage table, SOURCE column shows providers with mixed casing — `yfinance`, `fred`, `derived`, `binance` (lowercase) vs `CoinGecko`, `Binance` (TitleCase). Same provider — Binance — appears in two casings on the same table.

### [FE-LOW-021] [UX] Models tab REGIME section: MAX VIX and REGIME MULTIPLIER unlabeled (backtest scope)
  Description: Models tab's REGIME section has `MAX VIX: 30` and `REGIME MULTIPLIER: 1.0` inputs. No tooltips. Probably: "Don't trade if current VIX > MAX VIX" and "Multiply position size by REGIME MULTIPLIER based on current regime". Neither stated. Same MAX VIX value (30) appears in /regime as the Panic threshold — relationship between the two isn't explained.

### [FE-LOW-022] [UI] Calculator SETUP SUMMARY size precision differs from CALCULATED POSITION
  Description: For the same eligible BNBUSDT calc, two parts of the result panel show the same size with different precision:
    - **CALCULATED POSITION → SIZE (CONTRACTS)**: `0.0314` (4 decimals).
    - **SETUP SUMMARY → SIZE (CONTRACTS)**: `0.031400` (6 decimals).
  Same underlying value, two formatters.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session C screenshot ss_2612yxvc3.
  Impact: Cosmetic. Same family as FE-MED-021 (uniform 4-decimal precision on MFE/MAE) — number formatting needs centralization.
  Recommended fix: Single `format_contracts(symbol, value)` helper used in both places. Pick one precision — 4 decimals matches most other engine displays; 6 decimals matches Binance contract sizing for some pairs. Decide based on minimum tick size for the symbol.
  Effort: Trivial.
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-021 (adaptive precision pattern); FE-LOW-024 (5-decimal % precision).
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session C.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-LOW-023] [UX] Calculator result panel has yet another empty-state pattern
  Description: On a fresh page load with no submitted calc, the right-side panel shows `No entry price — enter a ticker and wait for live price, or type a price manually.` This is a useful informational empty state — it's actionable. But it's another distinct empty-state pattern not yet aligned with the engine's EmptyState primitive (Task 124).
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session C screenshot ss_50739w3m9 (fresh /calculator nav after refresh).
  Impact: Continued evidence for the EmptyState migration opportunity flagged in audit-01 FE-MED-006.
  Recommended fix: Migrate to the EmptyState primitive (Task 124's `empty_state(message, tone="info", ...)` macro). This instance is well-designed — could serve as one of the templates.
  Effort: Trivial (primitive call replaces inline markup).
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-006 (parent — EmptyState primitive migration). Bundle A migration is ongoing.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session C.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

### [FE-LOW-024] [Data] Calculator percentage values display 5-decimal precision for invalid TP/SL
  Description: When TP/SL are off-scale for the current symbol (e.g., TP=80000 when current BNB price is 644), the percent display gets absurd: `TP: 12314.75%` and `SL: 11694.81%`. Mathematically correct but uselessly imprecise — 5 significant digits when the data is clearly garbage. A clamp or sanity check at the display layer would avoid the visual noise.
  Evidence: Audit-02 Session C screenshot ss_4782sznes (ticker changed to BNB, TP=80000 still in form, %s blow up).
  Impact: Cosmetic; happens only during ticker-change transitions before user updates TP/SL.
  Recommended fix: When abs(percent) > 100%, display as `>100%` or trigger an inline warning ("TP/SL out of range for current price — update before calculating"). Or auto-clear TP/SL when ticker changes.
  Effort: Trivial.
  Cross-reference: FE-MED-021 + FE-LOW-022 — centralized number-formatting helper family.
  Discovered-during: audit-02 Session C.
  Filed-during: Task 138.
  Status: OPEN.

Inline confirmations of prior-resolved findings observed during the three sessions. These do NOT change Stat Summary counts (findings were already in their RESOLVED / PARTIAL states) — they're audit trail entries documenting that the resolutions hold up in operator-driven browser walkthroughs.

### FE-HIGH-005 — RESOLVED (confirmed Session B)
Regime Overview's CURRENT REGIME card shows `As of 2026-05-19 06:22 UTC / full mode` instead of `As of undefined / full mode`. Real timestamp displayed. The v2.4.1.1 hotfix field-name fix verified.

### FE-LOW-001 — RESOLVED (confirmed Session A')
Dashboard header, page titles ("Analytics — QUANTAMENTAL ENGINE v2.4.1.1"), and footer all consistently read `v2.4.1.1`. `PROJECT_VERSION_` constant bump verified.

### FE-MED-011 + FE-MED-012 — RESOLVED (confirmed Session C)
Calculator preview labels confirmed:

### FE-MED-008 — PARTIALLY MITIGATED (confirmed Session B, status unchanged from prior)
Both "not backfilled" cards (BTC Market Cap, Aggregate OI Change) now show "Use Backfill tab" CTA button — consistent action across both. Text layout still differs slightly (BTC Market Cap stacks the text on a separate line; Aggregate OI Change wraps it inline) but actionability is uniform. Substantially addressed; not perfectly resolved. Status remains PARTIAL — original entry already reflects this. Backfill pre-select feature work blocked downstream by FE-HIGH-008.

### FE-MED-006 — CONTINUED EXPANSION (Sessions A' + B + C all added datapoints)
Designed empty states accumulating across surfaces (Analytics R-Multiples / Risk Metrics, Backtest Models / Fetch Data "No jobs yet", Calculator's "No entry price..."). The EmptyState primitive (Task 124) has consumers to migrate; FE-LOW-023 surfaces one specific instance (Calculator). Pattern stays open — primitive exists, migration is per-consumer.

### FE-HIGH-001 — PARTIALLY ADDRESSED (Session A' verification; decision pending)
Session A' confirmed header now reads `Quantower · Disconnected · OFF +678ms`. Substantive concern (misread "engine offline") resolved by Task 121's StatusIndicator migration. **Remaining concern**: trailing `OFF +Xms` after "Disconnected" implies a separate status indicator — redundant when state is already "Disconnected" and the latency value is meaningless for a disconnected resource. Reported to operator as pending decision (see Task 138 commit message + report — two options below; operator decides post-report, neither auto-committed).

  **Option A — Reopen FE-HIGH-001 as PARTIAL:**
  Pros: keeps audit lineage intact; one finding number tracks the whole header-readability arc; reflects that the original concern is substantively (but not completely) resolved. The PARTIAL state is precedented in the ledger (FE-MED-008 is PARTIAL).
  Cons: the original ambiguity (HIGH-tier "engine offline misread") is fully resolved. Leaving the entry HIGH when the remaining polish is LOW-tier inflates the HIGH count for what's now a polish concern. Status field would need to read "PARTIALLY RESOLVED — only LOW-tier polish remains" which is a slight semantic mismatch with the HIGH severity.

  **Option B — File new FE-LOW-025 follow-up:**
  Pros: severity counts stay accurate (HIGH-001 fully closed; new LOW-25 reflects the actual remaining concern). Easier to scope a follow-up task — "fix trailing redundancy on Quantower indicator" is a self-contained LOW. Future Top-N reshuffles don't have to consider HIGH-001 as a slot candidate when its substantive concern is closed.
  Cons: lineage breaks across two ticket numbers. Reader has to follow cross-refs from FE-HIGH-001 → FE-LOW-025 to understand the full history.

  **Effort to apply either decision**: a single audit-doc edit (rebumping FE-HIGH-001 to PARTIAL, OR appending FE-LOW-025 with cross-references). No code changes either way.

### Audit-01 LOW-005 — CONFIRMED PATTERN (Session B added new instance)
Provider-name casing inconsistency (originally observed in Connections panel) now seen on Backfill SOURCE column — filed as FE-LOW-014. Same root cause, same recommended fix. Pattern observation stays as a discipline-direction note rather than a discipline-level finding.

## 33.C Cross-cutting observations from audit-02 (Task 138)

Pattern-level observations across all 3 sessions, filed for the calibration record rather than as individual findings.

**1. Error toast wording lag.** FE-MED-031 captures this. Task 108 + Task 134 solved error VISIBILITY (toast + target swap), but the message-string transformation step was never added. Two concrete instances across Sessions B + C; consistent enough to file as a cross-cutting MED rather than per-instance.

**2. Number-formatting centralization needed.** FE-MED-021 (MFE/MAE 4-decimal regardless of price), FE-LOW-022 (SIZE precision differs between two Calculator sites), FE-LOW-024 (5-decimal % on out-of-range values). Audit-01 also noted Trade History decimals + Calculator preview precision. A single `format_price(symbol, value)` / `format_contracts(symbol, value)` helper consulting exchange spec tick size would close all in one move. Not filed as a single architectural finding — each instance is independently filable, and the leverage emerges from accumulated count.

**3. Abbreviation-without-tooltip pattern is now the most-repeated finding shape.** Counted across audit-01 + audit-02:
  - FE-LOW-009 (CF, Adj Chg — resolved Task 131 by expansion).
  - FE-LOW-011 (Calendar T suffix, $XXX corner values).
  - FE-LOW-012 (MFE/MAE summary labels).
  - FE-LOW-015 (country codes).
  - FE-LOW-016 (P/E/A).
  - FE-LOW-017 (FH source tag).
  - FE-LOW-021 (MAX VIX, REGIME MULTIPLIER).
  - FE-MED-024 ("full mode" cross-reference).
  - Plus audit-01 FE-MED-011 / FE-MED-012 (resolved Task 112 by expansion).
That's 10+ instances. A TooltipDecorator primitive (or hover-tooltip pattern) would consolidate. **Filed as a discipline-direction note here; not a separate finding** — the per-instance LOWs already capture the surface count, and adding a meta-finding inflates without adding leverage.

**4. Read-only-tab-labeled-Config anti-pattern.** Two instances: audit-01's Configuration → Accounts (different shape — fixed by FE-CRIT-001 resolution), audit-02's Regime Config (FE-MED-023). Pattern observation: "Config" tabs should either offer editing or be renamed to "Reference" / "Rules". Single instance survives in the ledger (FE-MED-023); discipline-direction note here for future audit calibration.

**5. The countdown-widget cluster.** FE-HIGH-009 + FE-MED-030 strongly suspected to share root cause (calc_id resolution / swap-target update). Recommend investigating together in the FE-HIGH-009 fix task. Single-root-cause fix may auto-resolve FE-MED-030; if not, FE-MED-030 stays separate.

**6. Designed-empty-state accumulation continues** (FE-MED-006 trend). Each session added datapoints. EmptyState primitive (Task 124) is the leverage point; remaining work is per-consumer migration. Not refiling.

**7. Period-selector duplication pattern is now well-mapped.** FE-MED-007 (Regime — resolved T136), FE-MED-018 (Analytics Equity Curve — resolved T135), FE-MED-020 (Funding/Beta live-snapshot variant), FE-MED-022 (Calendar PnL in-card nav). The visual-normalization piece is closed; the remaining work is per-surface "hide entirely on dead-control sub-tabs" polish. Same family.

**8. Audit-02 calibration outcome.** Sessions A' + B + C ran with engine confirmed-up (CLAUDE.md audit-time-artifact discipline working). Zero 503s observed across all three sessions. Operator pre-flight check before each session prevented recurrence of the Task 127→128 false-positive cluster.

---

These are leverage observations, not individual findings. They are the structural roots most of the MED findings trace back to. Quoted verbatim from audit-01:

**Missing component primitives** is the single largest leverage point. The 5 self-found calibration findings + 9 of the 14 medium findings all point to the same root cause: there is no shared Card / Row / EmptyState / StatusIndicator / PeriodSelector. Each page rolls its own. Build the primitives, migrate page-by-page, get 14+ findings resolved for the cost of 5 component implementations.

**Empty states are not designed, they're emergent.** Seven different empty-state treatments. Some are centered text, some are inline text, some are placeholder labels in dropdowns. An EmptyState primitive with a tone (informational / actionable / error) and a slot for an action button would standardize this.

**Period selectors are repeated and not factored.** Regime page alone has at least three different period-selector implementations: page-level (Month/Week/Quarter/Year/30D/90D/All), Regime Timeline (Swim/Bars/Blocks/Heat/Stack + 30d/90d/1y/All), and per-Macro-Signal chart (30d/90d/1y/5y/All). Analytics has its own. History has yet another (Last 90 days / Last 30 days / Last 15 days / Last 7 days / Yesterday / Today). These don't all need the same choices, but they should share a PeriodSelector primitive with configurable options and a documented precedence rule for nested selectors.

**Status indicators are visually overloaded with no clear vocabulary.** "Plugin · OFF" with a red dot, "LIVE" tag, "Standalone" mode, green dot before Plugin, "Connected" / "Not Set" tags on Connections, the orange banner across every page. Each is correctly trying to communicate state, but together they form a soup. A StatusIndicator primitive with documented severity levels (success / warning / error / info / neutral) and consistent placement would clarify.

## 35. Frontend Coverage Gaps (carried from audit-01)

The audit prompt called for honest coverage gaps rather than claimed completeness. Auditor explicitly DID NOT cover:

- **Analytics sub-tabs**: only `Overview`. The other 8 sub-tabs (Equity Curve, Calendar PnL, Traded Pairs, MFE/MAE, R-Multiples, Risk Metrics, Funding, Beta Exposure) were not opened.
- **Regime sub-tabs**: only `Overview`. `Backfill`, `News`, `Config` not opened.
- **Backtest sub-tabs**: only `Run`. `Fetch Data`, `QT Import`, `Models` not opened.
- **Calculator populated state**: did NOT submit a calc to populate the right-side preview (avoided creating unwanted DB rows). The countdown widget across its 4 states (LINKABLE / EXPIRING_SOON / EXPIRED / LINKED_CONFIRMED) was NOT visually verified — unit tests pass; Aryo can verify visual states himself when next using the calc.
- **Live trade phase**: skipped per Aryo's choice. No real trade placement observed.
- **Mobile / narrow viewport**: not tested. Audit prompt said to resize to ~768 px and observe; auditor did not.
- **Multi-account behavior**: only one account exists; account-switching couldn't be tested.
- **Hover and focus states**: observed where they naturally surfaced; not systematically keyboard-navigated.
- **Number formatting comprehensive scan**: did not do a systematic same-value-in-different-places check (e.g., is "0.075822" the same number of decimals everywhere?). Some inconsistencies suspected; not exhaustively verified.
- **SSE flicker**: did not verify whether the dashboard rerenders cleanly on SSE-driven updates. Did not artificially disconnect WS to see the stale-data behaviour.
- **HTMX swap morphing**: did not check whether individual fragment swaps cause visible flicker.

A subsequent frontend audit pass (`audit-02`) could cover these in ~30 minutes per page.
